## I Dark Plottings at Blandings Castle / 布兰丁斯城堡密谋

### I / I

===Original===
At the open window of the great library of Blandings Castle, drooping like a wet sock, as was his habit when he had nothing to prop his spine against, the Earl of Emsworth, that amiable and boneheaded peer, stood gazing out over his domain.

===Chinese===
在布兰丁斯城堡（Blandings Castle）大书房敞开的窗前，埃姆斯沃思伯爵（the Earl of Emsworth）——这位和蔼可亲、脑筋不灵的贵族——正凭窗眺望着自己的领地。他整个人蔫蔫地垂着，活像一只湿袜子；每逢脊背后头没有东西可支，他向来就是这副仪态。

===Original===
It was a lovely morning and the air was fragrant with gentle summer scents. Yet in his lordship’s pale blue eyes there was a look of melancholy. His brow was furrowed, his mouth peevish. And this was all the more strange in that he was normally as happy as only a fluffy-minded man with excellent health and a large income can be. A writer, describing Blandings Castle in a magazine article, had once said: “Tiny mosses have grown in the cavities of the stones, until, viewed near at hand, the place seems shaggy with vegetation.” It would not have been a bad description of the proprietor. Fifty-odd years of serene and unruffled placidity had given Lord Emsworth a curiously moss-covered look. Very few things had the power to disturb him. Even his younger son, the Hon. Freddie Threepwood, could only do it occasionally.

===Chinese===
这是个明媚的早晨，空气里弥漫着夏日温柔的芬芳。然而勋爵那双浅蓝的眼睛里，却含着一抹忧郁。他眉头紧锁，嘴角愠恼。这就愈发奇怪了，因为他素来的快活，是只有那种脑子轻飘飘、身体康健、进项丰厚的人才有的快活。曾有位作家在杂志文章里这样描写布兰丁斯城堡：「细小的苔藓在石头的孔穴里生长，近前看来，整座宅子仿佛披了一身茸茸的绿装。」拿这话形容宅子的主人，倒也不算离谱。五十来年安宁恬淡、波澜不惊的日子，给埃姆斯沃思勋爵（Lord Emsworth）平添了一层奇异的苔藓气质。极少数事物才有搅扰他的本领。就连他的幼子、顶着荣誉头衔的弗雷迪·思里普伍德（the Hon. Freddie Threepwood），也只能偶尔做到。

===Original===
Yet now he was sad. And﻿—not to make a mystery of it any longer﻿—the reason of his sorrow was the fact that he had mislaid his glasses and without them was as blind, to use his own neat simile, as a bat. He was keenly aware of the sunshine that poured down on his gardens, and was yearning to pop out and potter among the flowers he loved. But no man, pop he never so wisely, can hope to potter with any good result if the world is a mere blur.

===Chinese===
可如今他伤心了。而且——不必再对此故弄玄虚——他伤心的缘由是：他把眼镜不知搁到哪儿去了，没了眼镜，用他自己那个漂亮的比方来说，他瞎得像只蝙蝠。阳光正倾泻在他的花园上，他对此感觉敏锐，一心想溜出去，到他心爱的花丛间闲逛摆弄一番。可是，任你出溜得再怎么明智，一旦整个世界只是一团模糊，谁也别指望闲逛出什么好结果。

===Original===
The door behind him opened, and Beach the butler entered, a dignified procession of one.

===Chinese===
身后的门开了，管家比奇（Beach）走了进来——好一支庄重的一人仪仗队。

===Original===
“Who’s that?” inquired Lord Emsworth, spinning on his axis.

===Chinese===
「是谁？」埃姆斯沃思勋爵问道，原地打了个转。

===Original===
“It is I, your lordship﻿—Beach.”

===Chinese===
「是我，勋爵大人——比奇。」

===Original===
“Have you found them?”

===Chinese===
「找到了吗？」

===Original===
“Not yet, your lordship,” sighed the butler.

===Chinese===
「还没有，勋爵大人。」管家叹了口气。

===Original===
“You can’t have looked.”

===Chinese===
「你们准是没找。」

===Original===
“I have searched assiduously, your lordship, but without avail. Thomas and Charles also announce non-success. Stokes has not yet made his report.”

===Chinese===
「我已尽心竭力地搜寻过了，勋爵大人，只是徒劳无功。托马斯（Thomas）与查尔斯（Charles）亦报告未能寻获。斯托克斯（Stokes）尚未回禀。」

===Original===
“Ah!”

===Chinese===
「啊！」

===Original===
“I am re-despatching Thomas and Charles to your lordship’s bedroom,” said the Master of the Hunt. “I trust that their efforts will be rewarded.”

===Chinese===
「我正再度派遣托马斯与查尔斯前往勋爵大人的卧室。」这位狩猎总管说道。「愿他们此番努力必有所成。」

===Original===
Beach withdrew, and Lord Emsworth turned to the window again. The scene that spread itself beneath him﻿—though he was unfortunately not able to see it﻿—was a singularly beautiful one, for the castle, which is one of the oldest inhabited houses in England, stands upon a knoll of rising ground at the southern end of the celebrated Vale of Blandings in the county of Shropshire. Away in the blue distance wooded hills ran down to where the Severn gleamed like an unsheathed sword; while up from the river rolling parkland, mounting and dipping, surged in a green wave almost to the castle walls, breaking on the terraces in a many-coloured flurry of flowers as it reached the spot where the province of Angus McAllister, his lordship’s head gardener, began. The day being June the thirtieth, which is the very high-tide time of summer flowers, the immediate neighbourhood of the castle was ablaze with roses, pinks, pansies, carnations, hollyhocks, columbines, larkspurs, London pride, Canterbury bells, and a multitude of other choice blooms of which only Angus could have told you the names. A conscientious man was Angus; and in spite of being a good deal hampered by Lord Emsworth’s amateur assistance, he showed excellent results in his department. In his beds there was much at which to point with pride, little to view with concern.

===Chinese===
比奇退了出去，埃姆斯沃思勋爵重新转向窗口。展现在他脚下的景致——只可惜他无缘看见——是格外出色的一片美景：这座城堡是英格兰有人居住的最古老宅邸之一，坐落在什罗普郡（Shropshire）著名的布兰丁斯谷（the Vale of Blandings）南端一座隆起的小丘上。远处湛蓝的天际里，林木覆盖的丘陵一路逶迤而下，直抵塞文河（the Severn）闪亮如一柄出鞘长剑的地方；而从河畔起，起伏绵延的庄园草场涨起一道绿浪，层层涌动，几乎直拍到城堡墙根，在台地上迸作漫天缤纷的花雨——涌到那里，便进入了勋爵大人的首席园丁安格斯·麦卡利斯特（Angus McAllister）的辖境。这天是六月三十日，正值夏花的极盛时节，城堡四近一片绚烂：玫瑰、石竹、三色堇、康乃馨、蜀葵、耧斗菜、翠雀、伦敦骄傲、坎特伯雷钟，还有无数旁的名贵花卉，唯有安格斯才说得出它们的名目。安格斯是位恪尽职守的人；尽管时常被埃姆斯沃思勋爵的业余「协助」掣肘，他分管的业务依然成绩斐然。在他的花圃里，足堪引以为荣者甚多，堪为隐忧者无几。

===Original===
Scarcely had Beach removed himself when Lord Emsworth was called upon to turn again. The door had opened for the second time, and a young man in a beautifully-cut suit of grey flannel was standing in the doorway. He had a long and vacant face topped by shining hair brushed back and heavily brilliantined after the prevailing mode, and he was standing on one leg. For Freddie Threepwood was seldom completely at his ease in his parent’s presence.

===Chinese===
比奇前脚刚走，埃姆斯沃思勋爵便不得不再度转身。门第二次开了，一个身穿剪裁极讲究的灰法兰绒西装的年轻人立在门口。他长着一张冗长而茫然的脸，头顶的头发油光锃亮，按当下时兴的样式厚厚抹了发蜡梳向后方，而且他正单腿站着。原来弗雷迪·思里普伍德在父亲面前，向来难得彻底自在。

===Original===
“Hallo, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
「哈罗，老爸。」

===Original===
“Well, Frederick?”

===Chinese===
「嗯，弗雷德里克？」

===Original===
It would be paltering with the truth to say that Lord Emsworth’s greeting was a warm one. It lacked the note of true affection. A few weeks before he had had to pay a matter of five hundred pounds to settle certain racing debts for his offspring; and, while this had not actually dealt an irretrievable blow at his bank account, it had undeniably tended to diminish Freddie’s charm in his eyes.

===Chinese===
若说埃姆斯沃思勋爵这一声招呼够得上热络，那便是歪曲事实了。它缺少真挚慈爱的调子。几个星期以前，他刚为这位后代掏了五百英镑上下的数目，偿清了若干赛马赌债；这笔支出虽说不至于给他的银行账户造成无可挽回的打击，却无可否认地使弗雷迪在他眼里的魅力打了折扣。

===Original===
“Hear you’ve lost your glasses, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
「听说您把眼镜弄丢了，老爸。」

===Original===
“That is so.”

===Chinese===
「正是。」

===Original===
“Nuisance, what?”

===Chinese===
「真讨厌，是不是？」

===Original===
“Undeniably.”

===Chinese===
「无可否认。」

===Original===
“Ought to have a spare pair.”

===Chinese===
「本该备一副替换的。」

===Original===
“I have broken my spare pair.”

===Chinese===
「我那副替换的打破了。」

===Original===
“Tough luck! And lost the other?”

===Chinese===
「真倒霉！另一副又丢了？」

===Original===
“And, as you say, lost the other.”

===Chinese===
「而且，正如你所说，另一副丢了。」

===Original===
“Have you looked for the bally things?”

===Chinese===
「这鬼玩意儿您找过了吗？」

===Original===
“I have.”

===Chinese===
「找了。」

===Original===
“Must be somewhere, I mean.”

===Chinese===
「总该在什么地方吧，我是说。」

===Original===
“Quite possibly.”

===Chinese===
「很有可能。」

===Original===
“Where,” asked Freddie, warming to his work, “did you see them last?”

===Chinese===
「您最后一次，」弗雷迪问道，越问越来劲，「是在哪儿见到它们的？」

===Original===
“Go away!” said Lord Emsworth, on whom his child’s conversation had begun to exercise an oppressive effect.

===Chinese===
「走开！」埃姆斯沃思勋爵说，他孩子的谈话已开始对他产生一种压抑的效果。

===Original===
“Eh?”

===Chinese===
「嗯？」

===Original===
“Go away!”

===Chinese===
「走开！」

===Original===
“Go away?”

===Chinese===
「走开？」

===Original===
“Yes, go away!”

===Chinese===
「对，走开！」

===Original===
“Right ho!”

===Chinese===
「好嘞！」

===Original===
The door closed. His lordship returned to the window once more.

===Chinese===
门关上了。勋爵大人又回到窗前。

===Original===
He had been standing there some few minutes when one of those miracles occurred which happen in libraries. Without sound or warning a section of books started to move away from the parent body and, swinging out in a solid chunk into the room, showed a glimpse of a small, study-like apartment. A young man in spectacles came noiselessly through and the books returned to their place.

===Chinese===
他在那儿站了几分钟，书房里特有的那种奇迹之一便发生了：一声不响、毫无预兆，一段书架脱离了主体，整块儿旋进屋内，露出一个书房模样的小间。一个戴眼镜的年轻人悄没声儿地走了出来，书架随即各归其位。

===Original===
The contrast between Lord Emsworth and the newcomer, as they stood there, was striking, almost dramatic. Lord Emsworth was so acutely spectacle-less; Rupert Baxter, his secretary, so pronouncedly spectacled. It was his spectacles that struck you first as you saw the man. They gleamed efficiently at you. If you had a guilty conscience, they pierced you through and through; and even if your conscience was one hundred percent pure you could not ignore them. “Here,” you said to yourself, “is an efficient young man in spectacles.”

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵与来客站在那里，两相对照，鲜明得近乎戏剧化。埃姆斯沃思勋爵是那样触目地没了眼镜；他的秘书鲁珀特·巴克斯特（Rupert Baxter），则是那样醒目地架着眼镜。你一见此人，先撞进眼帘的便是那副眼镜。它们高效地朝你熠熠生光。你若良心有愧，它们便把你刺个对穿；就算你的良心百分之百纯净，你也无法对它们视若无睹。「瞧，」你对自己说，「这是个戴眼镜的干练青年。」

===Original===
In describing Rupert Baxter as efficient, you did not overestimate him. He was essentially that. Technically but a salaried subordinate, he had become by degrees, owing to the limp amiability of his employer, the real master of the house. He was the Brains of Blandings, the man at the switch, the person in charge, and the pilot, so to speak, who weathered the storm. Lord Emsworth left everything to Baxter, only asking to be allowed to potter in peace; and Baxter, more than equal to the task, shouldered it without wincing.

===Chinese===
形容鲁珀特·巴克斯特干练，并无半分夸大。他骨子里就是干练二字。名义上不过是个领薪水的下属，但靠着雇主那软塌塌的和气，他已一步步成了这座宅子里真正的主事人。他是布兰丁斯的大脑，是扳道岔的把式，是总揽一切的头儿，也可以说，是顶着风暴掌舵的领航员。埃姆斯沃思勋爵万事都推给巴克斯特，只求获准安安静静侍弄花草；而巴克斯特对付这份差事绰绰有余，接过来眉头都不皱一下。

===Original===
Having got within range, Baxter coughed; and Lord Emsworth, recognising the sound, wheeled round with a faint flicker of hope. It might be that even this apparently insoluble problem of the missing pince-nez would yield before the other’s efficiency.

===Chinese===
进入射程之后，巴克斯特咳了一声；埃姆斯沃思勋爵听出这声响，带着一线微弱的希望转过身来。说不定，连夹鼻眼镜失踪这个显然无解的难题，在那人的干练面前也会束手就擒。

===Original===
“Baxter, my dear fellow, I’ve lost my glasses. My glasses. I have mislaid them. I cannot think where they can have gone to. You haven’t seen them anywhere by any chance?”

===Chinese===
「巴克斯特，亲爱的伙计，我把眼镜丢了。我的眼镜。我不知把它们搁哪儿了。我实在想不出它们能上哪儿去。你有没有碰巧在哪儿见过？」

===Original===
“Yes, Lord Emsworth,” replied the secretary, quietly equal to the crisis. “They are hanging down your back.”

===Chinese===
「见过，埃姆斯沃思勋爵。」秘书答道，从容不迫地应付着危机。「它们正垂在您的背上。」

===Original===
“Down my back? Why, bless my soul!” His lordship tested the statement and found it﻿—like all Baxter’s statements﻿—accurate. “Why, bless my soul, so they are! Do you know, Baxter, I really believe I must be growing absentminded.” He hauled in the slack, secured the pince-nez, adjusted them beamingly. His irritability had vanished like the dew off one of his roses. “Thank you, Baxter, thank you. You are invaluable.”

===Chinese===
「垂在我的背上？哎呀，我的天哪！」勋爵大人验证了这一陈述，发现它——与巴克斯特的全部陈述一样——准确无误。「哎呀，我的天哪，还真是在背上！你知道吗，巴克斯特，我真的相信我准是越来越心不在焉了。」他收起那截松垂的镜绳，把夹鼻眼镜抓稳，喜洋洋地架上了鼻梁。他的躁怒消失了，像晨露从他哪一株玫瑰上消失一样。「谢谢你，巴克斯特，谢谢你。你真是无价之宝。」

===Original===
And with a radiant smile Lord Emsworth made buoyantly for the door, en route for God’s air and the society of McAllister. The movement drew from Baxter another cough﻿—a sharp, peremptory cough this time; and his lordship paused, reluctantly, like a dog whistled back from the chase. A cloud fell over the sunniness of his mood. Admirable as Baxter was in so many respects, he had a tendency to worry him at times; and something told Lord Emsworth that he was going to worry him now.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵脸上焕发着光彩，脚步轻快地直奔门口而去，目的地是上帝的清新空气和麦卡利斯特的社交界。这一动向引得巴克斯特又咳了一声——这回是一声急促、不容分说的咳嗽；勋爵大人不情愿地驻了足，像一条追猎正酣却被哨音唤回的狗。一片阴云罩上了他晴朗的心情。巴克斯特尽管在那么多方面可敬可佩，却时不时有个让他烦心的毛病；某种直觉告诉埃姆斯沃思勋爵：他现在就要来烦他了。

===Original===
“The car will be at the door,” said Baxter with quiet firmness, “at two sharp.”

===Chinese===
「汽车两点整，」巴克斯特以平静的坚定说道，「会候在门口。」

===Original===
“Car? What car?”

===Chinese===
「汽车？什么汽车？」

===Original===
“The car to take you to the station.”

===Chinese===
「送您去车站的汽车。」

===Original===
“Station? What station?”

===Chinese===
「车站？什么车站？」

===Original===
Rupert Baxter preserved his calm. There were times when he found his employer a little trying, but he never showed it.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特·巴克斯特保持着镇定。有时候他也觉得雇主有点难缠，但他从不流露出来。

===Original===
“You have perhaps forgotten, Lord Emsworth, that you arranged with Lady Constance to go to London this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「您也许忘了，埃姆斯沃思勋爵，您跟康斯坦丝夫人（Lady Constance）约好了今天下午去伦敦。」

===Original===
“Go to London!” gasped Lord Emsworth, appalled. “In weather like this? With a thousand things to attend to in the garden? What a perfectly preposterous notion! Why should I go to London? I hate London.”

===Chinese===
「去伦敦！」埃姆斯沃思勋爵倒抽一口冷气，惊骇不已。「这种天气？花园里还有一千件事等着料理？这主意简直荒唐透顶！我为什么要去伦敦？我讨厌伦敦。」

===Original===
“You arranged with Lady Constance that you would give Mr. McTodd lunch tomorrow at your club.”

===Chinese===
「您跟康斯坦丝夫人商量好了，明天在您的俱乐部请麦克托德先生（Mr. McTodd）吃午饭。」

===Original===
“Who the devil is Mr. McTodd?”

===Chinese===
「麦克托德先生究竟是哪路神仙？」

===Original===
“The well-known Canadian poet.”

===Chinese===
「那位著名的加拿大诗人。」

===Original===
“Never heard of him.”

===Chinese===
「从没听说过。」

===Original===
“Lady Constance has long been a great admirer of his work. She wrote inviting him, should he ever come to England, to pay a visit to Blandings. He is now in London and is to come down tomorrow for two weeks. Lady Constance’s suggestion was that, as a compliment to Mr. McTodd’s eminence in the world of literature, you should meet him in London and bring him back here yourself.”

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人素来是他作品的热心崇拜者。她曾去信相邀：他若来英格兰，请到布兰丁斯一游。他眼下正在伦敦，明日便下乡小住两星期。康斯坦丝夫人的提议是：为表对麦克托德先生在文学界崇高声望的敬意，您应当到伦敦去迎他，再亲自把他接回来。

===Original===
Lord Emsworth remembered now. He also remembered that this positively infernal scheme had not been his sister Constance’s in the first place. It was Baxter who had made the suggestion, and Constance had approved. He made use of the recovered pince-nez to glower through them at his secretary; and not for the first time in recent months was aware of a feeling that this fellow Baxter was becoming a dashed infliction. Baxter was getting above himself, throwing his weight about, making himself a confounded nuisance. He wished he could get rid of the man. But where could he find an adequate successor? That was the trouble. With all his drawbacks, Baxter was efficient. Nevertheless, for a moment Lord Emsworth toyed with the pleasant dream of dismissing him. And it is possible, such was his exasperation, that he might on this occasion have done something practical in that direction, had not the library door at this moment opened for the third time, to admit yet another intruder﻿—at the sight of whom his lordship’s militant mood faded weakly.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵现在想起来了。他还想起来，这个十足坑人的方案，最初压根儿不是他妹妹康斯坦丝的主意。出主意的是巴克斯特，康斯坦丝只是点了头。他借助那副失而复得的夹鼻眼镜，隔着镜片对秘书怒目而视；近几个月来这已不是头一回，他有种感觉：这个巴克斯特正变成一桩天降的折磨。巴克斯特越来越不知天高地厚，到处作威作福，把自己弄成个讨厌的祸害。他巴不得能把这人打发走。可他上哪儿再找一个合格的接班人呢？麻烦就在这里。巴克斯特纵有百般缺点，毕竟干练。尽管如此，有一瞬间，埃姆斯沃思勋爵还是把开掉他的美梦把玩了一番。以其懊恼之深，这一回他没准儿真会朝那个方向干出点实际行动来——若非书房的门恰于此刻第三次打开，放进又一位不速之客的话；一见来人，勋爵大人的战斗情绪便蔫软地消散了。

===Original===
“Oh﻿—hallo, Connie!” he said, guiltily, like a small boy caught in the jam cupboard. Somehow his sister always had this effect upon him.

===Chinese===
「哦——哈罗，康妮（Connie）！」他说，一副心虚的模样，像在果酱橱里被当场拿住的小男孩。不知怎么，他妹妹对他总有这种效果。

===Original===
Of all those who had entered the library that morning the new arrival was the best worth looking at. Lord Emsworth was tall and lean and scraggy; Rupert Baxter thickset and handicapped by that vaguely grubby appearance which is presented by swarthy young men of bad complexion; and even Beach, though dignified, and Freddie, though slim, would never have got far in a beauty competition. But Lady Constance Keeble really took the eye. She was a strikingly handsome woman in the middle forties. She had a fair, broad brow, teeth of a perfect even whiteness, and the carriage of an empress. Her eyes were large and grey, and gentle﻿—and incidentally misleading, for gentle was hardly the adjective which anybody who knew her would have applied to Lady Constance. Though genial enough when she got her way, on the rare occasions when people attempted to thwart her she was apt to comport herself in a manner reminiscent of Cleopatra on one of the latter’s bad mornings.

===Chinese===
这天早晨进过书房的诸人当中，数这位新来的最耐看。埃姆斯沃思勋爵又高又瘦又干瘪；鲁珀特·巴克斯特五短身材，吃亏在那层说不清道不明的邋遢相——肤色黝黑、脸皮糟糕的年轻人素来这副观感；就连比奇，尽管气度庄重，弗雷迪，尽管身条苗条，拉去选美也走不出多远。可康斯坦丝·基布尔夫人（Lady Constance Keeble）实实在在夺人眼目。她是位四十五六岁、容貌出众的美人，额头白皙开阔，一口牙齿匀净雪白，仪态俨然女皇。她的眼睛大而灰，而且温柔——顺便说一句，这颇能骗人，因为凡是认识康斯坦丝夫人的，谁也不会拿「温柔」这个词来形容她。她诸事顺遂时固然和蔼可亲，可一旦有人胆敢作梗——这种场合虽然罕有——她的做派便容易叫人想起克娄巴特拉（Cleopatra）在某个坏脾气早晨的样子。

===Original===
“I hope I am not disturbing you,” said Lady Constance with a bright smile. “I just came in to tell you to be sure not to forget, Clarence, that you are going to London this afternoon to meet Mr. McTodd.”

===Chinese===
「希望我没打扰你们。」康斯坦丝夫人含着明朗的笑说。「我进来只是提醒你千万别忘了，克拉伦斯（Clarence），你今天下午要去伦敦接麦克托德先生。」

===Original===
“I was just telling Lord Emsworth,” said Baxter, “that the car would be at the door at two.”

===Chinese===
「我刚才正跟埃姆斯沃思勋爵说，」巴克斯特道，「汽车两点整会到门口。」

===Original===
“Thank you, Mr. Baxter. Of course I might have known that you would not forget. You are so wonderfully capable. I don’t know what in the world we would do without you.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢你，巴克斯特先生。我自然早该知道你不会忘的。你真是能干得不得了。真不知道没有你，我们可怎么办。」

===Original===
The Efficient Baxter bowed. But, though gratified, he was not overwhelmed by the tribute. The same thought had often occurred to him independently.

===Chinese===
能干的巴克斯特（the Efficient Baxter）躬了躬身。不过，这一番恭维虽令他受用，却不足以令他飘飘然。同样的想法，他早就独立产生过许多回了。

===Original===
“If you will excuse me,” he said, “I have one or two things to attend to﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「失陪了，」他说，「我还有一两件事要料理……」

===Original===
“Certainly, Mr. Baxter.”

===Chinese===
「当然，巴克斯特先生。」

===Original===
The Efficient One withdrew through the door in the bookshelf. He realised that his employer was in fractious mood, but knew that he was leaving him in capable hands.

===Chinese===
这位能人从书架里的那扇门退了出去。他清楚雇主正处在烦躁的关口，但他知道自己这是把他交到了一双可靠的手里。

===Original===
Lord Emsworth turned from the window, out of which he had been gazing with a plaintive detachment.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵从窗前转过身来；方才他一直望着窗外，一副哀怨的出神。

===Original===
“Look here, Connie,” he grumbled feebly. “You know I hate literary fellows. It’s bad enough having them in the house, but when it comes to going to London to fetch ’em﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我说，康妮，」他有气无力地咕哝。「你知道我讨厌文人。让他们住进家里已经够受的了，还要我去伦敦接他们……」

===Original===
He shuffled morosely. It was a perpetual grievance of his, this practice of his sister’s of collecting literary celebrities and dumping them down in the home for indeterminate visits. You never knew when she was going to spring another on you. Already since the beginning of the year he had suffered from a round dozen of the species at brief intervals; and at this very moment his life was being poisoned by the fact that Blandings was sheltering a certain Miss Aileen Peavey, the mere thought of whom was enough to turn the sunshine off as with a tap.

===Chinese===
他郁郁地蹭着脚步。妹妹这个癖好——收罗文坛名流，往家里一卸，做客期无限不定——是他的永久心病。你永远不知道她什么时候又会给你变出下一个来。打年初到现在，他已隔三差五领教了这个物种的整整一打；而此刻，他的日子正被一件事毒化着：布兰丁斯正庇护着某位艾琳·皮维小姐（Miss Aileen Peavey），单是想起此人，便足以像拧水龙头似的把阳光拧灭。

===Original===
“Can’t stand literary fellows,” proceeded his lordship. “Never could. And, by Jove, literary females are worse. Miss Peavey﻿ ﻿…” Here words temporarily failed the owner of Blandings. “Miss Peavey﻿ ﻿…” he resumed after an eloquent pause. “Who is Miss Peavey?”

===Chinese===
「文人我受不了，」勋爵大人接着说。「从来就受不了。而且，天哪，女文人更糟。皮维小姐……」说到此处，布兰丁斯的主人一时语塞。「皮维小姐……」在一段意味深长的停顿之后，他重新开腔。「皮维小姐究竟是谁？」

===Original===
“My dear Clarence,” replied Lady Constance tolerantly, for the fine morning had made her mild and amiable, “if you do not know that Aileen is one of the leading poetesses of the younger school, you must be very ignorant.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的克拉伦斯，」康斯坦丝夫人宽容地答道——晴朗的早晨已使她温和可亲，「你若不知道艾琳是新派诗人中的翘楚，那你也太无知了。」

===Original===
“I don’t mean that. I know she writes poetry. I mean who *is* she? You suddenly produced her here like a rabbit out of a hat,” said his lordship, in a tone of strong resentment. “Where did you find her?”

===Chinese===
「我不是那个意思。我知道她写诗。我是问她究竟*是*谁？你突然把她在这儿变了出来，像从帽子里掏出只兔子。」勋爵大人以强烈的怨愤口吻说。「你从哪儿把她找来的？」

===Original===
“I first made Aileen’s acquaintance on an Atlantic liner when Joe and I were coming back from our trip round the world. She was very kind to me when I was feeling the motion of the vessel.﻿ ﻿… If you mean what is her family, I think Aileen told me once that she was connected with the Rutlandshire Peaveys.”

===Chinese===
「我初识艾琳，是在乔（Joe）和我环游世界归途的大西洋邮轮上。我晕船晕得难受，她照料了我。……你若问的是她的家世，我记得艾琳说过一回，她家跟拉特兰郡（Rutlandshire）的皮维家沾着亲。」

===Original===
“Never heard of them!” snapped Lord Emsworth. “And, if they’re anything like Miss Peavey, God help Rutlandshire!”

===Chinese===
「从没听说过！」埃姆斯沃思勋爵厉声道。「要是他们跟皮维小姐一个模样，愿上帝保佑拉特兰郡！」

===Original===
Tranquil as Lady Constance’s mood was this morning, an ominous stoniness came into her grey eyes at these words, and there is little doubt that in another instant she would have discharged at her mutinous brother one of those shattering comebacks for which she had been celebrated in the family from nursery days onward; but at this juncture the Efficient Baxter appeared again through the bookshelf.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人今晨的心境尽管安详，听了这话，灰眼睛里却泛起一层不祥的冷硬；几乎毫无疑问，再过一瞬，她就要朝她那位犯上的兄弟放出那种雷霆式的抢白——自育婴室时代起，她便以此闻名全家；但不迟不早，恰在此时，能干的巴克斯特又从书架里现身了。

===Original===
“Excuse me,” said Baxter, securing attention with a flash of his spectacles. “I forgot to mention, Lord Emsworth, that, to suit everybody’s convenience, I have arranged that Miss Halliday shall call to see you at your club tomorrow after lunch.”

===Chinese===
「打扰了。」巴克斯特说，眼镜一闪，攫住了众人的注意。「我忘了提一句，埃姆斯沃思勋爵，为顾全各方的方便，我已安排好哈利迪小姐（Miss Halliday）明天午饭后到您的俱乐部去见您。」

===Original===
“Good Lord, Baxter!” The harassed peer started as if he had been bitten in the leg. “Who’s Miss Halliday? Not another literary female?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，巴克斯特！」这位焦头烂额的贵族惊得一跳，仿佛腿上叫什么咬了一口。「哈利迪小姐是谁？不会又是一位女文人吧？」

===Original===
“Miss Halliday is the young lady who is coming to Blandings to catalogue the library.”

===Chinese===
「哈利迪小姐就是那位要来布兰丁斯给藏书编目的年轻女士。」

===Original===
“Catalogue the library? What does it want cataloguing for?”

===Chinese===
「给藏书编目？藏书要编目干什么？」

===Original===
“It has not been done since the year 1885.”

===Chinese===
「自 1885 年以来就不曾编过。」

===Original===
“Well, and look how splendidly we’ve got along without it,” said Lord Emsworth acutely.

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛，瞧我们没这编目，这些年过得多红火。」埃姆斯沃思勋爵精辟地说。

===Original===
“Don’t be so ridiculous, Clarence,” said Lady Constance, annoyed. “The catalogue of a great library like this must be brought up to date.” She moved to the door. “I do wish you would try to wake up and take an interest in things. If it wasn’t for Mr. Baxter, I don’t know what would happen.”

===Chinese===
「别这么可笑，克拉伦斯。」康斯坦丝夫人生气地说。「这么一座大藏书楼，编目必须跟上时代。」她举步向门口走去。「我实在希望你想法子醒一醒，对事情上点儿心。若不是有巴克斯特先生，我真不知道会出什么乱子。」

===Original===
And with a beaming glance of approval at her ally she left the room. Baxter, coldly austere, returned to the subject under discussion.

===Chinese===
说罢，她朝自己的盟友投去赞许的灿然一瞥，出了房间。巴克斯特冷峻如霜，回到正题上来。

===Original===
“I have written to Miss Halliday suggesting two-thirty as a suitable hour for the interview.”

===Chinese===
「我已致函哈利迪小姐，提议两点半为会面的合适时间。」

===Original===
“But look here﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「可是，我说……」

===Original===
“You will wish to see her before definitely confirming the engagement.”

===Chinese===
「在正式敲定这桩聘任之前，您总要先见见她。」

===Original===
“Yes, but look here, I wish you wouldn’t go tying me up with all these appointments.”

===Chinese===
「是，可是我说，我希望你别拿这些个约会把我捆得死死的。」

===Original===
“I thought that as you were going to London to meet Mr. McTodd﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我以为，反正您要去伦敦接麦克托德先生……」

===Original===
“But I’m not going to London to meet Mr. McTodd,” cried Lord Emsworth with weak fury. “It’s out of the question. I can’t possibly leave Blandings. The weather may break at any moment. I don’t want to miss a day of it.”

===Chinese===
「可我根本不是要去伦敦接什么麦克托德先生。」埃姆斯沃思勋爵带着软弱的狂怒喊道。「绝无可能。我决不能离开布兰丁斯。天气随时可能变坏。我一天也不想错过。」

===Original===
“The arrangements are all made.”

===Chinese===
「一切都安排好了。」

===Original===
“Send the fellow a wire﻿ ﻿… ‘unavoidably detained.’ ”

===Chinese===
「给那家伙拍封电报……『因故受阻，恕难分身。』」

===Original===
“I could not take the responsibility for such a course myself,” said Baxter coldly. “But possibly if you were to make the suggestion to Lady Constance﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「这一处置，责任我本人可担待不起。」巴克斯特冷冷地说。「不过，您若把这建议向康斯坦丝夫人提一提，或许……」

===Original===
“Oh, dash it!” said Lord Emsworth unhappily, at once realising the impossibility of the scheme. “Oh, well, if I’ve got to go, I’ve got to go,” he said after a gloomy pause. “But to leave my garden and stew in London at this time of the year﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「噢，见鬼！」埃姆斯沃思勋爵怏怏不乐地说，登时明白了这方案绝无可能。「噢，好吧，要是非去不可，那就非去不可。」他黯然顿了顿，又开口道。「可是要撇下我的花园，赶在这个时节去伦敦受蒸熬……」

===Original===
There seemed nothing further to say on the subject. He took off his glasses, polished them, put them on again, and shuffled to the door. After all, he reflected, even though the car was coming for him at two, at least he had the morning, and he proposed to make the most of it. But his first careless rapture at the prospect of pottering among his flowers was dimmed, and would not be recaptured. He did not entertain any project so mad as the idea of defying his sister Constance, but he felt extremely bitter about the whole affair. Confound Constance!﻿ ﻿… Dash Baxter!﻿ ﻿… Miss Peavey﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这题目似乎再没什么可说的了。他摘下眼镜，擦了擦，重新戴上，蹭着步子朝门口走去。说到底，他寻思，汽车虽然两点就来接他，上午总还是他的，他打算物尽其用。但先前一想到要去花丛间闲逛时那股无忧无虑的欢畅已然黯淡，再也寻不回来了。他倒不曾动过什么疯狂到公然违抗妹妹康斯坦丝的念头，只觉得这整件事苦涩难当。让康斯坦丝见鬼去！……巴克斯特该死！……皮维小姐……

===Original===
The door closed behind Lord Emsworth.

===Chinese===
门在埃姆斯沃思勋爵身后关上了。

### II / II

===Original===
Lady Constance meanwhile, proceeding downstairs, had reached the big hall, when the door of the smoking-room opened and a head popped out. A round, grizzled head with a healthy pink face attached to it.

===Chinese===
与此同时，康斯坦丝夫人正往楼下走，已行至大厅，吸烟室的门开了，一颗脑袋探将出来。一颗圆滚滚、头发花白的脑袋，底下连着一张红润健康的粉红脸膛。

===Original===
“Connie!” said the head.

===Chinese===
「康妮！」那颗脑袋说。

===Original===
Lady Constance halted.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人收住了脚。

===Original===
“Yes, Joe?”

===Chinese===
「什么事，乔？」

===Original===
“Come in here a minute,” said the head. “Want to speak to you.”

===Chinese===
「进来待一会儿。」那颗脑袋说。「有话跟你说。」

===Original===
Lady Constance went into the smoking-room. It was large and cosily book-lined, and its window looked out on to an Italian garden. A wide fireplace occupied nearly the whole of one side of it, and in front of this, his legs spread to an invisible blaze, Mr. Joseph Keeble had already taken his stand. His manner was bluff, but an acute observer might have detected embarrassment in it.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人走进吸烟室。房间宽大，四壁排着书，温馨适意，窗子望出去是一座意大利式花园。一面宽大的壁炉几乎占了整整一侧墙，炉前，约瑟夫·基布尔先生（Mr. Joseph Keeble）早已就位，双腿朝着一炉看不见的火叉开。他神态爽直，但眼尖的看客或能从中辨出一丝窘迫。

===Original===
“What is it, Joe?” asked Lady Constance, and smiled pleasantly at her husband. When, two years previously, she had married this elderly widower, of whom the world knew nothing beyond the fact that he had amassed a large fortune in South African diamond mines, there had not been wanting cynics to set the match down as one of convenience, a purely business arrangement by which Mr. Keeble exchanged his money for Lady Constance’s social position. Such was not the case. It had been a genuine marriage of affection on both sides. Mr. Keeble worshipped his wife, and she was devoted to him, though never foolishly indulgent. They were a happy and united couple.

===Chinese===
「什么事，乔？」康斯坦丝夫人问，并向丈夫莞尔。两年前她嫁给这位年长的鳏夫——世人对他的全部了解，无非是他在南非钻石矿上攒下了一大笔家私——当时刻薄的闲人并不少见，把这门亲事断成一场便利婚姻，一桩纯然的买卖：基布尔先生以钱财，换康斯坦丝夫人的社会地位。事实绝非如此。这是一桩双方都出于真情的结合。基布尔先生敬爱妻子入骨，她也深爱着他，尽管从不愚蠢地姑息。他们是一对和美的夫妻。

===Original===
Mr. Keeble cleared his throat. He seemed to find some difficulty in speaking. And when he spoke it was not on the subject which he had intended to open, but on one which had already been worn out in previous conversations.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生清了清嗓子。他似乎开起口来有些费劲。而开了口，谈的也不是他原打算切入的正题，倒是一个先前谈话里早已磨破了嘴皮的话题。

===Original===
“Connie, I’ve been thinking about that necklace again.”

===Chinese===
「康妮，我又想起那条项链来了。」

===Original===
Lady Constance laughed.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人笑了。

===Original===
“Oh, don’t be silly, Joe. You haven’t called me into this stuffy room on a lovely morning like this to talk about that for the hundredth time.”

===Chinese===
「噢，别犯傻，乔。你总不会在这么个可爱的早晨，把我叫进这间闷罐子似的屋子，就为了第一百遍谈那桩事吧。」

===Original===
“Well, you know, there’s no sense in taking risks.”

===Chinese===
「呃，你知道，冒险是没有道理的。」

===Original===
“Don’t be absurd. What risks can there be?”

===Chinese===
「别荒唐了。能有什么险？」

===Original===
“There was a burglary over at Winstone Court, not ten miles from here, only a day or two ago.”

===Chinese===
「就在一两天前，温斯顿府（Winstone Court）遭了贼，离这儿还不到十英里。」

===Original===
“Don’t be so fussy, Joe.”

===Chinese===
「别这么大惊小怪，乔。」

===Original===
“That necklace cost nearly twenty thousand pounds,” said Mr. Keeble, in the reverent voice in which men of business traditions speak of large sums.

===Chinese===
「那条项链花了将近两万英镑。」基布尔先生说，用的是商界老传统的人谈到巨款时那种虔敬的声口。

===Original===
“I know.”

===Chinese===
「我知道。」

===Original===
“It ought to be in the bank.”

===Chinese===
「它本该存进银行。」

===Original===
“Once and for all, Joe,” said Lady Constance, losing her amiability and becoming suddenly imperious and Cleopatrine, “I will *not* keep that necklace in a bank. What on earth is the use of having a beautiful necklace if it is lying in the strongroom of a bank all the time? There is the County Ball coming on, and the Bachelors’ Ball after that, and﻿ ﻿… well, I *need* it. I will send the thing to the bank when we pass through London on our way to Scotland, but not till then. And I do wish you would stop worrying me about it.”

===Chinese===
「一句话说到底，乔，」康斯坦丝夫人和颜悦色荡然无存，陡然变得颐指气使、一派克娄巴特拉作风，「我*不*把那条项链存银行。一条美丽的项链，若一年到头躺在银行保险库里，要它究竟何用？郡舞会眼看就到，之后还有单身汉舞会，还有……总之，我*需要*它。等我们取道伦敦去苏格兰时，我自会把那物件送进银行，但在那之前休想。还有，我真希望你别再为这个来烦我。」

===Original===
There was a silence. Mr. Keeble was regretting now that his unfortunate poltroonery had stopped him from tackling in a straightforward and manly fashion the really important matter which was weighing on his mind: for he perceived that his remarks about the necklace, eminently sensible though they were, had marred the genial mood in which his wife had begun this interview. It was going to be more difficult now than ever to approach the main issue. Still, ruffled though she might be, the thing had to be done: for it involved a matter of finance, and in matters of finance Mr. Keeble was no longer a free agent. He and Lady Constance had a mutual banking account, and it was she who supervised the spending of it. This was an arrangement, subsequently regretted by Mr. Keeble, which had been come to in the early days of the honeymoon, when men are apt to do foolish things.

===Chinese===
一阵沉默。基布尔先生此刻懊悔不迭：悔他那倒霉的怯懦，害他没能以直截了当的男子汉做派，去解决那件真正压在心上的大事；因为他察觉，他那番关于项链的议论——尽管通情达理到十分——已经败坏了妻子开这场谈话时的和悦心情。如今再要切入正题，比以往任何时候都难了。然而，她纵使被撩恼了，这桩事也非办不可：因为它牵扯到财务，而在财务上，基布尔先生早已不是自由身。他与康斯坦丝夫人共有一个银行账户，而监督账户开销的正是她。这一安排——基布尔先生事后追悔莫及——是蜜月初期定下的，人在那段日子里最容易办糊涂事。

===Original===
Mr. Keeble coughed. Not the sharp, efficient cough which we have heard Rupert Baxter uttering in the library, but a feeble, strangled thing like the bleat of a diffident sheep.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生咳嗽了一声。不是我们方才听鲁珀特·巴克斯特在书房里咳嗽的那种干脆高效的咳，而是一声有气无力、憋在嗓子里的动静，像一只怯生生的羊在咩咩。

===Original===
“Connie,” he said. “Er﻿—Connie.”

===Chinese===
「康妮。」他说。「呃——康妮。」

===Original===
And at the words a sort of cold film seemed to come over Lady Constance’s eyes: for some sixth sense told her what subject it was that was now about to be introduced.

===Chinese===
这几个字一出口，一层冷膜仿佛蒙上了康斯坦丝夫人的眼睛：某种第六感告诉她，眼下将要引出的是什么话题。

===Original===
“Connie, I﻿—er﻿—had a letter from Phyllis this morning.”

===Chinese===
「康妮，我——呃——今天早上收到了菲利斯（Phyllis）一封信。」

===Original===
Lady Constance said nothing. Her eyes gleamed for an instant, then became frozen again. Her intuition had not deceived her.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人一言不发。她的眼睛倏地一亮，随即又冻结成冰。她的直觉没有骗她。

===Original===
Into the married life of this happy couple only one shadow had intruded itself up to the present. But unfortunately it was a shadow of considerable proportions, a kind of super-shadow; and its effect had been chilling. It was Phyllis, Mr. Keeble’s stepdaughter, who had caused it﻿—by the simple process of jilting the rich and suitable young man whom Lady Constance had attached to her (rather in the manner of a conjurer forcing a card upon his victim) and running off and marrying a far from rich and quite unsuitable person of whom all that seemed to be known was that his name was Jackson. Mr. Keeble, whose simple creed was that Phyllis could do no wrong, had been prepared to accept the situation philosophically; but his wife’s wrath had been deep and enduring. So much so that the mere mentioning of the girl’s name must be accounted to him for a brave deed, Lady Constance having specifically stated that she never wished to hear it again.

===Chinese===
迄今为止，这对美满夫妻的婚姻生活里只潜入过一道阴影。可惜这道阴影块头可观，堪称超级阴影；其效果寒气逼人。肇事的正是菲利斯，基布尔先生的继女——过程简单得很：她甩掉了康斯坦丝夫人替她拴好的那位有钱又般配的青年（手法颇似魔术师硬塞一张牌给观众），跟一个远说不上有钱、也全然不般配的家伙私奔成婚，而关于此公，世人似乎只晓得他姓杰克逊（Jackson）。基布尔先生的单纯信条是菲利斯永不会错，他本打算达观地认下这个局面；他妻子的雷霆之怒却既深且久。久到这般地步：光是提一提这姑娘的名字，都得算他一桩英勇事迹——康斯坦丝夫人可是白纸黑字说过，永远不想再听见它。

===Original===
Keenly alive to this prejudice of hers, Mr. Keeble stopped after making his announcement, and had to rattle his keys in his pocket in order to acquire the necessary courage to continue. He was not looking at his wife, but he knew just how forbidding her expression must be. This task of his was no easy, congenial task for a pleasant summer morning.

===Chinese===
对她这层偏见体察入微的基布尔先生，通报完毕便住了口，只得把口袋里的钥匙拨弄得咔啦作响，好攒足继续下去所必需的勇气。他没有看妻子，但他确切知道，她的脸色此刻必定何等凛然。他这桩差事，可不是宜人的夏日清晨那份轻省差事。

===Original===
“She says in her letter,” proceeded Mr. Keeble, his eyes on the carpet and his cheeks a deeper pink, “that young Jackson has got the chance of buying a big farm﻿ ﻿… in Lincolnshire, I think she said﻿ ﻿… if he can raise three thousand pounds.”

===Chinese===
「她在信里说，」基布尔先生续道，眼睛盯着地毯，脸蛋红得更深了，「小杰克逊得了个机会，能买下一座大农场……在林肯郡（Lincolnshire），我记得她是这么说的……只要他筹得出三千英镑。」

===Original===
He paused, and stole a glance at his wife. It was as he had feared. She had congealed. Like some spell, the name Jackson had apparently turned her to marble. It was like the Pygmalion and Galatea business working the wrong way round. She was presumably breathing, but there was no sign of it.

===Chinese===
他顿住，偷觑了妻子一眼。不出所料。她凝结了。「杰克逊」这个名字像一道咒语，分明把她变成了大理石。这好比皮格马利翁与加拉泰亚那套把戏倒着演了一遍。她想必还在呼吸，但看不出一丝迹象。

===Original===
“So I was just thinking,” said Mr. Keeble, producing another obbligato on the keys, “it just crossed my mind﻿ ﻿… it isn’t as if the thing were a speculation﻿ ﻿… the place is apparently coining money﻿ ﻿… present owner only selling because he wants to go abroad﻿ ﻿… it occurred to me﻿ ﻿… and they would pay good interest on the loan﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「所以我就在琢磨，」基布尔先生说，就着钥匙又奏了一段助奏，「我脑子里刚闪过……这事又不是投机……那地方明摆着财源滚滚……现在的东家只因为想出国才卖……我就寻思……再说他们借这笔钱愿意付可观的利息……」

===Original===
“What loan?” inquired the statue icily, coming to life.

===Chinese===
「什么借款？」那尊雕像冷冰冰地发问，活了过来。

===Original===
“Well, what I was thinking﻿ ﻿… just a suggestion, you know﻿ ﻿… what struck me was that if you were willing we might﻿ ﻿… good investment, you know, and nowadays it’s deuced hard to find good investments﻿ ﻿… I was thinking that we might lend them the money.”

===Chinese===
「呃，我是这么想的……不过是个提议，你知道……我琢磨的是，你要是愿意，咱们兴许可以……好投资啊，你知道，如今要寻个好投资难于上青天……我在想，咱们兴许可以把钱借给他们。」

===Original===
He stopped. But he had got the thing out and felt happier. He rattled his keys again, and rubbed the back of his head against the mantelpiece. The friction seemed to give him confidence.

===Chinese===
他收住了。但话已出了口，他心里痛快了些。他把钥匙又拨拉了一阵响，后脑勺在壁炉架上蹭来蹭去。这摩擦似乎给了他底气。

===Original===
“We had better settle this thing once and for all, Joe,” said Lady Constance. “As you know, when we were married, I was ready to do everything for Phyllis. I was prepared to be a mother to her. I gave her every chance, took her everywhere. And what happened?”

===Chinese===
「这件事咱们最好一了百了，乔。」康斯坦丝夫人说。「你也知道，我们结婚的时候，我原是什么都肯为菲利斯做的。我准备给她当母亲。我给她一切机会，带她见一切世面。结果怎样？」

===Original===
“Yes, I know. But﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「是，我知道。可是……」

===Original===
“She became engaged to a man with plenty of money﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「她跟一位家财万贯的先生订了婚……」

===Original===
“Shocking young ass,” interjected Mr. Keeble, perking up for a moment at the recollection of the late lamented, whom he had never liked. “And a rip, what’s more. I’ve heard stories.”

===Chinese===
「混账的小蠢驴。」基布尔先生插嘴道，想起那位已故的痛惜对象，一时来了精神——此人他向来就不喜欢。「而且还流气。我听过好些他的传闻。」

===Original===
“Nonsense! If you are going to believe all the gossip you hear about people, nobody would be safe. He was a delightful young man and he would have made Phyllis perfectly happy. Instead of marrying him, she chose to go off with this﻿—Jackson.” Lady Constance’s voice quivered. Greater scorn could hardly have been packed into two syllables. “After what has happened, I certainly intend to have nothing more to do with her. I shall not lend them a penny, so please do not let us continue this discussion any longer. I hope I am not an unjust woman, but I must say that I consider, after the way Phyllis behaved﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「胡扯！要是道听途说你都信，那天下就没有一个清白人了。他是位讨人喜欢的青年，本可以让菲利斯幸福美满。她倒不嫁，偏跟这个——杰克逊跑了。」康斯坦丝夫人的声音发颤。两个音节里，几乎再塞不进更多的轻蔑。「事已至此，我自然是打定主意跟她再无瓜葛。我一个便士也不借给他们，所以请别再让这场讨论继续下去了。我自认不是个不公道的女人，但我必须说，念及菲利斯的所作所为……」

===Original===
The sudden opening of the door caused her to break off. Lord Emsworth, mould-stained and wearing a deplorable old jacket, pottered into the room. He peered benevolently at his sister and his brother-in-law, but seemed unaware that he was interrupting a conversation.

===Chinese===
门突然打开，断了她的下文。埃姆斯沃思勋爵一身霉渍，穿着一件糟糕透顶的旧上衣，闲逛了进来。他和善地眯眼端详妹妹和妹夫，却似乎浑然不觉自己正打断一场谈话。

===Original===
“*Gardening as a Fine Art*,” he murmured. “Connie, have you seen a book called *Gardening as a Fine Art*? I was reading it in here last night. *Gardening as a Fine Art*. That is the title. Now, where can it have got to?” His dreamy eye flitted to and fro. “I want to show it to McAllister. There is a passage in it that directly refutes his anarchistic views on﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「《园艺艺术论》（*Gardening as a Fine Art*）。」他喃喃道。「康妮，你见过一本叫《园艺艺术论》的书吗？我昨晚就在这儿读的。《园艺艺术论》。书名就是这个。咦，它能跑哪儿去了呢？」他蒙眬的眼睛来回逡巡。「我要拿给麦卡利斯特瞧。里头有一段，直接驳倒了他那套关于……的无政府主义主张。」

===Original===
“It is probably on one of the shelves,” said Lady Constance shortly.

===Chinese===
「多半在哪层书架上。」康斯坦丝夫人淡然道。

===Original===
“On one of the shelves?” said Lord Emsworth, obviously impressed by this bright suggestion. “Why, of course, to be sure.”

===Chinese===
「在书架上？」埃姆斯沃思勋爵说，显然被这个机灵的主意镇住了。「哎呀，可不是嘛，准是。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble was rattling his keys moodily. A mutinous expression was on his pink face. These moments of rebellion did not come to him very often, for he loved his wife with a doglike affection and had grown accustomed to being ruled by her, but now resentment filled him. She was unreasonable, he considered. She ought to have realised how strongly he felt about poor little Phyllis. It was too infernally cold-blooded to abandon the poor child like an old shoe simply because﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
基布尔先生正闷头拨弄钥匙。他那张粉红的脸上是一副犯上的神情。这种反骨上头的时刻于他并不常有，因为他爱妻子爱得像条忠犬，也惯了受她辖制，可此刻他满腹怨气。她不近情理，他认定。她本该明白，他对可怜的小菲利斯感情有多深。只因为……就把那可怜的孩子当一只旧鞋似的甩了，未免狠心得没有天理……

===Original===
“Are you going?” he asked, observing his wife moving to the door.

===Chinese===
「你要走了？」他问，眼见妻子朝门口移动。

===Original===
“Yes. I am going into the garden,” said Lady Constance. “Why? Was there anything else you wanted to talk to me about?”

===Chinese===
「是啊。我到花园去。」康斯坦丝夫人说。「怎么？你还有别的事要同我谈？」

===Original===
“No,” said Mr. Keeble despondently. “Oh, no.”

===Chinese===
「没了。」基布尔先生颓然道。「哦，没了。」

===Original===
Lady Constance left the room, and a deep masculine silence fell. Mr. Keeble rubbed the back of his head meditatively against the mantelpiece, and Lord Emsworth scratched among the bookshelves.

===Chinese===
康斯坦丝夫人出了屋，一片深沉的、男子汉的寂静随之降临。基布尔先生若有所思地把后脑勺往壁炉架上蹭，埃姆斯沃思勋爵在书架间抓挠寻书。

===Original===
“Clarence!” said Mr. Keeble suddenly. An idea﻿—one might almost say an inspiration﻿—had come to him.

===Chinese===
「克拉伦斯！」基布尔先生霍然开口。一个主意——几乎可以说一线天启——落到了他头上。

===Original===
“Eh?” responded his lordship absently. He had found his book and was turning its pages, absorbed.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」勋爵大人心不在焉地应了一声。他已找到那本书，正埋头翻着书页。

===Original===
“Clarence, can you﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「克拉伦斯，你能不能……」

===Original===
“Angus McAllister,” observed Lord Emsworth bitterly, “is an obstinate, stiff-necked son of Belial. The writer of this book distinctly states in so many words﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「安格斯·麦卡利斯特，」埃姆斯沃思勋爵忿忿然评道，「是个顽固不化、硬着脖颈的彼列之子。这本书的作者白纸黑字、一清二楚地写着……」

===Original===
“Clarence, can you lend me three thousand pounds on good security and keep it dark from Connie?”

===Chinese===
「克拉伦斯，你能不能借我三千英镑，抵押从优，并且瞒住康妮？」

===Original===
Lord Emsworth blinked.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵眨了眨眼。

===Original===
“Keep something dark from Connie?” He raised his eyes from his book in order to peer at this visionary with a gentle pity. “My dear fellow, it can’t be done.”

===Chinese===
「瞒住康妮什么事？」他从书上抬起眼睛，好怀着温柔的怜悯端详这位空想家。「亲爱的伙计，办不到的。」

===Original===
“She would never know. I will tell you just why I want this money﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「她决不会知道。我这就告诉你，我到底为什么要这笔钱……」

===Original===
“Money?” Lord Emsworth’s eye had become vacant again. He was reading once more. “Money? Money, my dear fellow? Money? Money? What money? If I have said once,” declared Lord Emsworth, “that Angus McAllister is all wrong on the subject of hollyhocks, I’ve said it a hundred times.”

===Chinese===
「钱？」埃姆斯沃思勋爵的眼神又空了。他重新读上了书。「钱？钱，亲爱的伙计？钱？钱？什么钱？我要是说过一回，」埃姆斯沃思勋爵宣称，「安格斯·麦卡利斯特在蜀葵问题上是全盘皆错，那我准已说了一百回。」

===Original===
“Let me explain. This three thousand pounds﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「容我解释。这三千英镑……」

===Original===
“My dear fellow, no. No, no. It was like you,” said his lordship with a vague heartiness, “it was like you﻿—good and generous﻿—to make this offer, but I have ample, thank you, ample. I don’t *need* three thousand pounds.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的伙计，不。不，不。你真是高抬我了，」勋爵大人心不在焉地热络道，「你真是高抬我了——又好心又慷慨——肯这样惠赐，可惜我手头充裕，谢谢，充裕得很。我*不缺*这三千英镑。」

===Original===
“You don’t understand. I﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「你误会了。我……」

===Original===
“No, no. No, no. But I am very much obliged, all the same. It was kind of you, my dear fellow, to give me the opportunity. Very kind. Very, very, very kind,” proceeded his lordship, trailing to the door and reading as he went. “Oh, very, very, very﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「不，不。不，不。不过我还是感激不尽。谢谢你给我这个机会，亲爱的伙计，你太客气了。太客气了。太、太、太客气了。」勋爵大人絮絮叨叨，一路且读且行，蹭到门口。「哦，太、太、太……」

===Original===
The door closed behind him.

===Chinese===
门在他身后关上了。

===Original===
“Oh, *damn*!” said Mr. Keeble.

===Chinese===
「哦，*见鬼*！」基布尔先生说。

===Original===
He sank into a chair in a state of profound dejection. He thought of the letter he would have to write to Phyllis. Poor little Phyllis﻿ ﻿… he would have to tell her that what she asked could not be managed. And why, thought Mr. Keeble sourly, as he rose from his seat and went to the writing-table, could it not be managed? Simply because he was a weak-kneed, spineless creature who was afraid of a pair of grey eyes that had a tendency to freeze.

===Chinese===
他颓然陷进椅子里，一蹶不振。他想到那封不得不写给菲利斯的信。可怜的小菲利斯……他将不得不告诉她，她求的事办不成了。而为什么，基布尔先生酸楚地想，一面起身走向写字台，为什么就办不成？只因为他是个膝盖发软、没有脊梁的可怜虫，害怕一双动不动就结冰的灰眼睛。

===Original===
“My dear Phyllis,” he wrote.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的菲利斯。」他写道。

===Original===
Here he stopped. How on earth was he to put it? What a letter to have to write! Mr. Keeble placed his head between his hands and groaned aloud.

===Chinese===
写到这里他停住了。这可教他如何措辞？这是封多么难下笔的信！基布尔先生把脑袋埋进双手，出声地呻吟起来。

===Original===
“Hallo, Uncle Joe!”

===Chinese===
「哈罗，乔叔！」

===Original===
The letter-writer, turning sharply, was aware﻿—without pleasure﻿—of his nephew Frederick, standing beside his chair. He eyed him resentfully, for he was not only exasperated but startled. He had not heard the door open. It was as if the smooth-haired youth had popped up out of a trap.

===Chinese===
写信人猛地回头，看见——毫无欣喜——他的侄子弗雷德里克立在椅子旁。他怨怼地打量着对方，因为他不但恼火，而且受了惊。他没听见门响。这位头发抹得溜光的少爷，简直像从活板门里蹦出来的一样。

===Original===
“Came in through the window,” explained the Hon. Freddie. “I say, Uncle Joe.”

===Chinese===
「从窗户进来的。」弗雷迪少爷解释道。「我说，乔叔。」

===Original===
“Well, what is it?”

===Chinese===
「嗯，什么事？」

===Original===
“I say, Uncle Joe,” said Freddie, “can you lend me a thousand quid?”

===Chinese===
「我说，乔叔，」弗雷迪说，「你能借我一千镑吗？」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble uttered a yelp like a pinched Pomeranian.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生发出一声尖叫，像一只被掐了一下的博美犬。

### III / III

===Original===
As Mr. Keeble, red-eyed and overwrought, rose slowly from his chair and began to swell in ominous silence, his nephew raised his hand appealingly. It began to occur to the Hon. Freddie that he had perhaps not led up to his request with the maximum of smooth tact.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生眼圈发红、心潮翻涌，从椅子里缓缓站起，开始在不祥的沉默里鼓胀起来；他的侄子则讨饶似的举起手来。弗雷迪少爷这才渐渐觉出，自己方才引出请求的路数，或许未能把圆滑得体发挥到极致。

===Original===
“Half a jiffy!” he entreated. “I say, don’t go in off the deep end for just a second. I can explain.”

===Chinese===
「稍等一忽儿！」他告饶道。「我说，先别急着暴跳，就一秒钟。我能解释。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble’s feelings expressed themselves in a loud snort.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生的情绪，用一声响亮的鼻息表达了出来。

===Original===
“Explain!”

===Chinese===
「解释！」

===Original===
“Well, I can. Whole trouble was, I started at the wrong end. Shouldn’t have sprung it on you like that. The fact is, Uncle Joe, I’ve got a scheme. I give you my word that, if you’ll only put off having apoplexy for about three minutes,” said Freddie, scanning his fermenting relative with some anxiety, “I can shove you on to a good thing. Honestly I can. And all I say is, if this scheme I’m talking about is worth a thousand quid to you, will you slip it across? I’m game to spill it and leave it to your honesty to cash up if the thing looks good to you.”

===Chinese===
「呃，我能嘛。全部的症结在于，我从错误的那头起了个头。本不该那么劈头砸给您的。实情是，乔叔，我有个方案。我向您担保，只要您肯把中风推迟个三分钟左右，」弗雷迪说，一面带着几分忧色打量他那位正在发酵的亲戚，「我就能把您扶上一条财路。真心话。我全部的要求是：如果我讲的这个方案在您看来值一千镑，您肯不肯掏？我乐意和盘托出，成色好坏您自管验，看着值就凭良心付账。」

===Original===
“A thousand pounds!”

===Chinese===
「一千英镑！」

===Original===
“Nice round sum,” urged Freddie ingratiatingly.

===Chinese===
「整数，多圆整。」弗雷迪曲意逢迎地撺掇道。

===Original===
“Why,” demanded Mr. Keeble, now somewhat recovered, “do you want a thousand pounds?”

===Chinese===
「你，」业已稍稍复原的基布尔先生诘问道，「要一千英镑做什么？」

===Original===
“Well, who doesn’t, if it comes to that?” said Freddie. “But I don’t mind telling you my special reason for wanting it at just this moment, if you’ll swear to keep it under your hat as far as the guv’nor is concerned.”

===Chinese===
「这个，真要掰扯起来，谁不想要？」弗雷迪说。「不过只要您肯对天发誓，这事在老爸那头您守口如瓶，我倒不介意告诉你，我偏偏赶在这节骨眼上要它的特殊原因。」

===Original===
“If you mean that you wish me not to repeat to your father anything you may tell me in confidence, naturally I should not dream of doing such a thing.”

===Chinese===
「你若是指，要我不得把你私下里讲的话转述给你父亲，那我自然做梦也不会干这种事。」

===Original===
Freddie looked puzzled. His was no lightning brain.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪一脸困惑。他那脑子可不是闪电型的。

===Original===
“Can’t quite work that out,” he confessed. “Do you mean you will tell him or you won’t?”

===Chinese===
「这句我算不过来。」他招认道。「您是会说给他听，还是不会？」

===Original===
“I will not tell him.”

===Chinese===
「我不会告诉他。」

===Original===
“Good old Uncle Joe!” said Freddie, relieved. “A topper! I’ve always said so. Well, look here, you know all the trouble there’s been about my dropping a bit on the races lately?”

===Chinese===
「好一尊乔叔！」弗雷迪如释重负。「顶呱呱！我一向这么说。呃，是这样，近来我在赛马上折了点钱，惹出的那些乱子您都知道吧？」

===Original===
“I do.”

===Chinese===
「知道。」

===Original===
“Between ourselves, I dropped about five hundred of the best. And I just want to ask you one simple question. *Why* did I drop it?”

===Chinese===
「咱俩关起门说，我折了足足五百块响当当的英镑。我只想请教您一个简单问题。*为什么*我会折？」

===Original===
“Because you were an infernal young ass.”

===Chinese===
「因为你是头不可救药的小蠢驴。」

===Original===
“Well, yes,” agreed Freddie, having considered the point, “you might put it that way, of course. But why was I an ass?”

===Chinese===
「呃，对，」弗雷迪把这话掂量了一番，表示认可，「您当然可以这么讲。可我为什么会是头蠢驴？」

===Original===
“Good God!” exclaimed the exasperated Mr. Keeble. “Am I a psychoanalyst?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷！」被撩火了的基布尔先生失声道。「难道我是个精神分析学家？」

===Original===
“I mean to say, if you come right down to it, I lost all that stuff simply because I was on the wrong side of the fence. It’s a mug’s game betting on horses. The only way to make money is to be a bookie, and that’s what I’m going to do if you’ll part with that thousand. Pal of mine, who was up at Oxford with me, is in a bookie’s office, and they’re game to take me in too if I can put up a thousand quid. Only I must let them know quick, because the offer’s not going to be open for ever. You’ve no notion what a deuce of a lot of competition there is for that sort of job.”

===Chinese===
「我是说，真要刨到根上，我丢了那一票钱，纯粹因为我站错了栅栏这一边。押马是傻子才玩的游戏。挣钱的唯一路子是当赛马庄家，只要您肯割舍那一千镑，我就打算这么办。我一个哥们儿，当年跟我一道在牛津（Oxford）念书，如今在一家庄家办事处做事；他们说好，只要我拿得出那一千镑，也肯把我收进去。只是我得赶紧给他们回话，因为这机会不会永远敞着门。您根本想不到，那种差事抢的人有多多。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble, who had been endeavouring with some energy to get a word in during this harangue, now contrived to speak.

===Chinese===
在这通宏论期间一直铆足了劲想插嘴的基布尔先生，这会儿总算得逞了。

===Original===
“And do you seriously suppose that I would﻿ ﻿… But what’s the use of wasting time talking? I have no means of laying my hands on the sum you mention. If I had,” said Mr. Keeble wistfully. “If I had﻿ ﻿…” And his eye strayed to the letter on the desk, the letter which had got as far as My dear Phyllis and stuck there.

===Chinese===
「你当真以为我会……？可是白费唾沫有什么用？你说的那笔款项，我根本无从到手。我要是能有，」基布尔先生怅然道。「我要是能有……」他的目光溜到了写字台上那封信上——那封刚刚写到「亲爱的菲利斯」便卡了壳的信。

===Original===
Freddie gazed upon him with cordial sympathy.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪满怀热忱的同情，凝望着他。

===Original===
“Oh, I know how you’re situated, Uncle Joe, and I’m dashed sorry for you. I mean, Aunt Constance and all that.”

===Chinese===
「唉，您那本难念的经我知道，乔叔，我替您难过死了。我是说，康斯坦丝姑妈那一套什么的。」

===Original===
“What!” Irksome as Mr. Keeble sometimes found the peculiar condition of his financial arrangements, he had always had the consolation of supposing that they were a secret between his wife and himself. “What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
「什么！」基布尔先生有时也觉得自家财务安排的特殊状况恼人，但一直有个安慰：总以为那是他跟妻子两个人的秘密。「你什么意思？」

===Original===
“Well, I know that Aunt Constance keeps an eye on the doubloons and checks the outgoings pretty narrowly. And I think it’s a dashed shame that she won’t unbuckle to help poor old Phyllis. A girl,” said Freddie, “I always liked. Bally shame! Why the dickens shouldn’t she marry that fellow Jackson? I mean, love’s love,” said Freddie, who felt strongly on this point.

===Chinese===
「呃，我知道康斯坦丝姑妈一双眼睛盯着那些达布隆金币，出项进项查得一丝不苟。她不肯解开钱袋帮帮可怜的老菲利斯，我看简直岂有此理。那姑娘，」弗雷迪说，「我一向喜欢。岂有此理！她凭什么就不能嫁那个杰克逊？我是说，爱情就是爱情嘛。」弗雷迪说，在这一点上他立场坚定。

===Original===
Mr. Keeble was making curious gulping noises.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生发出一阵古怪的咕咚声。

===Original===
“Perhaps I ought to explain,” said Freddie, “that I was having a quiet after-breakfast smoke outside the window there and heard the whole thing. I mean, you and Aunt Constance going to the mat about poor old Phyllis and you trying to bite the guv’nor’s ear and so forth.”

===Chinese===
「也许我该交代一句，」弗雷迪说，「早饭后我正在外头窗户那儿安安静静抽袋烟，整件事都听了个满耳。我是说，您跟康斯坦丝姑妈为可怜的老菲利斯摔跤角力，还有您想咬老爸的耳朵化缘等等。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble bubbled for awhile.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生咕嘟咕嘟冒了一阵泡。

===Original===
“You﻿—you listened!” he managed to ejaculate at length.

===Chinese===
「你——你偷听！」他好歹憋出这么一句。

===Original===
“And dashed lucky for you,” said Freddie with a cordiality unimpaired by the frankly unfriendly stare under which a nicer-minded youth would have withered; “dashed lucky for you that I did. Because I’ve got a scheme.”

===Chinese===
「而且这可救了您，」弗雷迪说，一腔热忱丝毫未被对方那道公然不善的怒目削损——换个脸皮薄的青年早给瞪蔫了——「幸亏我偷听了。因为我有个方案。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble’s estimate of his young relative’s sagacity was not a high one, and it is doubtful whether, had the latter caught him in a less despondent mood, he would have wasted time in inquiring into the details of this scheme, the mention of which had been playing in and out of Freddie’s conversation like a will-o’-the-wisp. But such was his reduced state at the moment that a reluctant gleam of hope crept into his troubled eye.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生对这位年轻亲戚的睿智，向来看得不高；若非撞上他此刻这般颓丧，他会不会浪费时间打听这方案的详情，颇成问题——那方案在弗雷迪的谈吐里穿进穿出，活像一簇鬼火。但他眼下萎顿已极，一缕不情愿的希望，竟悄悄爬上了他忧烦的眼睛。

===Original===
“A scheme? Do you mean a scheme to help me out of﻿—out of my difficulty?”

===Chinese===
「方案？你是说一个帮我脱——脱困的方案？」

===Original===
“Absolutely! You want the best seats, we have ’em. I mean,” Freddie went on in interpretation of these peculiar words, “you want three thousand quid, and I can show you how to get it.”

===Chinese===
「正是！您要头等座，我们样样有。我是说，」弗雷迪继而解释这句怪话，「您要三千镑，我能给您指明来路。」

===Original===
“Then kindly do so,” said Mr. Keeble; and, having opened the door, peered cautiously out, and closed it again, he crossed the room and shut the window.

===Chinese===
「那就劳驾指明。」基布尔先生说；说罢开了门，小心地向外张了张，重新掩上，然后穿过房间，关严了窗户。

===Original===
“Makes it a bit fuggy, but perhaps you’re right,” said Freddie, eyeing these manoeuvres. “Well, it’s like this, Uncle Joe. You remember what you were saying to Aunt Constance about some bird being apt to sneak up and pinch her necklace?”

===Chinese===
「是有点儿闷，不过也许您做得对。」弗雷迪看着这一套动作说。「呃，事情是这样，乔叔。您还记得您跟康斯坦丝姑妈说的话吧——说什么没准儿有哪只鸟会摸进来，偷她的项链？」

===Original===
“I do.”

===Chinese===
「记得。」

===Original===
“Well, why not?”

===Chinese===
「呃，何乐而不为呢？」

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
「你什么意思？」

===Original===
“I mean, why don’t you?”

===Chinese===
「我的意思是，您怎么不下手？」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble regarded his nephew with unconcealed astonishment. He had been prepared for imbecility, but this exceeded his expectations.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生毫不掩饰地愕然望着侄子。他对低能是有备而来的，可这也超出了他的预期。

===Original===
“Steal my wife’s necklace!”

===Chinese===
「偷我妻子的项链！」

===Original===
“That’s it. Frightfully quick you are, getting on to an idea. Pinch Aunt Connie’s necklace. For, mark you,” continued Freddie, so far forgetting the respect due from a nephew as to tap his uncle sharply on the chest, “if a husband pinches anything from a wife, it isn’t stealing. That’s law. I found that out from a movie I saw in town.”

===Chinese===
「就是这个。您脑子转得真快，一点就透。偷康妮姑妈的项链。因为，您听好，」弗雷迪续道，一时把做侄子的体面忘了个干净，伸指在叔叔胸口笃笃地敲，「丈夫从妻子那儿摸走什么东西，不算偷。这是法律。我在城里看的一场电影里刚学的。」

===Original===
The Hon. Freddie was a great student of the movies. He could tell a super-film from a super-super-film at a glance, and what he did not know about erring wives and licentious clubmen could have been written in a subtitle.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪少爷是位电影大学问家。超级片和超超级片，他一眼便能分清；至于出轨的妻子和荒淫的俱乐部浪荡子，他不懂的那一点东西，一条字幕就写得下。

===Original===
“Are you insane?” growled Mr. Keeble.

===Chinese===
「你疯了吗？」基布尔先生低吼道。

===Original===
“It wouldn’t be hard for you to get hold of it. And once you’d got it everybody would be happy. I mean, all you’d have to do would be to draw a cheque to pay for another one for Aunt Connie﻿—which would make her perfectly chirpy, as well as putting you one up, if you follow me. Then you would have the other necklace, the pinched one, to play about with. See what I mean? You could sell it privily and by stealth, ship Phyllis her three thousand, push across my thousand, and what was left over would be a nice little private account for you to tuck away somewhere where Aunt Connie wouldn’t know anything about it. And a dashed useful thing,” said Freddie, “to have up your sleeve in case of emergencies.”

===Chinese===
「您要弄到手，一点也不难。而一到手，家家皆大欢喜。我是说，您只需开张支票，给康妮姑妈再置一条——这保准叫她心花怒放，同时也叫您赢回一分，您懂我的意思。这样一来，另一条项链，也就是摸来的那条，就归您摆布了。懂我的意思吧？您可以偷偷地、悄悄地把它出脱了，给菲利斯汇三千，再划拉给我一千，剩下的就是一笔小小的私房进账，您寻个康妮姑妈一无所知的去处一藏。再说这东西，」弗雷迪说，「掖在袖筒里应急，可是顶顶管用的。」

===Original===
“Are you﻿ ﻿… ?”

===Chinese===
「你是不是……」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble was on the point of repeating his previous remark when suddenly there came the realisation that, despite all preconceived opinions, the young man was anything but insane. The scheme, at which he had been prepared to scoff, was so brilliant, yet simple, that it seemed almost incredible that its sponsor could have worked it out for himself.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生正要把先前那句话再重复一遍，忽然醒悟：尽管成见重重，这年轻人绝非疯癫。那个他原打算嗤之以鼻的方案，如此精妙，又如此简单，其首倡者竟似不可能独自琢磨得出。

===Original===
“Not my own,” said Freddie modestly, as if in answer to the thought. “Saw much the same thing in a movie once. Only there the fellow, if I remember, wanted to do down an insurance company, and it wasn’t a necklace that he pinched but bonds. Still, the principle’s the same. Well, how do we go, Uncle Joe? How about it? Is that worth a thousand quid or not?”

===Chinese===
「不是我自个儿想出来的。」弗雷迪谦逊地说，仿佛答出了对方的心思。「有回在电影里见过差不多的。只不过那里头那位，如果我没记错，想坑的是家保险公司，他摸走的也不是项链，是债券。不过原理是一样的。呃，咱们怎么说法，乔叔？怎么样？这值不值一千镑？」

===Original===
Even though he had seen in person to the closing of the door and the window, Mr. Keeble could not refrain from a conspirator-like glance about him. They had been speaking with lowered voices, but now words came from him in an almost inaudible whisper.

===Chinese===
门窗虽说是他亲手关妥的，基布尔先生仍禁不住摆出一副阴谋家的架势，朝四下里溜了一眼。他们方才说话一直压着嗓门，此刻从他嘴里吐出的话，已低微得近乎耳语。

===Original===
“Could it really be done? Is it feasible?”

===Chinese===
「这事当真办得成吗？可行吗？」

===Original===
“Feasible? Why, dash it, what the dickens is there to stop you? You could do it in a second. And the beauty of the whole thing is that, if you were copped, nobody could say a word, because husband pinching from wife isn’t stealing. Law.”

===Chinese===
「可行？哎呀，见鬼，究竟有什么拦得住您？您一眨眼就能办妥。而这整件事的妙处在于：万一您被逮住，谁也张不开嘴，因为丈夫摸妻子的不算偷。法律。」

===Original===
The statement that in the circumstances indicated nobody could say a word seemed to Mr. Keeble so at variance with the facts that he was compelled to challenge it.

===Chinese===
「在上述情形下谁也张不开嘴」这一断言，在基布尔先生看来与事实相去太远，他不得不提出异议。

===Original===
“Your aunt would have a good deal to say,” he observed ruefully.

===Chinese===
「你姑妈要说的话可多着呢。」他悻悻然道。

===Original===
“Eh? Oh, yes, I see what you mean. Well, you would have to risk that. After all, the chances would be dead against her finding out.”

===Chinese===
「嗯？哦，是，我明白您的意思了。呃，那只好担着这点风险。毕竟，她识破的几率微乎其微。」

===Original===
“But she might.”

===Chinese===
「可她没准儿就识破了。」

===Original===
“Oh, well, if you put it like that, I suppose she might.”

===Chinese===
「呃，好吧，您要是这么讲，她没准儿是会。」

===Original===
“Freddie, my boy,” said Mr. Keeble weakly, “I daren’t do it!”

===Chinese===
「弗雷迪，我的孩子，」基布尔先生有气无力地说，「我不敢干呀！」

===Original===
The vision of his thousand pounds slipping from his grasp so wrought upon Freddie that he expressed himself in a manner far from fitting in one of his years towards an older man.

===Chinese===
眼看一千英镑要从掌握中溜走，这景象对弗雷迪刺激之深，竟使他出言失了分寸，大大不合一个小辈对尊长的体统。

===Original===
“Oh, I say, don’t be such a rabbit!”

===Chinese===
「噢喂，我说，别这么兔子胆！」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble shook his head.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No,” he repeated, “I daren’t.”

===Chinese===
「不行。」他重复道。「我不敢。」

===Original===
It might have seemed that the negotiations had reached a deadlock, but Freddie, with a thousand pounds in sight, was in far too stimulated a condition to permit so tame an ending to such a promising plot. As he stood there, chafing at his uncle’s pusillanimity, an idea was vouchsafed to him.

===Chinese===
谈判看样子是僵住了，可弗雷迪眼前分明吊着一千英镑，亢奋得厉害，决计容不得这么一桩大有奔头的阴谋，落个如此窝囊的收场。他站在那儿，为叔叔的怯懦暗自搓火，忽然天启降临，他得了个主意。

===Original===
“By Jove! I’ll tell you what!” he cried.

===Chinese===
「天哪！我有了！」他喊道。

===Original===
“Not so loud!” moaned the apprehensive Mr. Keeble. “Not so loud!”

===Chinese===
「小声点！」惊弓之鸟似的基布尔先生呻吟道。「小声点！」

===Original===
“I’ll tell you what,” repeated Freddie in a hoarse whisper. “How would it be if *I* did the pinching?”

===Chinese===
「我有了，」弗雷迪用嘶哑的耳语重复道。「要是*我*来下手摸，您看如何？」

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
“How would it﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「要是怎……」

===Original===
“Would you?” Hope, which had vanished from Mr. Keeble’s face, came flooding back. “My boy, would you really?”

===Chinese===
「你肯吗？」方才从基布尔先生脸上消散的希望，此刻滚滚回涌。「我的孩子，你当真肯？」

===Original===
“For a thousand quid you bet I would.”

===Chinese===
「为一千镑？您放一百个心，我肯定了。」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble clutched at his young relative’s hand and gripped it feverishly.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生一把攥住年轻亲戚的手，激动得直发抖。

===Original===
“Freddie,” he said, “the moment you place that necklace in my hands, I will give you not a thousand but two thousand pounds.”

===Chinese===
「弗雷迪，」他说，「那条项链一到我手里，我给你的就不是一千，而是两千英镑。」

===Original===
“Uncle Joe,” said Freddie with equal intensity, “it’s a bet!”

===Chinese===
「乔叔，」弗雷迪以同样的热度答道，「一言为定！」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble mopped at his forehead.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生抹了抹额头。

===Original===
“You think you can manage it?”

===Chinese===
「你觉得这事你办得下来？」

===Original===
“Manage it?” Freddie laughed a light laugh. “Just watch me!”

===Chinese===
「办得下来？」弗雷迪轻轻一笑。「您瞧好吧！」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble grasped his hand again with the utmost warmth.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生再度以最大的热忱握了握他的手。

===Original===
“I must go out and get some air,” he said. “I’m all upset. May I really leave this matter to you, Freddie?”

===Chinese===
「我得出去透透气。」他说。「我整个人都翻腾了。这事当真可以全托给你吗，弗雷迪？」

===Original===
“Rather!”

===Chinese===
「那还用说！」

===Original===
“Good! Then tonight I will write to Phyllis and say that I may be able to do what she wishes.”

===Chinese===
「好！那今晚我就给菲利斯写信，说我兴许能办成她心愿的事。」

===Original===
“Don’t say ‘may,’ ” cried Freddie buoyantly. “The word is ‘will.’ Bally will! What ho!”

===Chinese===
「别说『兴许』！」弗雷迪喜洋洋地嚷道。「字眼儿得是『一定』。板上钉钉的一定！好哇！」

### IV / IV

===Original===
Exhilaration is a heady drug; but, like other drugs, it has the disadvantage that its stimulating effects seldom last for very long. For perhaps ten minutes after his uncle had left him, Freddie Threepwood lay back in his chair in a sort of ecstasy. He felt strong, vigorous, alert. Then by degrees, like a chilling wind, doubt began to creep upon him﻿—faintly at first, then more and more insistently, till by the end of a quarter of an hour he was in a state of pronounced self-mistrust. Or, to put it with less elegance, he was suffering from an exceedingly severe attack of cold feet.

===Chinese===
亢奋是一剂上头的猛药；然而它同别的药品一样，有个短处：振奋之效难得持久。叔叔走后约莫十分钟，弗雷迪·思里普伍德仰在椅子里，沉浸在一种飘飘然的狂喜中。他觉得自己强壮、矫健、机警。随后，渐渐地，像一阵冷风，疑虑开始爬上他的心头——起初若有若无，继而越缠越紧，到一刻钟将尽，他已陷入显著的自我不信任状态。或者，说得不够文雅些：他正害着一阵极其严重的寒脚病——临阵发怵是也。

===Original===
The more he contemplated the venture which he had undertaken, the less alluring did it appear to him. His was not a keen imagination, but even he could shape with a gruesome clearness a vision of the frightful bust-up that would ensue should he be detected stealing his Aunt Constance’s diamond necklace. Common decency would in such an event seal his lips as regarded his Uncle Joseph’s share in the matter. And even if﻿—as might conceivably happen﻿—common decency failed at the crisis, reason told him that his Uncle Joseph would infallibly disclaim any knowledge of or connection with the rash act. And then where would he be? In the soup, undoubtedly. For Freddie could not conceal it from himself that there was nothing in his previous record to make it seem inconceivable to his nearest and dearest that he should steal the jewellery of a female relative for purely personal ends. The verdict in the event of detection would be one of uncompromising condemnation.

===Chinese===
他越琢磨自己揽下的这桩营生，它在他眼里就越不迷人。他的想象力原不算敏锐，可即便是他，也能以一种毛骨悚然的清晰，勾勒出那幅图景：万一他偷康斯坦丝姑妈的钻石项链时被当场拿获，接踵而至的将是何等骇人的大崩盘。果真到了那步田地，为人起码的体面会封住他的嘴，不许他供出约瑟夫叔叔在此事中的干系。而纵使——这并非不可想象——起码的体面在紧要关头垮了台，理智也告诉他：他的约瑟夫叔叔必定矢口否认对这桩莽撞行迹有任何知情或瓜葛。那他还能有什么下场？掉进汤锅里，毫无疑问。因为弗雷迪瞒不过自己：他以往的履历里，实在没有任何东西，能让他的至亲们觉得「他为一己私利去偷一位女亲戚的珠宝」有什么不可想象。一旦事败被识破，判决将是不留余地的全盘谴责。

===Original===
And yet he hated the idea of meekly allowing that two thousand pounds to escape from his clutch﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
然而，一想到要乖乖地任那两千英镑从掌心里逃走，他又一百个不情愿……

===Original===
A young man’s crossroads.

===Chinese===
一个年轻人的十字路口。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The agony of spirit into which these meditations cast him had brought him up with a bound from the comfortable depths of his armchair and had set him prowling restlessly about the room. His wanderings led him at this point to collide somewhat painfully with the long table on which Beach the butler, a tidy soul, was in the habit of arranging in a neat row the daily papers, weekly papers, and magazines which found their way into the castle. The shock had the effect of rousing him from his stupor, and in an absent way he clutched the nearest daily paper, which happened to be the *Morning Globe*, and returned to his chair in the hope of quieting his nerves with a perusal of the racing intelligence. For, though far removed now from any practical share in the doings of the racing world, he still took a faint melancholy interest in ascertaining what Captain Curb, the Head Lad, Little Brighteyes, and the rest of the newspaper experts fancied for the day’s big event. He lit a cigarette and unfolded the journal.

===Chinese===
这番沉思将他抛入的精神苦刑，猛地把他从扶手椅舒坦的深处弹了起来，撵得他在屋里焦躁地逡巡。踱到这个当口，他相当结实地撞上了那张长桌——管家比奇是个爱齐整的人，惯于把流进城堡的日报、周报和杂志在桌上排成齐整的一列。这一撞把他从恍惚中撞醒了，他心不在焉地抓起手边最近的一份日报，恰是《环球晨报》（*Morning Globe*），回到椅子上，指望读一读赛马情报，让神经平复下来。因为，尽管如今早已同赛马界的实务绝缘，他仍怀着一点淡淡的哀愁兴致，想弄清科布上尉、「首席马童」、「小亮眼」以及其余报端行家们看好当日大赛的哪一匹。他点上一支烟，展开了报纸。

===Original===
The next moment, instead of passing directly, as was his usual practice, to the last page, which was devoted to sport, he was gazing with a strange dry feeling in his throat at a certain advertisement on page one.

===Chinese===
下一瞬，他没有照老习惯径直翻到专载体育的末版，而是喉头带着一种发紧发干的怪异感觉，死死盯住了头版上的某则广告。

===Original===
It was a well-displayed advertisement, and one that had caught the eye of many other readers of the paper that morning. It was worded to attract attention, and it had achieved its object. But where others who read it had merely smiled and marvelled idly how anybody could spend good money putting nonsense like this in the paper, to Freddie its import was wholly serious. It read to him like the Real Thing. His motion-picture-trained mind accepted this advertisement at its face-value.

===Chinese===
那是则编排醒目的广告，那天早晨已勾住了报纸许多别的读者的眼光。它的措辞生来就为惹眼，而它也着实达到了目的。只是旁的读者读罢，无非付之一笑，闲闲地诧异怎么会有人肯花好钱，把这种胡话登到报上；而对弗雷迪，它的要旨全然严肃。在他读来，这俨然是真家伙。他那受过电影训练的头脑，对这则广告照单全收，深信不疑。

===Original===
It ran as follows:﻿—

===Chinese===
广告词如下：——

===Original===
> ***Leave it to Psmith!***
> Psmith Will Help You
> Psmith Is Ready For Anything
> **Do you want**
> Someone To Manage Your Affairs?
> Someone To Handle Your Business?
> Someone To Take The Dog For A Run?
> Someone To Assassinate Your Aunt?
> **Psmith will do it**
> **Crime not objected to**
> Whatever Job You Have To Offer
> (Provided It Has Nothing To Do With Fish)
> ***Leave it to Psmith!***
> Address Applications To “R. Psmith, Box 365”
> ***Leave it to Psmith!***

===Chinese===
> ***把这事交给皮史密斯吧！***
> 皮史密斯会帮您
> 皮史密斯什么都肯干
> **您是否需要**
> 有人替您打理事务？
> 有人替您操持生意？
> 有人替您遛狗散心？
> 有人替您暗杀姑妈？
> **皮史密斯都照办**
> **犯罪亦不推辞**
> 无论您手头有什么差事
> （只要与鱼无关）
> ***把这事交给皮史密斯吧！***
> 申请函请寄「R. 皮史密斯（R. Psmith），365 号信箱」
> ***把这事交给皮史密斯吧！***

===Original===
Freddie laid the paper down with a deep intake of breath. He picked it up again, and read the advertisement a second time. Yes, it sounded good.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪深深吸了一口气，把报纸放下。又拿起来，把广告读了第二遍。没错，听着靠谱。

===Original===
More, it had something of the quality of a direct answer to prayer. Very vividly now Freddie realised that what he had been wishing for was a partner to share the perils of this enterprise which he had so rashly undertaken. In fact, not so much to share them as to take them off his shoulders altogether. And such a partner he was now in a position to command. Uncle Joe was going to give him two thousand if he brought the thing off. This advertisement fellow would probably be charmed to come in for a few hundred﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
不止如此，它还颇有几分「有求必应的祈祷直获回音」的品质。弗雷迪此刻无比鲜明地意识到，他一直祈望的，原是位合伙人，来分担这桩被他如此冒失揽下的营生里的风险。说实在的，与其说是分担，不如说是干脆从他肩上整个儿卸下去。而这样一位合伙人，他如今是雇请得起的。事情办成，乔叔打算给他两千。这位登广告的老兄，多半肯为区区几百镑欣然入伙……

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Two minutes later, Freddie was at the writing-desk, scribbling a letter. From time to time he glanced furtively over his shoulder at the door. But the house was still. No footsteps came to interrupt him at his task.

===Chinese===
两分钟后，弗雷迪已坐在写字台前，挥笔疾书一封信。他不时贼头贼脑地回头瞟一眼门口。但宅子里静悄悄的，没有脚步声前来搅扰他的功课。

### V / V

===Original===
Freddie went out into the garden. He had not wandered far when from somewhere close at hand there was borne to him on the breeze a remark in a high voice about Scottish obstinacy, which could only have proceeded from one source. He quickened his steps.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪出了屋，走进花园。他还没逛出多远，近处某处便随风送来一句尖嗓门的、议论苏格兰式顽固的话，这话只可能出自一个源头。他加快了脚步。

===Original===
“Hallo, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
「哈罗，老爸。」

===Original===
“Well, Frederick?”

===Chinese===
「嗯，弗雷德里克？」

===Original===
Freddie shuffled.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪蹭了蹭脚。

===Original===
“I say, guv’nor, do you think I might go up to town with you this afternoon?”

===Chinese===
「我说，老爸，您看今天下午我能不能跟您一块儿上伦敦去？」

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
“Fact is, I ought to see my dentist. Haven’t been to him for a deuce of a time.”

===Chinese===
「实情是，我该去瞧我的牙医了。要命的老久没去瞧过了。」

===Original===
“I cannot see the necessity for you to visit a London dentist. There is an excellent man in Shrewsbury, and you know I have the strongest objection to your going to London.”

===Chinese===
「我看不出你有何必要去寻伦敦的牙医。什鲁斯伯里（Shrewsbury）就有一位顶好的人选，而且你知道，我对你上伦敦这件事，是百般反对的。」

===Original===
“Well, you see, this fellow understands my snappers. Always been to him, I mean to say. Anybody who knows anything about these things will tell you greatest mistake go buzzing about to different dentists.”

===Chinese===
「呃，您瞧，这位老兄摸得清我这口牙的脾气。向来都是找他，我是说。这种事但凡懂点儿行的人都会告诉您，满城乱换牙医是天字第一号的错误。」

===Original===
Already Lord Emsworth’s attention was wandering back to the waiting McAllister.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵的注意力，已经又飘回那位等候着的麦卡利斯特身上去了。

===Original===
“Oh, very well, very well.”

===Chinese===
「哦，也好，也好。」

===Original===
“Thanks awfully, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
「多谢了，老爸。」

===Original===
“But on one thing I insist, Frederick. I cannot have you loafing about London the whole day. You must catch the twelve-fifty train back.”

===Chinese===
「但有一条我必须说死，弗雷德里克。我不能容你在伦敦闲荡一整天。你必须搭十二点五十的火车回来。」

===Original===
“Right ho. That’ll be all right, guv’nor.”

===Chinese===
「好嘞。包在我身上，老爸。」

===Original===
“Now, listen to reason, McAllister,” said his lordship. “That is all I ask you to do﻿—listen to reason﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「好了，麦卡利斯特，你要讲道理。」勋爵大人说。「我求你的就这一桩——讲道理……」

## II Enter Psmith / 皮史密斯登场

### I / I

===Original===
At about the hour when Lord Emsworth’s train, whirling him and his son Freddie to London, had reached the halfway point in its journey, a very tall, very thin, very solemn young man, gleaming in a speckless top hat and a morning-coat of irreproachable fit, mounted the steps of Number Eighteen, Wallingford Street, West Kensington, and rang the front-door bell. This done, he removed the hat; and having touched his forehead lightly with a silk handkerchief, for the afternoon sun was warm, gazed about him with a grave distaste.

===Chinese===
大约就在埃姆斯沃思勋爵（Lord Emsworth）载着他和儿子弗雷迪（Freddie）风驰电掣驶往伦敦的火车行至旅程中点的那个钟点，一位个子极高、身形极瘦、神色极庄重的年轻人，头顶一尘不染的大礼帽，身穿剪裁无可挑剔的晨礼服，通身光彩照人，登上了西肯辛顿（West Kensington）沃灵福德街（Wallingford Street）十八号的台阶，按响了前门门铃。按罢，他摘下帽子，因午后阳光正暖，用一方丝质手帕在额上轻轻按了按，随即带着一种郑重其事的不以为然环顾四周。

===Original===
“A scaly neighbourhood!” he murmured.

===Chinese===
「好一个寒碜的地段！」他低声自语。

===Original===
The young man’s judgment was one at which few people with an eye for beauty would have cavilled. When the great revolution against London’s ugliness really starts and yelling hordes of artists and architects, maddened beyond endurance, finally take the law into their own hands and rage through the city burning and destroying, Wallingford Street, West Kensington, will surely not escape the torch. Long since it must have been marked down for destruction. For, though it possesses certain merits of a low practical kind, being inexpensive in the matter of rents and handy for the buses and the Underground, it is a peculiarly beastly little street. Situated in the middle of one of those districts where London breaks out into a sort of eczema of red brick, it consists of two parallel rows of semidetached villas, all exactly alike, each guarded by a ragged evergreen hedge, each with coloured glass of an extremely regrettable nature let into the panels of the front door; and sensitive young impressionists from the artists’ colony up Holland Park way may sometimes be seen stumbling through it with hands over their eyes, muttering between clenched teeth “How long? How long?”

===Chinese===
这位年轻人的判词，凡稍具审美眼光者，怕都无从非议。待到那场针对伦敦丑陋的大革命当真爆发，嚎叫的艺术家与建筑师大军忍无可忍、终于私设公堂，怒吼着烧遍全城、一路捣毁之际，西肯辛顿的沃灵福德街断然逃不过那把火。想必它早被圈进了待毁名单。因为，这条街虽说也具备几样低级而实用的长处——租金便宜，搭公共汽车、乘地铁都方便——却是一条出奇讨厌的小街。它坐落于那样一种街区的正中：伦敦城在那儿发作起一种红砖湿疹；街身由两排平行的半独立小别墅组成，幢幢一模一样，各家门前守着一道蓬乱参差的常青树篱，各家前门的门板上都嵌着性质极端令人遗憾的彩色玻璃；而荷兰公园（Holland Park）一带艺术家聚居区里那些敏感的年轻印象派，有时会被人瞧见双手掩目、跌跌撞撞地穿行其间，从咬紧的牙关里挤出一句「何时方休？何时方休？」

===Original===
A small maid-of-all-work appeared in answer to the bell, and stood transfixed as the visitor, producing a monocle, placed it in his right eye and inspected her through it.

===Chinese===
一个打杂的小女仆应铃而来，一见来客掏出单片眼镜嵌入右眼、隔着镜片打量她，顿时呆立当场。

===Original===
“A warm afternoon,” he said cordially.

===Chinese===
「午后真暖和。」他和蔼可亲地说。

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
「是，先生。」

===Original===
“But pleasant,” urged the young man. “Tell me, is Mrs. Jackson at home?”

===Chinese===
「不过也宜人。」年轻人坚持道。「请问，杰克逊太太在家吗？」

===Original===
“No, sir.”

===Chinese===
「不在，先生。」

===Original===
“Not at home?”

===Chinese===
「不在家？」

===Original===
“No, sir.”

===Chinese===
「不在，先生。」

===Original===
The young man sighed.

===Chinese===
年轻人叹了口气。

===Original===
“Ah well,” he said, “we must always remember that these disappointments are sent to us for some good purpose. No doubt they make us more spiritual. Will you inform her that I called? The name is Psmith. P-smith.”

===Chinese===
「唉，也罢，」他说，「我们必须时刻铭记：这些失望降临到我们头上，总有其为善的用意。毫无疑问，它们能让我们更显灵性。劳驾转告她我来过，好吗？敝姓皮史密斯（Psmith）。P-smith。」

===Original===
“Peasmith, sir?”

===Chinese===
「豌豆史密斯（Peasmith），先生？」

===Original===
“No, no. P-s-m-i-t-h. I should explain to you that I started life without the initial letter, and my father always clung ruggedly to the plain Smith. But it seemed to me that there were so many Smiths in the world that a little variety might well be introduced. Smythe I look on as a cowardly evasion, nor do I approve of the too prevalent custom of tacking another name on in front by means of a hyphen. So I decided to adopt the Psmith. The p, I should add for your guidance, is silent, as in phthisis, psychic, and ptarmigan. You follow me?”

===Chinese===
「不，不。P-s-m-i-t-h。容我向您解释：我降生人世时，姓名本没有这个起首字母，家父也一直坚贞不屈地守着素朴的史密斯（Smith）本姓。但在我看来，世上史密斯实在太多，未尝不可以引入一点变化。斯迈思（Smythe）那种写法，我视为懦夫的遁词；把另一个姓氏用连字符缀在前头的风气过于盛行，我也不以为然。于是我决定采用皮史密斯。顺带提请留意：打头的 p 不发音，一如 phthisis（肺痨）、psychic（通灵）与 ptarmigan（雷鸟）中的 p。您听明白了吗？」

===Original===
“Y-yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
「明——明白了，先生。」

===Original===
“You don’t think,” he said anxiously, “that I did wrong in pursuing this course?”

===Chinese===
「您不觉得，」他焦急地说，「我走这一步走错了吗？」

===Original===
“N-no, sir.”

===Chinese===
「没——没有，先生。」

===Original===
“Splendid!” said the young man, flicking a speck of dust from his coat-sleeve. “Splendid! Splendid!”

===Chinese===
「好极了！」年轻人说着，从衣袖上弹去一星微尘。「好极了！好极了！」

===Original===
And with a courteous bow he descended the steps and made his way down the street. The little maid, having followed him with bulging eyes till he was out of sight, closed the door and returned to her kitchen.

===Chinese===
随即他谦恭一躬，走下台阶，沿街而去。小女仆瞪圆了眼睛目送他，直到人影不见，方才关门，回厨房去。

===Original===
Psmith strolled meditatively on. The genial warmth of the afternoon soothed him. He hummed lightly﻿—only stopping when, as he reached the end of the street, a young man of his own age, rounding the corner rapidly, almost ran into him.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯一路信步而行，若有所思。午后融融的暖意抚慰着他。他轻轻哼起小曲——刚哼到街尾，一个与他同龄的年轻人飞快地拐过街角，险些撞上他，他这才收声。

===Original===
“Sorry,” said the young man. “Hallo, Smith.”

===Chinese===
「对不住。」那年轻人说。「哈啰，史密斯。」

===Original===
Psmith gazed upon him with benevolent affection.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯以慈爱的目光注视着他。

===Original===
“Comrade Jackson,” he said, “this is well met. The one man of all others whom I would have wished to encounter. We will pop off somewhere, Comrade Jackson, should your engagements permit, and restore our tissues with a cup of tea. I had hoped to touch the Jackson family for some slight refreshment, but I was informed that your wife was out.”

===Chinese===
「杰克逊同志（Comrade Jackson），」他说，「相逢得再好不过。普天之下，我惟愿遇见的正是足下。杰克逊同志，倘若您的日程允许，咱们这就寻个去处，用一杯茶滋补滋补各自的机体组织。我本指望到杰克逊府上讨些小点心，却被告知尊夫人外出了。」

===Original===
Mike Jackson laughed.

===Chinese===
迈克·杰克逊（Mike Jackson）笑了。

===Original===
“Phyllis isn’t out. She﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「菲利斯没出去。她……」

===Original===
“Not out? Then,” said Psmith, pained, “there has been dirty work done this day. For I was turned from the door. It would not be exaggerating to say that I was given the bird. Is this the boasted Jackson hospitality?”

===Chinese===
「没出去？那么，」皮史密斯痛心地说，「今日便有人行了卑鄙的勾当。因为我竟被从门口打发走了。毫不夸张地说，我是吃了闭门羹还外带被喝了倒彩。这就是杰克逊家素来夸耀的好客之道吗？」

===Original===
“Phyllis is giving a tea to some of her old school pals,” explained Mike. “She told the maid to say she wasn’t at home to anybody else. I’m not allowed in myself.”

===Chinese===
「菲利斯（Phyllis）正请几位老同学喝茶，」迈克解释道，「她吩咐女仆，旁人一概回说不在家。连我自己都不许进。」

===Original===
“Enough, Comrade Jackson!” said Psmith agreeably. “Say no more. If you yourself have been booted out in spite of all the loving, honouring, and obeying your wife promised at the altar, who am I to complain? And possibly, one can console oneself by reflecting, we are well out of it. These gatherings of old girls’-school chums are not the sort of function your man of affairs wants to get lugged into. Capital company as we are, Comrade Jackson, we should doubtless have been extremely in the way. I suppose the conversation would have dealt exclusively with reminiscences of the dear old school, of tales of surreptitious cocoa-drinking in the dormitories and what the deportment mistress said when Angela was found chewing tobacco in the shrubbery. Yes, I fancy we have not missed a lot.﻿ ﻿… By the way, I don’t think much of the new home. True, I only saw it from the outside, but﻿ ﻿… no, I don’t think much of it.”

===Chinese===
「够了，杰克逊同志！」皮史密斯欣然说道。「不必再讲。倘若连你本人——不顾尊夫人在圣坛前许下的爱、敬与服从——都被一脚踢了出来，我又有什么资格抱怨？再说，细想之下，或许还能聊以自慰：咱们躲开倒好。这种老女校同窗的聚会，本就不是大忙人乐意被拽进去凑数的那类场合。咱们虽是上乘的宾客，杰克逊同志，去了无疑也会碍事碍得厉害。我料想席间的话题，无非缅怀亲爱的母校，讲宿舍里如何偷冲可可，讲安杰拉在灌木丛里嚼烟草被逮个正着时，仪态课女教师说了些什么。是啊，我看咱们没错过多少东西。……对了，这处新居我可不怎么看得上。诚然，我只看了个外观，可是……不，我评价不高。」

===Original===
“Best we can afford.”

===Chinese===
「这已是咱们力所能及的最好房子了。」

===Original===
“And who,” said Psmith, “am I to taunt my boyhood friend with his honest poverty? Especially as I myself am standing on the very brink of destitution.”

===Chinese===
「而我，」皮史密斯说，「又岂能拿清白无辜的穷困，去数落我少年时代的朋友？何况我本人此刻正站在赤贫的深渊边缘。」

===Original===
“You?”

===Chinese===
「你？」

===Original===
“I in person. That low moaning sound you hear is the wolf bivouacked outside my door.”

===Chinese===
「正是在下。你听见的那阵低沉呜咽，是头正在我家门外扎营的狼。」

===Original===
“But I thought your uncle gave you rather a good salary.”

===Chinese===
「可我还以为，你叔父给你的薪水相当优厚呢。」

===Original===
“So he did. But my uncle and I are about to part company. From now on he, so to speak, will take the high road and I’ll take the low road. I dine with him tonight, and over the nuts and wine I shall hand him the bad news that I propose to resign my position in the firm.
