I have no doubt that he supposed he was doing me a good turn by starting me in his fish business, but even what little experience I have had of it has convinced me that it is not my proper sphere. The whisper flies round the clubs ‘Psmith has not found his niche!’

===Chinese===
「优厚是优厚。但家叔与我不久就要分道扬镳了。从今往后，他，不妨这么说，将取高路而行，我将取低路而走。今晚我与他共膳，待端上果仁与醇酒，我便向他奉上那则噩耗：本人拟辞去商号中的职位。我毫不怀疑，当年把我安插进他的鱼行生意，他自认为帮了我一个大忙；但哪怕我仅有的那一点阅历，也已令我确信，此非我的用武之地。耳语正飞传于各家俱乐部：『皮史密斯尚未觅得归宿！』」

===Original===
“I am not,” said Psmith, “an unreasonable man. I realise that humanity must be supplied with fish. I am not averse from a bit of fish myself. But to be professionally connected with a firm that handles the material in the raw is not my idea of a large lifework. Remind me to tell you some time what it feels like to sling yourself out of bed at four a.m. and go down to toil in Billingsgate Market. No, there is money in fish﻿—my uncle has made a pot of it﻿—but what I feel is that there must be other walks in life for a bright young man. I chuck it tonight.”

===Chinese===
「我这个人，」皮史密斯说，「并非蛮不讲理。我认识到，人类总得有鱼供应。我自己也不反对来上一点鱼。但以职业之身，效力于一家经手生鱼原料的商号，实在不合我为一番宏大人生事业所勾画的图景。改天提醒我给你讲讲：清晨四点把自己从床上甩起来、赶去比林斯门市场（Billingsgate Market）卖力苦干，是何等滋味。不，鱼里头确乎有钱可赚——家叔就捞了一罐——只是我总觉得，一个聪明伶俐的年轻人，人生在世总还有别的路可走。今晚我就洗手不干了。」

===Original===
“What are you going to do, then?”

===Chinese===
「那你打算做什么呢？」

===Original===
“That, Comrade Jackson, is more or less on the knees of the gods. Tomorrow morning I think I will stroll round to an employment agency and see how the market for bright young men stands. Do you know a good one?”

===Chinese===
「那个嘛，杰克逊同志，就多半要坐上诸神的膝头听凭发落了。明天上午，我想不妨踱到哪家职业介绍所去转转，看看聪明年轻人的行市如何。你知道哪家可靠吗？」

===Original===
“Phyllis always goes to Miss Clarkson’s in Shaftesbury Avenue. But﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「菲利斯一向去沙夫茨伯里大道（Shaftesbury Avenue）的克拉克森小姐（Miss Clarkson）那里。不过……」

===Original===
“Miss Clarkson’s in Shaftesbury Avenue. I will make a note of it﻿ ﻿… Meanwhile, I wonder if you saw the *Morning Globe* today?”

===Chinese===
「沙夫茨伯里大道的克拉克森小姐处。谨记下了……话说回来，不知你今天看过《环球晨报》（Morning Globe）没有？」

===Original===
“No. Why?”

===Chinese===
「没有。怎么？」

===Original===
“I had an advertisement in it, in which I expressed myself as willing﻿—indeed, eager﻿—to tackle any undertaking that had nothing to do with fish. I am confidently expecting shoals of replies. I look forward to winnowing the heap and selecting the most desirable.”

===Chinese===
「我在上面登了一则广告，申明本人甘愿——非但甘愿，而且热切——承接任何与鱼无涉的事业。我笃定地期待着回信如鱼群般大批涌来。我盼着届时扬场筛拣，从那一堆里挑出最称心的。」

===Original===
“Pretty hard to get a job these days,” said Mike doubtfully.

===Chinese===
「如今找份差事可不容易。」迈克将信将疑地说。

===Original===
“Not if you have something superlatively good to offer.”

===Chinese===
「若你拿得出超群绝伦的货色，就不难。」

===Original===
“What have you got to offer?”

===Chinese===
「你拿得出什么货色？」

===Original===
“My services,” said Psmith with faint reproach.

===Chinese===
「在下的效劳。」皮史密斯说，语带淡淡的责备。

===Original===
“What as?”

===Chinese===
「以什么身份效劳？」

===Original===
“As anything. I made no restrictions. Would you care to take a look at my manifesto? I have a copy in my pocket.”

===Chinese===
「什么身份都行。我未设任何限制。愿不愿赏光看看我的宣言？我口袋里正有一份。」

===Original===
Psmith produced from inside his immaculate waistcoat a folded clipping.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯从一尘不染的马甲内袋里，掏出一张折起的剪报。

===Original===
“I should welcome your opinion of it, Comrade Jackson. I have frequently said that for sturdy common sense you stand alone. Your judgment should be invaluable.”

===Chinese===
「杰克逊同志，我恭候你的高见。我常说，论朴实的常识，你举世无双。你的品鉴必定价值连城。」

===Original===
The advertisement, which some hours earlier had so electrified the Hon. Freddie Threepwood in the smoking-room at Blandings Castle, seemed to affect Mike, whose mind was of the stolid and serious type, somewhat differently. He finished his perusal and stared speechlessly.

===Chinese===
数小时前，这则广告曾在布兰丁斯城堡（Blandings Castle）的吸烟室里，令弗雷迪·思里普伍德（the Hon. Freddie Threepwood）如遭电击；此刻它对迈克——他的头脑属于沉稳持重一路——的效力，却似乎颇有不同。他读罢，哑然瞪视。

===Original===
“Neat, don’t you think?” said Psmith. “Covers the ground adequately? I think so, I think so.”

===Chinese===
「干净利落，是不是？」皮史密斯说。「覆盖面够周全吧？我看够，我看够。」

===Original===
“Do you mean to say you’re going to put drivel like that in the paper?” asked Mike.

===Chinese===
「你该不会是说，你要把这种呓语登到报上去吧？」迈克问。

===Original===
“I *have* put it in the paper. As I told you, it appeared this morning. By this time tomorrow I shall no doubt have finished sorting out the first batch of replies.”

===Chinese===
「我不是打算登，是已经登了。跟你说过，今早已经见报。到明天这时候，第一批回信想必已经拣完了。」

===Original===
Mike’s emotion took him back to the phraseology of school days.

===Chinese===
迈克的情绪把他带回了学生时代的用语。

===Original===
“You *are* an ass!”

===Chinese===
「你可真是一头蠢驴！」

===Original===
Psmith restored the clipping to his waistcoat pocket.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯把剪报收回马甲口袋。

===Original===
“You wound me, Comrade Jackson,” he said. “I had expected a broader outlook from you. In fact, I rather supposed that you would have rushed round instantly to the offices of the journal and shoved in a similar advertisement yourself. But nothing that you can say can damp my buoyant spirit. The cry goes round Kensington (and district) ‘Psmith is off!’ In what direction the cry omits to state: but that information the future will supply. And now, Comrade Jackson, let us trickle into yonder teashop and drink success to the venture in a cup of the steaming. I had a particularly hard morning today among the whitebait, and I need refreshment.”

===Chinese===
「你伤了我的心，杰克逊同志。」他说。「我原指望你的眼界能更开阔些。说实在的，我颇以为你会立刻飞奔到报社，自己也塞去一则同样的广告。但任你说什么，也浇不灭我蓬蓬勃勃的兴致。呼声正传遍肯辛顿（及其周边）：『皮史密斯动身啦！』至于朝哪个方向动身，呼声未及说明：不过这项情报，将来自会补上。好了，杰克逊同志，让咱们涓涓流入那边那家茶馆，以一杯滚烫热茶，祝这项大业马到成功。今天上午我在银鱼堆里干得格外辛苦，亟需滋补。」

### II / II

===Original===
After Psmith had withdrawn his spectacular person from it, there was an interval of perhaps twenty minutes before anything else occurred to brighten the drabness of Wallingford Street. The lethargy of afternoon held the thoroughfare in its grip. Occasionally a tradesman’s cart would rattle round the corner, and from time to time cats appeared, stalking purposefully among the evergreens. But at ten minutes to five a girl ran up the steps of Number Eighteen and rang the bell.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯那引人瞩目的身形撤离之后，约有二十分钟，再没有别的事情发生，来为沃灵福德街的灰暗增光。午后的倦怠牢牢攫住了整条街。偶尔有送货商车的轱辘声转过街角，猫儿也不时现身，目标坚定地潜行于常青树篱之间。但到五点差十分，一位姑娘跑上十八号的台阶，按响了门铃。

===Original===
She was a girl of medium height, very straight and slim; and her fair hair, her cheerful smile, and the boyish suppleness of her body all contributed to a general effect of valiant gaiety, a sort of golden sunniness﻿—accentuated by the fact that, like all girls who looked to Paris for inspiration in their dress that season, she was wearing black.

===Chinese===
她中等身材，身姿笔挺纤瘦；金色的头发、爽朗的笑容，加上男孩子似的柔韧体态，共同酿成一种英气焕发、欢天喜地的总体印象，一种金灿灿的明媚——而如下一桩事实，又为这份明媚更添光彩：正如那一季所有向巴黎寻求服饰灵感的姑娘一样，她一身黑衣。

===Original===
The small maid appeared again.

===Chinese===
小女仆又出现了。

===Original===
“Is Mrs. Jackson at home?” said the girl. “I think she’s expecting me. Miss Halliday.”

===Chinese===
「杰克逊太太在家吗？」姑娘说。「我想她在等我。哈利迪小姐（Miss Halliday）。」

===Original===
“Yes, miss?”

===Chinese===
「是，小姐？」

===Original===
A door at the end of the narrow hall had opened.

===Chinese===
狭窄门厅尽头的一扇门开了。

===Original===
“Is that you, Eve?”

===Chinese===
「是你吗，伊芙（Eve）？」

===Original===
“Hallo, Phyl, darling.”

===Chinese===
「哈啰，菲尔，亲爱的。」

===Original===
Phyllis Jackson fluttered down the passage like a rose-leaf on the wind, and hurled herself into Eve’s arms. She was small and fragile, with great brown eyes under a cloud of dark hair. She had a wistful look, and most people who knew her wanted to pet her. Eve had always petted her, from their first days at school together.

===Chinese===
菲利斯·杰克逊像风中的一瓣玫瑰叶，翩然飘过走廊，一头扎进伊芙怀里。她娇小纤弱，一头乌云般的深色头发下，是一双棕色的大眼睛。她生着一副楚楚动人的模样，认识她的人大都想要疼她。伊芙向来疼她，从两人同校的第一天起就是。

===Original===
“Am I late or early?” asked Eve.

===Chinese===
「我来晚了还是早了？」伊芙问。

===Original===
“You’re the first, but we won’t wait. Jane, will you bring tea into the drawing-room.”

===Chinese===
「你是头一个，不过咱们不等旁人了。简（Jane），把茶送到客厅来。」

===Original===
“Yes’m.”

===Chinese===
「是，太太。」

===Original===
“And, remember, I don’t want to see anyone for the rest of the afternoon. If anybody calls, tell them I’m not at home. Except Miss Clarkson and Mrs. McTodd, of course.”

===Chinese===
「还有，记着，今天下午余下的时间我谁也不见。有人来的话，就说我不在家。当然，克拉克森小姐和麦克托德太太（Mrs. McTodd）除外。」

===Original===
“Yes’m.”

===Chinese===
「是，太太。」

===Original===
“Who is Mrs. McTodd?” inquired Eve. “Is that Cynthia?”

===Chinese===
「麦克托德太太是谁？」伊芙问。「是辛西娅（Cynthia）吗？」

===Original===
“Yes. Didn’t you know she had married Ralston McTodd, the Canadian poet? You knew she went out to Canada?”

===Chinese===
「是她。你不知道她嫁给了加拿大诗人拉尔斯顿·麦克托德（Ralston McTodd）吗？她去了加拿大，这个你知道吧？」

===Original===
“I knew that, yes. But I hadn’t heard that she was married. Funny how out of touch one gets with girls who were one’s best friends at school. Do you realise it’s nearly two years since I saw you?”

===Chinese===
「这我知道。可她结了婚的事，我倒没听说。真怪，从前在学校里最要好的姑娘，一别竟会这样音讯杳然。你意识到没有，我差不多有两年没见你了？」

===Original===
“I know. Isn’t it awful! I got your address from Elsa Wentworth two or three days ago, and then Clarkie told me that Cynthia was over here on a visit with her husband, so I thought how jolly it would be to have a regular reunion. We three were such friends in the old days.﻿ ﻿… You remember Clarkie, of course? Miss Clarkson, who used to be English mistress at Wayland House.”

===Chinese===
「可不是，多糟糕！两三天前我从埃尔莎·温特沃斯（Elsa Wentworth）那里要到你的地址，后来小克拉克（Clarkie）又告诉我，辛西娅陪丈夫回英国来了，我就想，咱们好好聚一聚该多美。从前咱们仨是多好的朋友呀。……你当然记得小克拉克？克拉克森小姐，从前在韦兰德女子学校（Wayland House）教英文的那位。」

===Original===
“Yes, of course. Where did you run into her?”

===Chinese===
「当然记得。你在哪儿碰上她的？」

===Original===
“Oh, I see a lot of her. She runs a Domestic Employment Agency in Shaftesbury Avenue now, and I have to go there about once a fortnight to get a new maid. She supplied Jane.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我常见她。她如今在沙夫茨伯里大道开了一家家政职业介绍所，我差不多每隔半个月就得跑一趟，另雇个新女仆。简就是她荐来的。」

===Original===
“Is Cynthia’s husband coming with her this afternoon?”

===Chinese===
「辛西娅的丈夫今天下午也来吗？」

===Original===
“No. I wanted it to be simply us four. Do you know him? But of course you don’t. This is his first visit to England.”

===Chinese===
「不来。我想就咱们四个聚。你认识他吗？噢，当然不认识。这是他头一回来英国。」

===Original===
“I know his poetry. He’s quite a celebrity. Cynthia’s lucky.”

===Chinese===
「我读过他的诗。他挺有名气的。辛西娅有福气。」

===Original===
They had made their way into the drawing-room, a gruesome little apartment full of all those antimacassars, wax flowers, and china dogs inseparable from the cheaper type of London furnished house. Eve, though the exterior of Number Eighteen should have prepared her for all this, was unable to check a slight shudder as she caught the eye of the least prepossessing of the dogs, goggling at her from the mantelpiece.

===Chinese===
两人进了客厅。那是间阴森可怖的小房间，摆满了椅背搭巾、蜡花和瓷狗——与伦敦廉价带家具出租的房屋结不解之缘的那几样。伊芙虽说早看过十八号的外表，理应心中有数，可当她的目光与狗群里那只最不堪入目的对上——它正从壁炉台上瞪眼瞅着她——仍禁不住微微打了个寒噤。

===Original===
“Don’t look at them,” recommended Phyllis, following her gaze. “I try not to. We’ve only just moved in here, so I haven’t had time to make the place nice. Here’s tea. All right, Jane, put it down there. Tea, Eve?”

===Chinese===
「别看它们。」菲利斯顺着她的目光劝道。「我自己就尽量不看。我们刚搬进来，还没工夫把这地方拾掇像样。茶来了。好的，简，搁那儿吧。喝茶吗，伊芙？」

===Original===
Eve sat down. She was puzzled and curious. She threw her mind back to the days at school and remembered the Phyllis of that epoch as almost indecently opulent. A millionaire stepfather there had been then, she recollected. What had become of him now, that he should allow Phyllis to stay in surroundings like this? Eve scented a mystery, and in her customary straightforward way went to the heart of it.

===Chinese===
伊芙坐了下来。她又疑惑又好奇。她的思绪回到学生时代，记得那一时期的菲利斯，阔绰得近乎有伤风化。她记得，那时是有一位百万富翁继父的。他如今怎么了，竟肯让菲利斯住在这样的环境里？伊芙嗅出了谜团，便照她一贯直来直去的路数，直捣谜心。

===Original===
“Tell me all about yourself,” she said, having achieved as much comfort as the peculiar structure of her chair would permit. “And remember that I haven’t seen you for two years, so don’t leave anything out.”

===Chinese===
「把你的事从头到尾讲给我听。」她说，一面在构造奇特的椅子里，安置下椅子所容许的那一点舒适。「记住，我有两年没见你了，什么也不许漏掉。」

===Original===
“It’s so difficult to know where to start.”

===Chinese===
「真难知道从哪儿说起。」

===Original===
“Well, you signed your letter ‘Phyllis Jackson.’ Start with the mysterious Jackson. Where does he come in? The last I heard about you was an announcement in the *Morning Post* that you were engaged to﻿—I’ve forgotten the name, but I’m certain it wasn’t Jackson.”

===Chinese===
「这样吧，你信末署的是『菲利斯·杰克逊』。就从这位神秘的杰克逊说起。他是打哪儿冒出来的？我听到的关于你的最后消息，是《晨邮报》（Morning Post）上一则订婚启事，说你与人订了婚——名字我忘了，但我敢肯定不叫杰克逊。」

===Original===
“Rollo Mountford.”

===Chinese===
「罗洛·芒特福德（Rollo Mountford）。」

===Original===
“Was it? Well, what has become of Rollo? You seem to have mislaid him. Did you break off the engagement?”

===Chinese===
「是吗？那罗洛后来怎么样了？你像是把他弄丢了。婚约是你解除的吗？」

===Original===
“Well, it﻿—sort of broke itself off. I mean, you see, I went and married Mike.”

===Chinese===
「呃，那个——算是它自己解除的吧。我是说，你看，我一转身嫁给了迈克。」

===Original===
“Eloped with him, do you mean?”

===Chinese===
「你是说，跟他私奔了？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是的。」

===Original===
“Good heavens!”

===Chinese===
「天哪！」

===Original===
“I’m awfully ashamed about that, Eve. I suppose I treated Rollo awfully badly.”

===Chinese===
「这事我惭愧极了，伊芙。我想我把罗洛坑苦了。」

===Original===
“Never mind. A man with a name like that was made for suffering.”

===Chinese===
「不打紧。生就叫那种名字的男人，天生是受苦的坯子。」

===Original===
“I never really cared for him. He had horrid swimmy eyes﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我从来没有真正喜欢过他。他那双眼睛水汪汪、雾蒙蒙的，讨厌得很……」

===Original===
“I understand. So you eloped with your Mike. Tell me about him. Who is he? What does he do?”

===Chinese===
「我明白了。于是你跟你的迈克私奔了。讲讲他吧。他是谁？做什么的？」

===Original===
“Well, at present he’s master at a school. But he doesn’t like it. He wants to get back to the country again. When I met him, he was agent on a place in the country belonging to some people named Smith. Mike had been at school and Cambridge with the son. They were very rich then and had a big estate. It was the next place to the Edgelows. I had gone to stay with Mary Edgelow﻿—I don’t know if you remember her at school? I met Mike first at a dance, and then I met him out riding, and then﻿—well, after that we used to meet every day. And we fell in love right from the start and we went and got married. Oh, Eve, I wish you could have seen our darling little house. It was all over ivy and roses, and we had horses and dogs and﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「眼下他在一所学校当教师。可他不喜欢。他想重回乡下去。我认识他的时候，他在乡下给一户姓史密斯的人家当庄园经纪人。迈克从前和那家的儿子同学，后来又一同进的剑桥。他们那时非常有钱，有很大一片产业，就挨着埃杰洛家的产业。我去玛丽·埃杰洛（Mary Edgelow）家住——不知你还记不记得她在学校时的样子？我头一回碰到迈克是在一次舞会上，后来骑马出去又碰上，再后来——呃，打那以后我们就天天见面。我们从头一天起就相爱了，然后就去结了婚。噢，伊芙，你要是能亲眼看看我们那可爱的小房子就好了。满墙满架的常春藤和蔷薇，我们还有马、有狗，还有……」

===Original===
Phyllis’ narrative broke off with a gulp. Eve looked at her sympathetically. All her life she herself had been joyously impecunious, but it had never seemed to matter. She was strong and adventurous, and revelled in the perpetual excitement of trying to make both ends meet. But Phyllis was one of those sweet porcelain girls whom the roughnesses of life bruise instead of stimulating. She needed comfort and pleasant surroundings. Eve looked morosely at the china dog, which leered back at her with an insufferable good-fellowship.

===Chinese===
菲利斯的叙述哽咽一声，中断了。伊芙同情地望着她。伊芙自己一辈子穷得兴高采烈，却从不觉得穷是回事。她坚强，爱闯荡，在月月拆东墙补西墙的持久刺激里如鱼得水。可菲利斯是那种甜美的瓷人儿姑娘，生活的粗粝落在她们身上，只会造成瘀伤，谈不上激励。她需要安逸，需要惬意的环境。伊芙郁郁地看了一眼那只瓷狗，瓷狗也回看她，一脸叫人受不了的亲热相。

===Original===
“We had hardly got married,” resumed Phyllis, blinking, “when poor Mr. Smith died and the whole place was broken up. He must have been speculating or something, I suppose, because he hardly left any money, and the estate had to be sold. And the people who bought it﻿—they were coal people from Wolverhampton﻿—had a nephew for whom they wanted the agent job, so Mike had to go. So here we are.”

===Chinese===
「我们结婚还没多久，」菲利斯眨着眼，续道，「可怜的史密斯先生就去世了，整个家业散了架。我猜他准是干了投机之类的名堂，因为几乎没留下钱，产业只好卖掉。买下产业的那户人家——是伍尔弗汉普顿（Wolverhampton）做煤生意的——自家有个侄子要安插到经纪人位子上，迈克只好走人。于是我们就落到这步田地。」

===Original===
Eve put the question which she had been waiting to ask ever since she had entered the house.

===Chinese===
伊芙提出了自打进这所房子起就一直等着要问的问题。

===Original===
“But what about your stepfather? Surely, when we were at school, you had a rich stepfather in the background. Has he lost his money, too?”

===Chinese===
「可你的继父呢？咱们上学那会儿，你背后分明有位阔气的继父。他也把钱丢了不成？」

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
「没有。」

===Original===
“Well, why doesn’t he help you, then?”

===Chinese===
「那他为什么不帮你们呢？」

===Original===
“He would, I know, if he was left to himself. But it’s Aunt Constance.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，由着他自己做主的话，他是肯的。可拦路的是康斯坦丝姑妈（Aunt Constance）。」

===Original===
“What’s Aunt Constance? And who *is* Aunt Constance?”

===Chinese===
「康斯坦丝姑妈是怎么回事？她到底又是何方神圣？」

===Original===
“Well, I call her that, but she’s really my stepmother﻿—sort of. I suppose she’s really my step-stepmother. My stepfather married again two years ago. It was Aunt Constance who was so furious when I married Mike. She wanted me to marry Rollo. She has never forgiven me, and she won’t let my stepfather do anything to help us.”

===Chinese===
「呃，我是那么叫她，其实她算是我的继母——勉强算吧。细究起来，该是继继母。我继父两年前续的弦。我嫁给迈克时，大发雷霆的就是这位康斯坦丝姑妈。她原要我嫁罗洛。她从没饶过我，也不许我继父帮我们半点忙。」

===Original===
“But the man must be a worm!” said Eve indignantly. “Why doesn’t he insist? You always used to tell me how fond he was of you.”

===Chinese===
「可这男人准是条软骨头！」伊芙愤然道。「他为什么不据理力争？你从前老跟我说，他多么疼你。」

===Original===
“He isn’t a worm, Eve. He’s a dear. It’s just that he has let her boss him. She’s rather a terror, you know. She can be quite nice, and they’re awfully fond of each other, but she is as hard as nails sometimes.” Phyllis broke off. The front door had opened, and there were footsteps in the hall. “Here’s Clarkie. I hope she has brought Cynthia with her. She was to pick her up on her way. Don’t talk about what I’ve been telling you in front of her, Eve, there’s an angel.”

===Chinese===
「他不是软骨头，伊芙。他是个可人儿。只是他让她管他管惯了。她可是个厉害角色，你知道。她也能相当和气，他们两口子彼此喜欢得很，只是她有时硬得像铁钉。」菲利斯顿住。前门开了，门厅里响起脚步声。「小克拉克来了。但愿她把辛西娅也一并带来了。她本该顺路去接的。伊芙，当着她的面，可别提我方才讲的这些事，行行好。」

===Original===
“Why not?”

===Chinese===
「为什么不能提？」

===Original===
“She’s so motherly about it. It’s sweet of her, but﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「她对这种事慈母劲儿十足。她心是好的，可是……」

===Original===
Eve understood.

===Chinese===
伊芙明白了。

===Original===
“All right. Later on.”

===Chinese===
「好吧。回头再谈。」

===Original===
The door opened to admit Miss Clarkson.

===Chinese===
门开了，克拉克森小姐驾到。

===Original===
The adjective which Phyllis had applied to her late schoolmistress was obviously well chosen. Miss Clarkson exuded motherliness. She was large, wholesome, and soft, and she swooped on Eve like a hen on its chicken almost before the door had closed.

===Chinese===
菲利斯加在那位昔日女教师身上的形容词，显然选得恰如其分。克拉克森小姐通身散发着慈母气质。她块头大，康健，绵软，门几乎还没关严，就老母鸡扑小鸡似的朝伊芙扑了过来。

===Original===
“Eve! How nice to see you after all this time! My dear, you’re looking perfectly lovely! And *so* prosperous. What a beautiful hat!”

===Chinese===
「伊芙！隔了这么久还能见到你，多好呀！亲爱的，你看上去美极了！而且还阔气得很哪。多漂亮的一顶帽子！」

===Original===
“I’ve been envying it ever since you came, Eve,” said Phyllis. “Where did you get it?”

===Chinese===
「打你一进门我就眼馋这顶帽子了，伊芙。」菲利斯说。「哪儿买的？」

===Original===
“Madeleine Sœurs, in Regent Street.”

===Chinese===
「摄政街（Regent Street）的马德琳姐妹（Madeleine Sœurs）。」

===Original===
Miss Clarkson, having acquired and stirred a cup of tea, started to improve the occasion. Eve had always been a favourite of hers at school. She beamed affectionately upon her.

===Chinese===
克拉克森小姐弄到一杯茶、搅了搅，便开始借机谆谆教诲。伊芙在校时一向是她的得意门生。她慈爱地朝她满脸堆笑。

===Original===
“Now doesn’t this show﻿—what I always used to say to you in the dear old days, Eve﻿—that one must never despair, however black the outlook may seem? I remember you at school, dear, as poor as a church mouse, and with no prospects, none whatever. And yet here you are﻿—rich﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「这不正印证了——我在那亲爱的往昔里常对你说的话么，伊芙——无论前景看起来多么漆黑，人都万不可绝望？我记得你上学那会儿，亲爱的，穷得像教堂里的老鼠，前途半点没有，半点都没有。可如今你瞧——阔了……」

===Original===
Eve laughed. She got up and kissed Miss Clarkson. She regretted that she was compelled to strike a jarring note, but it had to be done.

===Chinese===
伊芙笑了。她起身吻了吻克拉克森小姐。很遗憾，她不得不弹出一个不谐和音，可这一音非弹不可。

===Original===
“I’m awfully sorry, Clarkie dear,” she said, “but I’m afraid I’ve misled you. I’m just as broke as I ever was. In fact, when Phyllis told me you were running an Employment Agency, I made a note to come and see you and ask if you had some attractive billet to dispose of. Governess to a thoroughly angelic child would do. Or isn’t there some nice cosy author or something who wants his letters answered and his press-clippings pasted in an album?”

===Chinese===
「实在对不起，亲爱的小克拉克，」她说，「恐怕是我误导了你。我还是一如既往地一文不名。其实，菲利斯一告诉我你开着职业介绍所，我就记下要来见你，问问你手里有没有什么诱人的差事可以出手。给一个彻头彻尾天使般的孩子当家庭教师也行。要不然，有没有哪位安适可亲的作家之类的人物，需要有人替他回信、把剪报贴进剪贴簿？」

===Original===
“Oh, my dear!” Miss Clarkson was deeply concerned. “I did hope﻿ ﻿… That hat﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，亲爱的！」克拉克森小姐深深关切起来。「我原本还指望……那顶帽子……！」

===Original===
“The hat’s the whole trouble. Of course I had no business even to think of it, but I saw it in the shopwindow and coveted it for days, and finally fell. And then, you see, I had to live up to it﻿—buy shoes and a dress to match. I tell you it was a perfect orgy, and I’m thoroughly ashamed of myself now. Too late, as usual.”

===Chinese===
「祸根全在那顶帽子。当然，我本来连想都不该想它，可我在橱窗里见了它，眼馋了好几天，最后终于失守。接着呢，你瞧，我还得配得上它——买鞋、买相衬的连衣裙。跟你说吧，那简直是一场彻头彻尾的狂欢，如今我把自己羞得体无完肤。照例，为时已晚。」

===Original===
“Oh, dear! You always were such a wild, impetuous child, even at school. I remember how often I used to speak to you about it.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀呀！你从前就是这样一个野性难驯、莽莽撞撞的孩子，上学时就如此。我记得我曾多少次为这个说你。」

===Original===
“Well, when it was all over and I was sane again, I found I had only a few pounds left, not nearly enough to see me through till the relief expedition arrived. So I thought it over and decided to invest my little all.”

===Chinese===
「呃，等一切尘埃落定、我神智恢复如常，才发现自己只剩几镑钱了，远远撑不到救援远征队抵达。于是我前思后想，决定把我那点儿全部家当投出去。」

===Original===
“I hope you chose something safe?”

===Chinese===
「但愿你选的是稳妥的？」

===Original===
“It ought to have been. The *Sporting Express* called it ‘Today’s Safety Bet.’ It was Bounding Willie for the two-thirty race at Sandown last Wednesday.”

===Chinese===
「本该是稳妥的。《体育快报》（Sporting Express）管它叫『今日保险注』。就是上周三桑当（Sandown）两点半那一场的『飞奔威利』（Bounding Willie）。」

===Original===
“Oh, dear!”

===Chinese===
「哎呀呀！」

===Original===
“That’s what I said when poor old Willie came in sixth. But it’s no good worrying, is it? What it means is that I simply must find something to do that will carry me through till I get my next quarter’s allowance. And that won’t be till September.﻿ ﻿… But don’t let’s talk business here. I’ll come round to your office, Clarkie, tomorrow.﻿ ﻿… Where’s Cynthia? Didn’t you bring her?”

===Chinese===
「可怜的老威利跑了个第六名时，我说的也正是这句。不过光发愁没用，对吧？这意味着，我干脆必须找点事做，好撑到下一季津贴到手。而那要到九月呢。……不过咱们别在这儿谈正事了。明天我到你办公室去，小克拉克。……辛西娅呢？你没把她接来吗？」

===Original===
“Yes, I thought you were going to pick Cynthia up on your way, Clarkie,” said Phyllis.

===Chinese===
「是呀，我还当你顺路会把辛西娅接来呢，小克拉克。」菲利斯说。

===Original===
If Eve’s information as to her financial affairs had caused Miss Clarkson to mourn, the mention of Cynthia plunged her into the very depths of woe. Her mouth quivered and a tear stole down her cheek. Eve and Phyllis exchanged bewildered glances.

===Chinese===
如果说伊芙的财务状况只是令克拉克森小姐哀悼，那么辛西娅这名字一提，便把她径直掷进了悲伤的万丈深渊。她嘴唇哆嗦，一滴眼泪悄悄溜下脸颊。伊芙和菲利斯交换了一下大惑不解的眼色。

===Original===
“I say,” said Eve after a moment’s pause and a silence broken only by a smothered sob from their late instructress, “we aren’t being very cheerful, are we, considering that this is supposed to be a joyous reunion? Is anything wrong with Cynthia?”

===Chinese===
「我说，」沉吟片刻——其间只有昔日女教师一声强压下去的呜咽打破寂静——伊芙开口道，「咱们这样子可不太喜庆，是不是？要知道今天本该是场欢聚呢。辛西娅出什么事了吗？」

===Original===
So poignant was Miss Clarkson’s anguish that Phyllis, in a flutter of alarm, rose and left the room swiftly in search of the only remedy that suggested itself to her﻿—her smelling-salts.

===Chinese===
克拉克森小姐的苦痛如此锥心，菲利斯惊慌失措，起身疾步离屋，去找她能想出的惟一特效药——她的嗅盐。

===Original===
“Poor dear Cynthia!” moaned Miss Clarkson.

===Chinese===
「可怜的亲爱的辛西娅！」克拉克森小姐呻吟道。

===Original===
“Why, what’s the matter with her?” asked Eve. She was not callous to Miss Clarkson’s grief, but she could not help the tiniest of smiles. In a flash she had been transported to her schooldays, when the other’s habit of extracting the utmost tragedy out of the slimmest material had been a source of ever-fresh amusement to her. Not for an instant did she expect to hear any worse news of her old friend than that she was in bed with a cold or had twisted her ankle.

===Chinese===
「哟，她怎么了？」伊芙问。她对克拉克森小姐的悲痛并非无动于衷，却还是禁不住露出一丝最细微的笑。霎时间她被送回了学生时代——那时，对方从最单薄的素材里榨取极致悲剧的本事，于她是常看常新的乐子。她一刹那也没料到，会听到什么比老朋友卧床感冒或崴了脚脖子更糟的消息。

===Original===
“She’s married, you know,” said Miss Clarkson.

===Chinese===
「她结了婚了，你知道吧。」克拉克森小姐说。

===Original===
“Well, I see no harm in that, Clarkie. If a few more Safety Bets go wrong, I shall probably have to rush out and marry someone myself. Some nice, rich, indulgent man who will spoil me.”

===Chinese===
「这我看不出有什么不好，小克拉克。要是再错上几注保险注，我八成也得冲出门去，赶紧嫁个人。嫁个又好、又阔、又惯着我的男人，把我宠上天。」

===Original===
“Oh, Eve, my dear,” pleaded Miss Clarkson, bleating with alarm, “do please be careful whom you marry. I never hear of one of my girls marrying without feeling that the worst may happen and that, all unknowing, she may be stepping over a grim precipice!”

===Chinese===
「噢，伊芙，亲爱的，」克拉克森小姐告饶似的说，惊惶得咩咩直叫，「嫁人千万千万要挑仔细。每逢听说我哪个姑娘出嫁，我没有一次不觉得，大祸可能临头，觉得她一无所知地，也许正迈过一道阴森森的悬崖！」

===Original===
“You don’t *tell* them that, do you? Because I should think it would rather cast a damper on the wedding festivities. Has Cynthia gone stepping over grim precipices? I was just saying to Phyllis that I envied her, marrying a celebrity like Ralston McTodd.”

===Chinese===
「这话你总不见得真跟她们讲吧？要我看，那可要给婚礼的喜庆浇不小的冷水了。辛西娅已经迈过阴森悬崖了吗？我方才还跟菲利斯说，真羡慕她，嫁了拉尔斯顿·麦克托德这样的名人。」

===Original===
Miss Clarkson gulped.

===Chinese===
克拉克森小姐倒抽一口气。

===Original===
“The man must be a *fiend*!” she said brokenly. “I have just left poor dear Cynthia in floods of tears at the Cadogan Hotel﻿—she has a very nice quiet room on the fourth floor, though the carpet does not harmonise with the wallpaper.﻿ ﻿… She was brokenhearted, poor child. I did what I could to console her, but it was useless. She always was so highly strung. I must be getting back to her very soon. I only came on here because I did not want to disappoint you two dear girls﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「那男人准是个恶魔！」她断断续续地说。「我刚把可怜的亲爱的辛西娅留在卡多根酒店（Cadogan Hotel），她哭得泪如决堤——她在四楼有间挺不错的清静房间，虽说地毯与墙纸不大协调。……她心碎透了，可怜的孩子。我竭力安慰了她，可是没用。她一向就是神经这么纤细。我不久就得赶回她那儿去。我之所以还到这儿来，只因不愿叫你们两个宝贝姑娘失望……」

===Original===
“Why?” said Eve with quiet intensity. She knew from experience that Miss Clarkson, unless firmly checked, would pirouette round and round the point for minutes without ever touching it.

===Chinese===
「为什么？」伊芙说，语气平静而用力。她凭经验知道，克拉克森小姐若不加以果断拦阻，会绕着正题打转好几分钟，就是不着边际。

===Original===
“Why?” echoed Miss Clarkson, blinking as if the word was something solid that had struck her unexpectedly.

===Chinese===
「为什么？」克拉克森小姐应声重复，眨着眼，仿佛这个词是件实物，冷不丁砸在了她身上。

===Original===
“Why was Cynthia in floods of tears?”

===Chinese===
「辛西娅干吗哭得泪如决堤？」

===Original===
“But I’m telling you, my dear. That man has left her!”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，我正要告诉你呀。那男人把她甩了！」

===Original===
“Left her!”

===Chinese===
「甩了她！」

===Original===
“They had a quarrel, and he walked straight out of the hotel. That was the day before yesterday, and he has not been back since. This afternoon the curtest note came from him to say that he never intended to return. He had secretly and in a most underhand way arranged for his luggage to be removed from the hotel to a District Messenger office, and from there he has taken it no one knows where. He has completely disappeared.”

===Chinese===
「两口子吵了一架，他就大步走出旅馆，一去不回。那是前天的事，打那以后他再没回来过。今天下午来了他一封简慢到家的短简，说他永远不打算回来了。他还鬼鬼祟祟、用极其阴损的手段，安排人把行李从旅馆搬去一家区信差代办处，又从那儿取走，搬到了没人知道的地方。他彻底失踪了。」

===Original===
Eve stared. She had not been prepared for news of this momentous order.

===Chinese===
伊芙瞪大了眼。如此量级的消息，她是全无思想准备的。

===Original===
“But what did they quarrel about?”

===Chinese===
「可他们为什么吵呢？」

===Original===
“Cynthia, poor child, was too overwrought to tell me!”

===Chinese===
「辛西娅，可怜的孩子，激动得过了头，讲不出来！」

===Original===
Eve clenched her teeth.

===Chinese===
伊芙咬紧了牙关。

===Original===
“The beast!﻿ ﻿… Poor old Cynthia.﻿ ﻿… Shall I come round with you?”

===Chinese===
「这畜生！……可怜的老辛西娅。……要不要我陪你一道去？」

===Original===
“No, my dear, better let me look after her alone. I will tell her to write and let you know when she can see you. I must be going, Phyllis dear,” she said, as her hostess reentered, bearing a small bottle.

===Chinese===
「不必了，亲爱的，还是让我独自照应她吧。我会叫她写信，什么时候能见你了通知你。我得走了，亲爱的菲利斯。」这时女主人拿着一只小瓶子回到屋里，她说道。

===Original===
“But you’ve only just come!” said Phyllis, surprised.

===Chinese===
「可你刚到呀！」菲利斯讶然道。

===Original===
“Poor old Cynthia’s husband has left her,” explained Eve briefly. “And Clarkie’s going back to look after her. She’s in a pretty bad way, it seems.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的老辛西娅被丈夫甩了。」伊芙简短地解释。「小克拉克要回去照应她。看样子她情形不妙。」

===Original===
“Oh, no!”

===Chinese===
「噢，不会吧！」

===Original===
“Yes, indeed. And I really must be going at once,” said Miss Clarkson.

===Chinese===
「是真的。而且我实在必须马上走了。」克拉克森小姐说。

===Original===
Eve waited in the drawing-room till the front door banged and Phyllis came back to her. Phyllis was more wistful than ever. She had been looking forward to this tea-party, and it had not been the happy occasion she had anticipated. The two girls sat in silence for a moment.

===Chinese===
伊芙在客厅里守到前门砰地关上，菲利斯回到她身边。菲利斯比先前更是一脸怅然。她原本一心盼着这场茶会，可它终究不是她期盼的那种欢会。两个姑娘默默坐了片刻。

===Original===
“What brutes some men are!” said Eve at length.

===Chinese===
「有些男人可真不是东西！」伊芙终于开口。

===Original===
“Mike,” said Phyllis dreamily, “is an angel.”

===Chinese===
「迈克呀，」菲利斯悠悠地说，「是位天使。」

===Original===
Eve welcomed the unspoken invitation to return to a more agreeable topic. She felt very deeply for the stricken Cynthia, but she hated aimless talk, and nothing could have been more aimless than for her and Phyllis to sit there exchanging lamentations concerning a tragedy of which neither knew more than the bare outlines. Phyllis had her tragedy, too, and it was one where Eve saw the possibility of doing something practical and helpful. She was a girl of action, and was glad to be able to attack a living issue.

===Chinese===
这无人明言的邀请，正中伊芙下怀——回到一个更愉快的话题上去。她对遭难的辛西娅深感痛惜，可她厌恶漫无目的的空谈，而天下最漫无目的的事，莫过于她和菲利斯坐在那里，对一出两人都只知个大概轮廓的悲剧你叹我怜。菲利斯也有她的悲剧，而且在那出悲剧里，伊芙看到了做点切实有益之事的可能。她是个行动派的姑娘，巴不得能向一个活生生的问题发起进攻。

===Original===
“Yes, let’s go on talking about you and Mike,” she said. “At present I can’t understand the position at all. When Clarkie came in, you were just telling me about your stepfather and why he wouldn’t help you. And I thought you made out a very poor case for him. Tell me some more. I’ve forgotten his name, by the way.”

===Chinese===
「对，咱们接着谈你和迈克。」她说。「眼下这局面我压根儿摸不着头脑。小克拉克进来的时候，你正跟我讲你继父，讲他为什么不帮你们。而且我觉得，你把他这案子辩护得够糟的。再多讲点。对了，他叫什么名字来着，我忘了。」

===Original===
“Keeble.”

===Chinese===
「基布尔（Keeble）。」

===Original===
“Oh? Well, I think you ought to write and tell him how hard-up you are. He may be under the impression that you are still living in luxury and don’t need any help. After all, he can’t know unless you tell him. And I should ask him straight out to come to the rescue. It isn’t as if it was your Mike’s fault that you’re broke. He married you on the strength of a very good position which looked like a permanency, and lost it through no fault of his own. I should write to him, Phyl. Pitch it strong.”

===Chinese===
「哦？我说，你该写信告诉他，你们的日子多紧巴。他没准儿还当你们过着锦衣玉食的日子，用不着人帮。说到底，你不告诉他，他无从知道。要我就干脆直截了当，请他出手救驾。你们眼下缺钱，又不能怪你的迈克。他娶你的时候，凭的是一份好得看似终身制的职位，丢了它也全不是他的错。要写就写信给他，菲尔。下笔要狠。」

===Original===
“I have. I wrote today. Mike’s just been offered a wonderful opportunity. A sort of farm place in Lincolnshire. You know. Cows and things. Just what he would like and just what he would do awfully well. And we only need three thousand pounds to get it.﻿ ﻿… But I’m afraid nothing will come of it.”

===Chinese===
「写了。今天就写的。迈克刚得到一个绝好的机会。林肯郡（Lincolnshire）的一种农场差事。你知道的，奶牛什么的。正对他的心思，也正是一样他会干得非常出色的事。我们只消三千英镑就成。……可恐怕到头来是一场空。」

===Original===
“Because of Aunt Constance, you mean?”

===Chinese===
「你是说，因为康斯坦丝姑妈？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是的。」

===Original===
“You must *make* something come of it.” Eve’s chin went up. She looked like a Goddess of Determination. “If I were you, I’d haunt their doorstep till they had to give you the money to get rid of you. The idea of anybody doing that absurd driving-into-the-snow business in these days! Why *shouldn’t* you marry the man you were in love with? If I were you, I’d go and chain myself to their railings and howl like a dog till they rushed out with chequebooks just to get some peace. Do they live in London?”

===Chinese===
「你就非让它成不可。」伊芙下巴一扬。她那模样，活像一尊决心女神。「我要是你，就在他们家门槛上死缠烂打，缠到他们不得不掏钱打发你。都什么年代了，居然还有人玩『往风雪里赶人』那套荒唐把戏！你爱上了谁，凭什么不能嫁？我要是你，就跑去把自己拴在他们家栏杆上，学狗一样嚎，嚎到他们攥着支票簿冲出来买清静。他们住伦敦吗？」

===Original===
“They are down in Shropshire at present at a place called Blandings Castle.”

===Chinese===
「他们眼下在什罗普郡（Shropshire），一个叫布兰丁斯城堡的地方。」

===Original===
Eve started.

===Chinese===
伊芙一怔。

===Original===
“Blandings Castle? Good gracious!”

===Chinese===
「布兰丁斯城堡？天哪！」

===Original===
“Aunt Constance is Lord Emsworth’s sister.”

===Chinese===
「康斯坦丝姑妈是埃姆斯沃思勋爵的妹妹。」

===Original===
“But this is the most extraordinary thing. I’m going to Blandings myself in a few days.”

===Chinese===
「这可真是天下第一巧事了。过几天我自己就要去布兰丁斯呢。」

===Original===
“No!”

===Chinese===
「不会吧！」

===Original===
“They’ve engaged me to catalogue the castle library.”

===Chinese===
「他们请了我去给城堡的藏书编目。」

===Original===
“But, Eve, were you only joking when you asked Clarkie to find you something to do? She took you quite seriously.”

===Chinese===
「可是伊芙，你刚才请小克拉克帮你找事做，难道只是说着玩的？她可是当了真的。」

===Original===
“No, I wasn’t joking. There’s a drawback to my going to Blandings. I suppose you know the place pretty well?”

===Chinese===
「不是玩笑。我去布兰丁斯这事，有桩美中不足。这地方你该很熟吧？」

===Original===
“I’ve often stayed there. It’s beautiful.”

===Chinese===
「我常去小住。那地方美极了。」

===Original===
“Then you know Lord Emsworth’s second son, Freddie Threepwood?”

===Chinese===
「那你认识埃姆斯沃思勋爵的次子弗雷迪·思里普伍德吧？」

===Original===
“Of course.”

===Chinese===
「当然认识。」

===Original===
“Well, he’s the drawback. He wants to marry me, and I certainly don’t want to marry him. And what I’ve been wondering is whether a nice easy job like that, which would tide me over beautifully till September, is attractive enough to make up for the nuisance of having to be always squelching poor Freddie. I ought to have thought of it right at the beginning, of course, when he wrote and told me to apply for the position, but I was so delighted at the idea of regular work that it didn’t occur to me. Then I began to wonder. He’s such a persevering young man. He proposes early and often.”

===Chinese===
「呃，美中不足就是他。他想娶我，而我绝对不想嫁他。我近来一直在琢磨：那样一份舒坦轻省的差事——能让我体体面面撑到九月——它的诱人之处，抵不抵得过这份劳神：老是要狠下心去，扑灭可怜的弗雷迪的痴心。当然，早在他来信叫我去应聘的时候，我一开头就该想到这层，可我一想到有份正经工作，就乐昏了头，压根儿没往这儿想。后来我才起了疑心。这位青年可真锲而不舍。他求婚既早且勤。」

===Original===
“Where did you meet Freddie?”

===Chinese===
「你在哪儿认识弗雷迪的？」

===Original===
“At a theatre party. About two months ago. He was living in London then, but he suddenly disappeared and I had a heartbroken letter from him, saying that he had been running up debts and things and his father had snatched him away to live at Blandings, which apparently is Freddie’s idea of the Inferno. The world seems full of hard-hearted relatives.”

===Chinese===
「在一次看戏的聚会上。约莫两个月前。他那时住在伦敦，后来突然人间蒸发，我收到他一封肝肠寸断的信，说他欠了一屁股债等等，被父亲一把拎去布兰丁斯居住——看那意思，布兰丁斯在弗雷迪心目中俨然就是地狱。这世上的狠心亲戚可真不少。」

===Original===
“Oh, Lord Emsworth isn’t really hard-hearted. You will love him. He’s so dreamy and absentminded. He potters about the garden all the time. I don’t think you’ll like Aunt Constance much. But I suppose you won’t see a great deal of her.”

===Chinese===
「噢，埃姆斯沃思勋爵倒不是真狠心。你会喜欢他的。他那么恍惚，那么心不在焉，成天在园子里闲逛悠。康斯坦丝姑妈嘛，我看你未必喜欢。不过你想必也见不着她几面。」

===Original===
“Whom *shall* I see much of﻿—except Freddie, of course?”

===Chinese===
「那我倒会常见着谁呢——当然，弗雷迪不算？」

===Original===
“Mr. Baxter, Lord Emsworth’s secretary, I expect. I don’t like him at all. He’s a sort of spectacled caveman.”

===Chinese===
「大概是巴克斯特先生（Mr. Baxter）吧，埃姆斯沃思勋爵的秘书。我一点儿也不喜欢他。他算是戴眼镜的穴居人。」

===Original===
“He doesn’t sound attractive. But you say the place is nice?”

===Chinese===
「听着就不讨人喜欢。可你说那地方不错？」

===Original===
“It’s gorgeous. I should go, if I were you, Eve.”

===Chinese===
「美轮美奂。我要是你，就去，伊芙。」

===Original===
“Well, I had intended not to. But now you’ve told me about Mr. Keeble and Aunt Constance, I’ve changed my mind. I’ll have to look in at Clarkie’s office tomorrow and tell her I’m fixed up and shan’t need her help. I’m going to take your sad case in hand, darling. I shall go to Blandings, and I will dog your stepfather’s footsteps.﻿ ﻿… Well, I must be going. Come and see me to the front door, or I’ll be losing my way in the miles of stately corridors.﻿ ﻿… I suppose I mayn’t smash that china dog before I go? Oh, well, I just thought I’d ask.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，我原是不打算去的。可如今你把基布尔先生和康斯坦丝姑妈的事一说，我改主意了。明天我得到小克拉克的办公室去一趟，告诉她我有着落了，用不着她帮忙了。你这笔伤心案，交到我手里来办吧，亲爱的。我要去布兰丁斯，还要死死盯住你继父的脚步。……好了，我该走了。送我到前门口来，不然我会在这几英里长的堂皇走廊里迷路的。……我想，走之前总不能把那只瓷狗砸了吧？唉，罢了，随口一问。」

===Original===
Out in the hall the little maid-of-all-work bobbed up and intercepted them.

===Chinese===
到了门厅，那个打杂的小女仆冷不丁冒出来，拦住了她们。

===Original===
“I forgot to tell you, mum, a gentleman called. I told him you was out.”

===Chinese===
「太太，我忘了说了，有位先生来过。我告诉他您外出了。」

===Original===
“Quite right, Jane.”

===Chinese===
「做得对，简。」

===Original===
“Said his name was Smith, ’m.”

===Chinese===
「他说他姓史密斯，太太。」

===Original===
Phyllis gave a cry of dismay.

===Chinese===
菲利斯发出一声懊恼的惊呼。

===Original===
“Oh, no! What a shame! I particularly wanted you to meet him, Eve. I wish I’d known.”

===Chinese===
「噢，不！多可惜！我特别想让你见见他，伊芙。早知道就好了。」

===Original===
“Smith?” said Eve. “The name seems familiar. Why were you so anxious for me to meet him?”

===Chinese===
「史密斯？」伊芙说。「这名字好像有点耳熟。你为什么这么急着让我见他？」

===Original===
“He’s Mike’s best friend. Mike worships him. He’s the son of the Mr. Smith I was telling you about﻿—the one Mike was at school and Cambridge with. He’s a perfect darling, Eve, and you would love him. He’s just your sort. I do wish we had known. And now you’re going to Blandings for goodness knows how long, and you won’t be able to see him.”

===Chinese===
「他是迈克最要好的朋友。迈克崇拜他。他就是我刚才说到的史密斯先生的儿子——迈克和他同学、又一同进剑桥的就是这位。他是个百里挑一的可人儿，伊芙，你准会喜欢他。他正是你那一路的人。我们要是早知道就好了。这下你要去布兰丁斯，一去不知多久，就见不着他了。」

===Original===
“What a pity,” said Eve, politely uninterested.

===Chinese===
「真可惜。」伊芙说，客气而毫无兴致。

===Original===
“I’m so sorry for him.”

===Chinese===
「我真替他惋惜。」

===Original===
“Why?”

===Chinese===
「为什么？」

===Original===
“He’s in the fish business.”

===Chinese===
「他在鱼行做事。」

===Original===
“Ugh!”

===Chinese===
「呸！」

===Original===
“Well, he hates it, poor dear. But he was left stranded like all the rest of us after the crash, and he was put into the business by an uncle who is a sort of fish magnate.”

===Chinese===
「唉，他厌恶透了，可怜的人。可是家道骤败之后，他和我们大家一样被撂在了滩上，又被一位鱼业大亨般的叔父塞进了鱼行。」

===Original===
“Well, why does he stay there, if he dislikes it so much?” said Eve with indignation. The helpless type of man was her pet aversion. “I hate a man who’s got no enterprise.”

===Chinese===
「那他既然这么不喜欢，干吗还待在那儿？」伊芙愤愤地说。无能型的男人，是她最看不上的一类。「我恨没有闯劲的男人。」

===Original===
“I don’t think you could call him unenterprising. He never struck me like that.﻿ ﻿… You simply must meet him when you come back to London.”

===Chinese===
「我看你可不能说他没有闯劲。他从没给我那种印象。……你回伦敦后，说什么也得见见他。」

===Original===
“All right,” said Eve indifferently. “Just as you like. I might put business in his way. I’m very fond of fish.”

===Chinese===
「好吧。」伊芙淡淡地说。「随你的便。没准儿我还能给他介绍点生意。我很爱吃鱼。」

## III Eve Borrows an Umbrella / 伊芙借伞

===Original===
What strikes the visitor to London most forcibly, as he enters the heart of that city’s fashionable shopping district, is the almost entire absence of ostentation in the shopwindows, the studied avoidance of garish display. About the front of the premises of Messrs. Thorpe & Briscoe, for instance, who sell coal in Dover Street, there is as a rule nothing whatever to attract fascinated attention. You might give the place a glance as you passed, but you would certainly not pause and stand staring at it as at the Sistine Chapel or the Taj Mahal. Yet at ten-thirty on the morning after Eve Halliday had taken tea with her friend Phyllis Jackson in West Kensington, Psmith, lounging gracefully in the smoking-room window of the Drones Club, which is immediately opposite the Thorpe & Briscoe establishment, had been gazing at it fixedly for a full five minutes. One would have said that the spectacle enthralled him. He seemed unable to take his eyes off it.

===Chinese===
游客一踏进伦敦时髦商业区的腹地，感受最强烈的莫过于：各家橱窗几乎全然不事铺张，刻意回避着花哨扎眼的陈列。譬如多佛街（Dover Street）上卖煤的索普-布里斯科商店（Messrs. Thorpe & Briscoe），其门面照例便无一丝足以令人流连注目之处。路过时你兴许会瞥它一眼，却决计不会像瞻仰西斯廷礼拜堂或泰姬陵那般驻足痴望。然而，在伊芙·哈利迪（Eve Halliday）去西肯辛顿的朋友菲利斯·杰克逊（Phyllis Jackson）家喝过茶的翌日上午十点半，皮史密斯（Psmith）——他正风度优雅地闲倚在「蜂子俱乐部」（the Drones Club）吸烟室的窗边，而俱乐部恰恰与索普-布里斯科商店隔街相望——却已对着那片门面整整凝望了五分钟。简直可以说，这番景象把他彻底迷住了。他似乎连眼睛都无法从上面挪开。

===Original===
There is always a reason for the most apparently inexplicable happenings. It is the practice of Thorpe (or Briscoe) during the months of summer to run out an awning over the shop. A quiet, genteel awning, of course, nothing to offend the eye﻿—but an awning which offers a quite adequate protection against those sudden showers which are such a delightfully piquant feature of the English summer: one of which had just begun to sprinkle the West End of London with a good deal of heartiness and vigour. And under this awning, peering plaintively out at the rain, Eve Halliday, on her way to the Ada Clarkson Employment Bureau, had taken refuge. It was she who had so enchained Psmith’s interest. It was his considered opinion that she improved the Thorpe & Briscoe frontage by about ninety-five percent.

===Chinese===
再貌似离奇的变故，背后也总有缘故。每逢夏季，索普（或者布里斯科）照例要在店门外支出一领遮阳篷。自然是顶素净体面的遮阳篷，绝无半点刺眼之处——但它足可抵挡骤雨，而骤雨，素来是英国夏天里一道别有风味的景致。此刻恰好有一场，正以十足的热忱和干劲，朝伦敦西区噼里啪啦地泼洒下来。而正在去艾达·克拉克森职业介绍所（Ada Clarkson Employment Bureau）路上的伊芙·哈利迪，便躲在这领遮阳篷下，哀怨地凝望着雨。把皮史密斯的兴味如此牢牢锁住的，正是她。依他深思熟审的见解，她给索普-布里斯科商店的门面增色了约莫百分之九十五。

===Original===
Pleased and gratified as Psmith was to have something nice to look at out of the smoking-room window, he was also somewhat puzzled. This girl seemed to him to radiate an atmosphere of wealth. Starting at farthest south and proceeding northward, she began in a gleam of patent-leather shoes. Fawn stockings, obviously expensive, led up to a black crêpe frock. And then, just as the eye was beginning to feel that there could be nothing more, it was stunned by a supreme hat of soft, dull satin with a black bird of Paradise feather falling down over the left shoulder. Even to the masculine eye, which is notoriously to seek in these matters, a whale of a hat. And yet this sumptuously upholstered young woman had been marooned by a shower of rain beneath the awning of Messrs. Thorpe & Briscoe. Why, Psmith asked himself, was this? Even, he argued, if Charles the chauffeur had been given the day off or was driving her father the millionaire to the City to attend to his vast interests, she could surely afford a cab-fare? We, who are familiar with the state of Eve’s finances, can understand her inability to take cabs, but Psmith was frankly perplexed.

===Chinese===
吸烟室窗外有了悦目风景可看，皮史密斯固然欢喜受用，却也颇有几分纳闷。在他看来，这位姑娘周身焕发着一股富贵之气。自最南端起，循北而上：起手是漆皮鞋一抹亮泽；小鹿色的长袜一看便价格不菲，向上接一袭黑色绉纱连衣裙；而正当眼睛渐渐觉得此间再无胜景，一顶登峰造极的帽子又将它震住：软缎制成，柔光内敛，一支黑色天堂鸟翎羽自左肩翩然垂下。即便以男性的眼光——众所周知，男人在这类事上向来不甚灵敏——这也是一顶了不得的帽子。然而这位装潢得富丽堂皇的姑娘，竟被一阵骤雨困在索普-布里斯科商店的遮阳篷底下。这是为何？皮史密斯暗自纳闷。他推想，纵然司机查尔斯今日歇班，或是正开车送她那位百万富翁父亲上金融城去照管他的宏图伟业，她总还付得起一趟车钱吧？我们这些熟知伊芙财务状况的人，自能明白她何以雇不起车，可皮史密斯委实一头雾水。

===Original===
Being, however, both ready-witted and chivalrous, he perceived that this was no time for idle speculation. His not to reason why; his obvious duty was to take steps to assist Beauty in distress. He left the window of the smoking-room, and, having made his way with a smooth dignity to the club’s cloakroom, proceeded to submit a row of umbrellas to a close inspection. He was not easy to satisfy. Two which he went so far as to pull out of the rack he returned with a shake of the head. Quite good umbrellas, but not fit for this special service. At length, however, he found a beauty, and a gentle smile flickered across his solemn face. He put up his monocle and gazed searchingly at this umbrella. It seemed to answer every test. He was well pleased with it.

===Chinese===
不过，皮史密斯素来机敏，又具骑士肝胆，当即看出此刻绝非闲想玄思之时。追问缘由，非他本分；他昭然若揭的义务，是设法搭救落难的美人。他离开吸烟室窗边，以从容的气派踱到俱乐部衣帽间，着手对一排雨伞逐把严加检验。他可不容易满足。有两把他都从架上抽了出来，末了还是摇头放了回去。伞是好伞，只是当不起这趟特别的差事。不过，他终于寻出一把妙品，庄重的脸上掠过一丝温和的浅笑。他戴上单片眼镜，将这把伞细细审视。它似乎经得起一切考验。他大为满意。

===Original===
“Whose,” he inquired of the attendant, “is this?”

===Chinese===
「这把伞，」他向侍者打听，「是哪位的？」

===Original===
“Belongs to the Honourable Mr. Walderwick, sir.”

===Chinese===
「回先生，是沃尔德威克少爷的。」

===Original===
“Ah!” said Psmith tolerantly.

===Chinese===
「啊！」皮史密斯宽容地说。

===Original===
He tucked the umbrella under his arm and went out.

===Chinese===
他把伞往腋下一夹，出门去了。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Meanwhile Eve Halliday, lightening up the sombre austerity of Messrs. Thorpe & Briscoe’s shopfront, continued to think hard thoughts of the English climate and to inspect the sky in the hope of detecting a spot of blue. She was engaged in this cheerless occupation when at her side a voice spoke.

===Chinese===
与此同时，还在替索普-布里斯科商店阴沉肃穆的门面增光添彩的伊芙·哈利迪，继续对英国气候恶狠狠地腹诽，一面检视天空，指望能侦察出一角蓝天。她正埋头于这项凄清的活计，身旁忽然有人开了口。

===Original===
“Excuse me!”

===Chinese===
「打扰一下！」

===Original===
A hatless young man was standing beside her, holding an umbrella. He was a striking-looking young man, very tall, very thin, and very well dressed. In his right eye there was a monocle, and through this he looked down at her with a grave friendliness. He said nothing further, but, taking her fingers, clasped them round the handle of the umbrella, which he had obligingly opened, and then with a courteous bow proceeded to dash with long strides across the road, disappearing through the doorway of a gloomy building which, from the number of men who had gone in and out during her vigil, she had set down as a club of some sort.

===Chinese===
一个未戴帽子的年轻人立在她身旁，手里执一把伞。他是个模样出挑的年轻人，个子极高，身形极瘦，衣着极讲究。他右眼上嵌着单片眼镜，正透过镜片，以庄重而友善的神情俯视她。他再无二话，只牵起她的手指，替它们扣上伞柄——伞已殷勤地撑开——随即谦恭一躬，迈开大步横穿马路，消失在对面一座阴沉沉的楼房门内；凭她守候期间进进出出的男士数目，她早断定那是家什么俱乐部。

===Original===
A good many surprising things had happened to Eve since first she had come to live in London, but nothing quite so surprising as this. For several minutes she stood where she was without moving, staring round-eyed at the building opposite. The episode was, however, apparently ended. The young man did not reappear. He did not even show himself at the window. The club had swallowed him up. And eventually Eve, deciding that this was not the sort of day on which to refuse umbrellas even if they dropped inexplicably from heaven, stepped out from under the awning, laughing helplessly, and started to resume her interrupted journey to Miss Clarkson’s.

===Chinese===
自打来伦敦安身，伊芙经历的稀奇事着实不少，却没有一桩及得上这桩稀奇。她原地纹丝不动站了好几分钟，圆睁双眼呆望着对面那座楼。不过，这段插曲显然已然收场。年轻人没有再露面，甚至连窗口也不曾现身。俱乐部把他整个吞了下去。终于，伊芙认定，今天可不是回绝雨伞的日子——哪怕这伞来得莫名其妙、直如天降——便从遮阳篷下跨出，无可奈何地笑着，重新踏上那趟被打断的旅程，往克拉克森小姐（Miss Clarkson）那里去了。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The offices of the Ada Clarkson International Employment Bureau (“Promptitude﻿—Courtesy﻿—Intelligence”) are at the top of Shaftesbury Avenue, a little way past the Palace Theatre. Eve, closing the umbrella, which had prevented even a spot of rain falling on her hat, climbed the short stair leading to the door and tapped on the window marked “Enquiries.”

===Chinese===
艾达·克拉克森国际职业介绍所（Ada Clarkson International Employment Bureau，所训「敏捷——殷勤——睿智」）的办公处设在沙夫茨伯里大道顶端，过了宫殿剧院（Palace Theatre）再往前走一小段便是。伊芙收拢雨伞——有它保驾，连一丝雨星也不曾沾上她的帽子——登上通向大门的短短台阶，在标着「问询」的窗上敲了敲。

===Original===
“Can I see Miss Clarkson?”

===Chinese===
「我能见见克拉克森小姐吗？」

===Original===
“What name, please?” responded Enquiries promptly and with intelligent courtesy.

===Chinese===
「请问尊姓大名？」问询处应声作答，既敏捷，又殷勤，还透着睿智。

===Original===
“Miss Halliday.”

===Chinese===
「哈利迪小姐。」

===Original===
Brief interlude, involving business with speaking-tube.

===Chinese===
短暂过场，其间经通话管接洽了一回公务。

===Original===
“Will you go into the private office, please,” said Enquiries a moment later, in a voice which now added respect to the other advertised qualities, for she had had time to observe and digest the hat.

===Chinese===
「请您进里间办公室来。」片刻之后，问询处又开了腔——此刻的嗓音，在招牌所标榜的诸般品质之外又添了一层敬意，因为她已有工夫把那顶帽子端详清楚、消化完毕。

===Original===
Eve passed in through the general waiting-room with its magazine-covered table, and tapped at the door beyond marked “Private.”

===Chinese===
伊芙穿过外间大候客室——室内的桌上摊满了杂志——在里头那扇标着「私人」的门上叩了叩。

===Original===
“Eve, dear!” exclaimed Miss Clarkson the moment she had entered, “I don’t know how to tell you, but I have been looking through my books and I have nothing, simply nothing. There is not a single place that you could possibly take. What *is* to be done?”

===Chinese===
「伊芙，亲爱的！」她一进门，克拉克森小姐便嚷了起来，「我真不知道怎么开口才好。我把登记簿翻了个遍，结果什么空缺也没有，压根儿什么也没有。连一个你能去的职位都寻不出来。这可到底怎么办呢？」

===Original===
“That’s all right, Clarkie.”

===Chinese===
「不打紧，小克拉克（Clarkie）。」

===Original===
“But﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「可是……」

===Original===
“I didn’t come to talk business. I came to ask after Cynthia. How is she?”

===Chinese===
「我不是来谈正事的。我是来问候辛西娅（Cynthia）的。她怎么样了？」

===Original===
Miss Clarkson sighed.

===Chinese===
克拉克森小姐叹了口气。

===Original===
“Poor child, she is still in a dreadful state, and no wonder. No news at all from her husband. He has simply deserted her.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的孩子，她还是难受得厉害，这也难怪。她丈夫那边音信全无。他简直是把她抛弃了。」

===Original===
“Poor darling! Can’t I see her?”

===Chinese===
「可怜的人儿！我不能去看看她吗？」

===Original===
“Not at present. I have persuaded her to go down to Brighton for a day or two. I think the sea air will pick her up. So much better than mooning about in a London hotel. She is leaving on the eleven o’clock train. I gave her your love, and she was most grateful that you should have remembered your old friendship and be sorry for her in her affliction.”

===Chinese===
「眼下不行。我劝她去布莱顿（Brighton）待上一两天，我想海边的空气会让她缓过来，总比在伦敦一家旅馆里愁眉苦脸地闲荡强得多。她搭十一点钟的火车走。你的心意我已带到，她十分感激你还念着旧日的情分、还怜惜她的遭际。」

===Original===
“Well, I can write to her. Where is she staying?”

===Chinese===
「那好，我可以写信给她。她住在哪儿？」

===Original===
“I don’t know her Brighton address, but no doubt the Cadogan Hotel would forward letters. I think she would be glad to hear from you, dear.”

===Chinese===
「她在布莱顿的地址我不知道，不过卡多根酒店（Cadogan Hotel）想必肯代转信件。我想她收到你的信一定高兴，亲爱的。」

===Original===
Eve looked sadly at the framed testimonials which decorated the wall. She was not often melancholy, but it was such a beast of a day and all her friends seemed to be having such a bad time.

===Chinese===
伊芙怅然望着墙上装裱在镜框里的那些褒扬信。她平日不大伤怀，可今天这天气实在讨厌透顶，而她的朋友们又似乎个个都在遭难。

===Original===
“Oh, Clarkie,” she said, “what a lot of trouble there is in the world!”

===Chinese===
「唉，小克拉克，」她说，「这世上的烦恼可真多啊！」

===Original===
“Yes, yes!” sighed Miss Clarkson, a specialist on this subject.

===Chinese===
「可不是，可不是！」克拉克森小姐叹道——论这门学问，她是专家。

===Original===
“All the horses you back finish sixth and all the girls you like best come croppers. Poor little Phyllis! weren’t you sorry for her?”

===Chinese===
「你押的马匹匹以第六名收场，你最喜欢的姑娘个个栽跟头。可怜的小菲利斯！你当时不替她难过吗？」

===Original===
“But her husband, surely, is most devoted?”

===Chinese===
「可她的丈夫，总归是一片深情吧？」

===Original===
“Yes, but she’s frightfully hard up, and you remember how opulent she used to be at school. Of course, it must sound funny hearing me pitying people for having no money. But somehow other people’s hard-upness always seems so much worse than mine. Especially poor old Phyl’s, because she really isn’t fit to stand it. I’ve been used to being absolutely broke all my life. Poor dear father always seemed to be writing an article against time, with creditors scratching earnestly at the door.” Eve laughed, but her eyes were misty. “He was a brick, wasn’t he? I mean, sending me to a first-class school like Wayland House when he often hadn’t enough money to buy tobacco, poor angel. I expect he wasn’t always up to time with fees, was he?”

===Chinese===
「深情是深情，可她穷得吓人，你也记得她从前在学校里是何等阔气。当然，听我来怜惜别人没钱，八成显得滑稽。可不知怎么，别人的拮据总好像比我的惨得多。尤其是可怜的老菲利斯的，因为她实在不是熬穷的料。我这辈子可是穷惯了的。可怜的亲爱的父亲，似乎永远在跟时间赛跑地赶稿子，门外债主殷勤地挠着门。」伊芙笑了，眼里却蒙起一层雾。「他可真是个好人，是不是？我是说，他自己常常连买烟丝的钱都不凑手，却还送我进韦兰德女子学校（Wayland House）那样一流的学堂，可怜的天使。我料想他的学费未必回回按时缴清，是吧？」

===Original===
“Well, my dear, of course I was only an assistant mistress at Wayland House and had nothing to do with the financial side, but I did hear sometimes﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「呃，亲爱的，当然啦，我当年在韦兰德不过是个助理女教师，财务上的事一概与我无干，只是偶尔也确曾听见些……」

===Original===
“Poor darling father! Do you know, one of my earliest recollections﻿—I couldn’t have been more than ten﻿—is of a ring at the front-door bell and father diving like a seal under the sofa and poking his head out and imploring me in a hoarse voice to hold the fort. I went to the door and found an indignant man with a blue paper. I prattled so prettily and innocently that he not only went away quite contentedly but actually patted me on the head and gave me a penny. And when the door had shut father crawled out from under the sofa and gave me twopence, making threepence in all﻿—a good morning’s work. I bought father a diamond ring with it at a shop down the street, I remember. At least I thought it was a diamond. They may have swindled me, for I was very young.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的亲爱的父亲！你知道吗，我最早的记忆之一——那年我至多十岁——便是前门铃一响，父亲海豹似的一个猛子扎进沙发底下，探出脑袋，哑着嗓子央求我守住阵地。我开了门，见到一位怒气冲冲的先生，手里攥着一张蓝纸催款单。我咿咿呀呀，说得又乖巧又天真，他不但心满意足地走了，居然还拍拍我的头，赏了我一个便士。等门一关，父亲从沙发底下爬出来，又给了我两便士——合计三便士，一个上午的进账着实不俗。我记得还拿这笔钱在街那头一家铺子里给父亲买了枚钻石戒指。起码我当时以为那是钻石。没准儿他们蒙了我，那会儿我年纪还小。」

===Original===
“You have had a hard life, dear.”

===Chinese===
「你这一辈子过得够苦的，亲爱的。」

===Original===
“Yes, but hasn’t it been a lark! I’ve loved every minute of it. Besides, you can’t call me really one of the submerged tenth. Uncle Thomas left me a hundred and fifty pounds a year, and mercifully I’m not allowed to touch the capital. If only there were no hats or safety bets in the world, I should be smugly opulent.﻿ ﻿… But I mustn’t keep you any longer, Clarkie dear. I expect the waiting-room is full of dukes who want cooks and cooks who want dukes, all fidgeting and wondering how much longer you’re going to keep them. Goodbye, darling.”

===Chinese===
「是苦，可多有意思呀！我每一分钟都爱极了。再说，你也不能真把我算作人间最底层的那十分之一。托马斯叔叔留给我一年一百五十英镑，而且万幸，本金不许我动。这世上要是没有帽子要买、没有保险注要下，我早就可以心满意足地过阔日子了。……不过，亲爱的小克拉克，我可不能再占用你的工夫了。我看候客室里这会儿准坐满了要找厨子的公爵和要找公爵的厨子，个个如坐针毡，掂量着你还要把他们晾多久。再见了，亲爱的。」

===Original===
And, having kissed Miss Clarkson fondly and straightened her hat, which the other’s motherly embrace had disarranged, Eve left the room.

===Chinese===
末了，伊芙亲热地吻过克拉克森小姐，又扶正帽子——方才对方慈母般的一抱把它蹭歪了——便走出房去。

## IV Painful Scene at the Drones Club / 蜂子俱乐部的难堪一幕

===Original===
Meanwhile, at the Drones Club, a rather painful scene had been taking place. Psmith, regaining the shelter of the building, had made his way to the washroom, where, having studied his features with interest for a moment in the mirror, he smoothed his hair, which the rain had somewhat disordered, and brushed his clothes with extreme care. He then went to the cloakroom for his hat. The attendant regarded him as he entered with the air of one whose mind is not wholly at rest.

===Chinese===
与此同时，在蜂子俱乐部，一幕颇为难堪的场面刚刚上演。皮史密斯重新回到这座建筑的庇护之下，便径直去了盥洗室，对着镜子饶有兴味地端详了片刻自己的容貌，抹平被雨水搅得略见凌乱的头发，又极为细致地掸了掸衣服。随后他去衣帽间取帽子。他走进来时，侍者望着他，那副神情，活像一个内心不得全然安宁的人。

===Original===
“Mr. Walderwick was in here a moment ago, sir,” said the attendant.

===Chinese===
“沃尔德威克（Walderwick）先生刚才来过这儿，先生。”侍者说。

===Original===
“Yes?” said Psmith, mildly interested. “An energetic, bustling soul, Comrade Walderwick. Always somewhere. Now here, now there.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”皮史密斯略感兴味地说。“沃尔德威克同志是个精力充沛、风风火火的灵魂。总归是在什么地方。忽而在此，忽而在彼。”

===Original===
“Asking about his umbrella, he was,” pursued the attendant with a touch of coldness.

===Chinese===
“人家是来打听他那把伞的。”侍者带着一丝冷淡继续说。

===Original===
“Indeed? Asking about his umbrella, eh?”

===Chinese===
“哦？打听他的伞，嗯？”

===Original===
“Made a great fuss about it, sir, he did.”

===Chinese===
“他可为这事大惊小怪了一通呢，先生，可不。”

===Original===
“And rightly,” said Psmith with approval. “The good man loves his umbrella.”

===Chinese===
“这就对了，”皮史密斯赞许道。“好人爱自己的伞嘛。”

===Original===
“Of course I had to tell him that you had took it, sir.”

===Chinese===
“我当然只好告诉他，伞是您拿走的，先生。”

===Original===
“I would not have it otherwise,” assented Psmith heartily. “I like this spirit of candour. There must be no reservations, no subterfuges between you and Comrade Walderwick. Let all be open and aboveboard.”

===Chinese===
“我正希望如此，别无他求，”皮史密斯热忱地附和道。“我喜欢这种坦率的精神。您和沃尔德威克同志之间，绝不该有任何保留、任何遁词。让一切都开诚布公、光明磊落。”

===Original===
“He seemed very put out, sir. He went off to find you.”

===Chinese===
“他看样子气得不轻，先生。他找您去了。”

===Original===
“I am always glad of a chat with Comrade Walderwick,” said Psmith. “Always.”

===Chinese===
“同沃尔德威克同志一叙，我随时都是乐意的，”皮史密斯说。“随时。”

===Original===
He left the cloakroom and made for the hall, where he desired the porter to procure him a cab. This having drawn up in front of the club, he descended the steps and was about to enter it, when there was a hoarse cry in his rear, and through the front door there came bounding a pinkly indignant youth, who called loudly:

===Chinese===
他离开衣帽间，向大厅走去，吩咐门房替他叫一辆车。车在俱乐部门前停稳，他走下台阶，正要跨进车厢，忽听得身后一声嘶哑的喊叫，一个怒气冲冲、涨红着粉红脸的青年从前门里蹦了出来，大声喊道：

===Original===
“Here! Hi! Smith! Dash it!”

===Chinese===
“喂！嘿！史密斯！搞什么！”

===Original===
Psmith climbed into the cab and gazed benevolently out at the newcomer.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯跨进车里，慈和地望着这位来者。

===Original===
“Ah, Comrade Walderwick!” he said. “What have we on our mind?”

===Chinese===
“啊，沃尔德威克同志！”他说。“我们这是有什么心事呀？”

===Original===
“Where’s my umbrella?” demanded the pink one. “The cloakroom waiter says you took my umbrella. I mean, a joke’s a joke, but that was a dashed good umbrella.”

===Chinese===
“我的伞呢？”粉红脸的那位质问道。“衣帽间侍者说是你拿了我的伞。我是说，玩笑归玩笑，那可是一把好得没话说的伞啊。”

===Original===
“It was, indeed,” Psmith agreed cordially. “It may be of interest to you to know that I selected it as the only possible one from among a number of competitors. I fear this club is becoming very mixed, Comrade Walderwick. You with your pure mind would hardly believe the rottenness of some of the umbrellas I inspected in the cloakroom.”

===Chinese===
“的确如此，”皮史密斯由衷地表示同意。“您或许有兴趣知道，我是在一批竞争者当中把它遴选出来的——唯它一个堪当此任。依我看，这家俱乐部正变得越来越杂，沃尔德威克同志。以您那颗纯净的心灵，恐怕很难相信我在衣帽间里检阅过的某些雨伞是何等败坏。”

===Original===
“Where is it?”

===Chinese===
“它在哪儿？”

===Original===
“The cloakroom? You turn to the left as you go in at the main entrance and﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“衣帽间？从正门进去往左拐就……”

===Original===
“My umbrella, dash it! Where’s my umbrella?”

===Chinese===
“我的伞，搞什么！我的伞在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Ah, there,” said Psmith, and there was a touch of manly regret in his voice, “you have me. I gave it to a young lady in the street. Where she is at the present moment I could not say.”

===Chinese===
“啊，这一问，”皮史密斯说，声音里带着一丝男子汉的憾意，“您可算问着了。我把它送给街上的一位小姐了。她此刻身在何处，我无从奉告。”

===Original===
The pink youth tottered slightly.

===Chinese===
粉红青年身子微微一晃。

===Original===
“You gave my umbrella to a girl?”

===Chinese===
“你把我的伞送给了一个姑娘？”

===Original===
“A very loose way of describing her. You would not speak of her in that light fashion if you had seen her. Comrade Walderwick, she was wonderful! I am a plain, blunt, rugged man, above the softer emotions as a general thing, but I frankly confess that she stirred a chord in me which is not often stirred. She thrilled my battered old heart, Comrade Walderwick. There is no other word. Thrilled it!”

===Chinese===
“这个说法未免太随便了。您若见过她，绝不会用这般轻飘飘的口吻谈论她。沃尔德威克同志，她妙极了！我是个朴实、直率、粗犷的人，向来超然于种种柔情之外，但我坦白承认，她拨动了我心中一根不常被拨动的弦。她让我这颗饱经磨砺的老心为之一颤，沃尔德威克同志。舍此别无一词。为之一颤！”

===Original===
“But, dash it!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“可是，搞什么！……”

===Original===
Psmith reached out a long arm and laid his hand paternally on the other’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯伸出长长的手臂，像老父亲似的把手搭在对方肩上。

===Original===
“Be brave, Comrade Walderwick!” he said. “Face this thing like a man! I am sorry to have been the means of depriving you of an excellent umbrella, but as you will readily understand I had no alternative. It was raining. She was over there, crouched despairingly beneath the awning of that shop. She wanted to be elsewhere, but the moisture lay in wait to damage her hat. What could I do? What could any man worthy of the name do but go down to the cloakroom and pinch the best umbrella in sight and take it to her? Yours was easily the best. There was absolutely no comparison. I gave it to her, and she has gone off with it, happy once more. This explanation,” said Psmith, “will, I am sure, sensibly diminish your natural chagrin. You have lost your umbrella, Comrade Walderwick, but in what a cause! In what a cause, Comrade Walderwick! You are now entitled to rank with Sir Philip Sidney and Sir Walter Raleigh. The latter is perhaps the closer historical parallel. He spread his cloak to keep a queen from wetting her feet. You﻿—by proxy﻿—yielded up your umbrella to save a girl’s hat. Posterity will be proud of you, Comrade Walderwick. I shall be vastly surprised if you do not go down in legend and song. Children in ages to come will cluster about their grandfather’s knees, saying, ‘Tell us how the great Walderwick lost his umbrella, grandpapa!’ And he will tell them, and they will rise from the recital better, deeper, broader children.﻿ ﻿… But now, as I see that the driver has started his meter, I fear I must conclude this little chat﻿—which I, for one, have heartily enjoyed. Drive on,” he said, leaning out of the window. “I want to go to Ada Clarkson’s International Employment Bureau in Shaftesbury Avenue.”

===Chinese===
“勇敢些，沃尔德威克同志！”他说。“要像个男子汉那样面对这件事！我很抱歉，自己竟成了令您痛失一把上好雨伞的因由，但您不难理解，我当时别无选择。天正下着雨。她就在街对面，绝望地蜷缩在那家商店的雨棚底下。她想到别处去，可潮气正埋伏在旁，专等毁坏她的帽子。我能怎么办呢？凡配得上‘男子汉’这个名号的人，除了下到衣帽间，把眼前最好的一把伞顺手牵走，再给她送去之外，还能怎么办呢？您那把无疑是最好的，完全无从比较。我把它给了她，她带着它走了，重新快活起来。这番解释，”皮史密斯说，“我相信定能切实纾解您自然而生的懊恼。您失去了您的伞，沃尔德威克同志，可这是为了何等的事业！何等的事业啊，沃尔德威克同志！您如今已有资格与菲利普·西德尼爵士和沃尔特·雷利爵士并肩而立了。后一位或许是更贴切的历史对照：他铺开斗篷，免得一位王后打湿了双脚；您——经由他人代劳——献出了雨伞，保全了一位姑娘的帽子。后世将以您为荣，沃尔德威克同志。您若不能载入传说与歌谣，我将大为诧异。来日方长的岁月里，孩子们会围坐在祖父的膝边，说：‘给我们讲讲伟大的沃尔德威克是怎么丢了伞的吧，爷爷！’他会讲给他们听，而他们听完这段讲述起身时，已然是更好、更深沉、更宽广的孩子了。……不过现在，鉴于我看见司机已经打开了计价器，恐怕我必须结束这场小小的谈话了——就我个人而言，这场谈话实在是令人由衷畅快。开车吧。”他探出车窗说。“我要去沙夫茨伯里大道，艾达·克拉克森（Ada Clarkson）国际职业介绍所。”

===Original===
The cab moved off. The Hon. Hugo Walderwick, after one passionate glance in its wake, realised that he was getting wet and went back into the club.

===Chinese===
车子开动了。荣誉头衔的雨果·沃尔德威克（Hugo Walderwick）朝车影投去饱含激情的一瞥，随即发觉自己正被雨淋着，便转身回俱乐部去了。

===Original===
---

Arriving at the address named, Psmith paid his cab and, having mounted the stairs, delicately knuckled the ground-glass window of Enquiries.

===Chinese===
到了所说的地址，皮史密斯付了车钱，上楼之后，用指节轻轻叩了叩问询处的磨砂玻璃窗。

===Original===
“My dear Miss Clarkson,” he began in an affable voice, the instant the window had shot up, “if you can spare me a few moments of your valuable time﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的克拉克森小姐，”窗子弹起的一瞬间，他便用和蔼可亲的声音开了腔，“您若能从宝贵的时间里赏我几分钟……”

===Original===
“Miss Clarkson’s engaged.”

===Chinese===
“克拉克森小姐正忙着呢。”

===Original===
Psmith scrutinised her gravely through his monocle.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯透过单片眼镜，郑重其事地端详着她。

===Original===
“Aren’t *you* Miss Clarkson?”

===Chinese===
“难道*您*不是克拉克森小姐？”

===Original===
Enquiries said she was not.

===Chinese===
问询处表示她不是。

===Original===
“Then,” said Psmith, “there has been a misunderstanding, for which,” he added cordially, “I am to blame. Perhaps I could see her anon? You will find me in the waiting-room when required.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”皮史密斯说，“这里头有了一场误会，而这场误会，”他诚恳地补充道，“咎在于我。或许我稍后能见她一面？需要时，您可在等候室寻到我。”

===Original===
He went into the waiting-room, and, having picked up a magazine from the table, settled down to read a story in *The Girl’s Pet*﻿—the January number of the year 1919, for Employment Agencies, like dentists, prefer their literature of a matured vintage. He was absorbed in this when Eve came out of the private office.

===Chinese===
他走进等候室，从桌上拿起一本杂志，安顿下来读《少女的宠儿》（The Girl’s Pet）上的一篇小说——那是 1919 年的 1 月号，因为职业介绍所跟牙医一样，都偏爱陈年佳酿般的读物。伊芙从里面的私人办公室走出来时，他正读得入神。

## V Psmith Applies for Employment / 皮史密斯求职

===Original===

Psmith rose courteously as she entered.

“My dear Miss Clarkson,” he said, “if you can spare me a moment of your valuable time﻿ ﻿…”

“Good gracious!” said Eve. “How extraordinary!”

“A singular coincidence,” agreed Psmith.

===Chinese===

她进门时，皮史密斯（Psmith）彬彬有礼地欠身而起。

“亲爱的克拉克森小姐（Miss Clarkson），”他说，“倘蒙拨冗，惠赐片刻……”

“天哪！”伊芙（Eve）说，“这也太巧了！”

“绝非寻常的巧合。”皮史密斯深表赞同。

===Original===

“You never gave me time to thank you for the umbrella,” said Eve reproachfully. “You must have thought me awfully rude. But you took my breath away.”

“My dear Miss Clarkson, please do not﻿ ﻿…”

“Why do you keep calling me that?”

“Aren’t *you* Miss Clarkson either?”

===Chinese===

“为了那把伞，你一直没给我道谢的机会，”伊芙微嗔道，“你准以为我失礼透了吧。可你那一下真把我惊得说不出话来。”

“亲爱的克拉克森小姐，请千万别……”

“你怎么一口一个这个称呼？”

“怎么，*你*也不是克拉克森小姐？”

===Original===

“Of course I’m not.”

“Then,” said Psmith, “I must start my quest all over again. These constant checks are trying to an ardent spirit. Perhaps you are a young bride come to engage her first cook?”

“No. I’m not married.”

“Good!”

Eve found his relieved thankfulness a little embarrassing. In the momentary pause which followed his remark, Enquiries entered alertly.

===Chinese===

“我当然不是。”

“那么，”皮史密斯说，“在下只得从头再访。这般接二连三的顿挫，最是磨折一颗热切的心。莫非您是位新嫁娘，来为府上延聘第一任厨娘？”

“不是。我还没结婚。”

“太好了！”

他那副如释重负、感恩戴德的模样，让伊芙颇觉窘迫。他的话音落下，片刻冷场之间，问询处的姑娘脚步轻快地走了进来。

===Original===

“Miss Clarkson will see you now, sir.”

“Leave us,” said Psmith with a wave of his hand. “We would be alone.”

Enquiries stared; then, awed by his manner and general appearance of magnificence, withdrew.

“I suppose really,” said Eve, toying with the umbrella, “I ought to give this back to you.” She glanced at the dripping window. “But it *is* raining rather hard, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===

“克拉克森小姐现在可以见您了，先生。”

“你且退下，”皮史密斯一挥手，“我们要单独叙话。”

问询处的姑娘直愣愣地望着他；随即被他那气度与通身的华贵震住，退了出去。

“说实在的，”伊芙手里摆弄着那把伞，“我本该把它还你才对。”她朝雨水直淌的窗户瞥了一眼。“不过这雨*确实*下得不小，你说呢？”

===Original===

“Like the dickens,” assented Psmith.

“Then would you mind very much if I kept it till this evening?”

“Please do.”

“Thanks ever so much. I will send it back to you tonight if you will give me the name and address.”

Psmith waved his hand deprecatingly.

===Chinese===

“下得可凶啦。”皮史密斯深然其说。

“那么，借我撑到今晚，你不至于太介意吧？”

“请便。”

“真是多谢了。请把姓名住址告诉我，今晚我就把伞给你送回来。”

皮史密斯连连摆手，谦逊不受。

===Original===

“No, no. If it is of any use to you, I hope that you will look on it as a present.”

“A present!”

“A gift,” explained Psmith.

“But I really can’t go about accepting expensive umbrellas from people. Where shall I send it?”

“If you insist, you may send it to the Hon. Hugo Walderwick, Drones Club, Dover Street. But it really isn’t necessary.”

===Chinese===

“不，不。这伞于您既有一用，就请您把它当作一件赠礼吧。”

“赠礼！”

“礼物，”皮史密斯解释道。

“可我实在不能到处收人家贵重的雨伞。该寄到哪儿去？”

“您若执意要还，就寄给多佛街蜂子俱乐部（Drones Club）的雨果·沃尔德威克少爷（the Hon. Hugo Walderwick）吧。不过实在不必。”

===Original===

“I won’t forget. And thank you very much, Mr. Walderwick.”

“Why do you call me that?”

“Well, you said﻿ ﻿…”

“Ah, I see. A slight confusion of ideas. No, I am not Mr. Walderwick. And between ourselves I should hate to be. His is a very C3 intelligence. Comrade Walderwick is merely the man to whom the umbrella belongs.”

Eve’s eyes opened wide.

===Chinese===

“我忘不了的。多谢您啦，沃尔德威克先生。”

“您缘何这般称呼我？”

“咦，您方才不是说……”

“啊，我明白了。一点小小的概念混淆。不，在下并非沃尔德威克先生。而且说句咱们之间的私房话，叫我做他，我还千般不情愿。他那副智力，顶够上 C3 等（陆军体格分级里最末一档的成色）。沃尔德威克同志不过是这把伞的原主罢了。”

伊芙的眼睛瞪圆了。

===Original===

“Do you mean to say you gave me somebody else’s umbrella?”

“I had unfortunately omitted to bring my own out with me this morning.”

“I never heard of such a thing!”

“Merely practical Socialism. Other people are content to talk about the Redistribution of Property. I go out and do it.”

===Chinese===

“你是说，你把别人的伞给了我？”

“不巧得很，今晨出门时，在下把自己那把遗忘在了寓所。”

“这种事我还闻所未闻！”

“区区实践派的社会主义而已。旁人满足于空谈‘财产重新分配’，在下却出门躬行。”

===Original===

“But won’t he be awfully angry when he finds out it has gone?”

“He *has* found out. And it was pretty to see his delight. I explained the circumstances, and he was charmed to have been of service to you.”

The door opened again, and this time it was Miss Clarkson in person who entered. She had found Enquiries’ statement over the speaking-tube rambling and unsatisfactory, and had come to investigate for herself the reason why the machinery of the office was being held up.

===Chinese===

“可等他发觉伞没了，岂不是要暴跳如雷？”

“他*已经*发觉了。他那欣喜之态，煞是可观。我将来龙去脉一说，能为姑娘效力，他表示喜不自胜。”

门再度打开，这回进来的是克拉克森小姐本人。问询处姑娘通过传话筒所作的那番汇报，在她听来语焉不详、难惬人意，她便亲自前来查究，所中这台公务机器究竟因何搁浅。

===Original===

“Oh, I must go,” said Eve, as she saw her. “I’m interrupting your business.”

“I’m so glad you’re still here, dear,” said Miss Clarkson. “I have just been looking over my files, and I see that there *is* one vacancy. For a nurse,” said Miss Clarkson with a touch of the apologetic in her voice.

“Oh, no, that’s all right,” said Eve. “I don’t really need anything. But thanks ever so much for bothering.”

She smiled affectionately upon the proprietress, bestowed another smile upon Psmith as he opened the door for her, and went out. Psmith turned away from the door with a thoughtful look upon his face.

===Chinese===

“哎呀，我得走了。”伊芙一见她便说，“我搅扰你们办公了。”

“你还在这里，我真高兴，亲爱的，”克拉克森小姐说，“我方才查阅了档案，确实有一处空缺。护士一职。”克拉克森小姐补了一句，语气里带着一丝歉意。

“哦，不必了，没关系，”伊芙说，“我其实什么都不缺。不过多谢你这般费心。”

她朝女所长亲亲热热地一笑；皮史密斯为她开门时，又赏了他一笑，这才出门去。皮史密斯从门边转过身来，脸上若有所思。

===Original===

“Is that young lady a nurse?” he asked.

“Do you want a nurse?” inquired Miss Clarkson, at once the woman of business.

“I want that nurse,” said Psmith with conviction.

“She is a delightful girl,” said Miss Clarkson with enthusiasm. “There is no one in whom I would feel more confidence in recommending to a position. She is a Miss Halliday, the daughter of a very clever but erratic writer, who died some years ago. I can speak with particular knowledge of Miss Halliday, for I was for many years an assistant mistress at Wayland House, where she was at school. She is a charming, warmhearted, impulsive girl.﻿ ﻿… But you will hardly want to hear all this.”

===Chinese===

“那位小姐是位护士？”他问道。

“您想聘护士？”克拉克森小姐诘问，霎时又成了干练的女掌柜。

“在下想聘的，正是那位护士。”皮史密斯言之凿凿。

“她是个惹人喜爱的姑娘，”克拉克森小姐满腔热忱地说，“若荐人上任，再没有人比我更放心。她姓哈利迪（Halliday），父亲是位才华横溢却行止无定的作家，数年前已经过世。谈到哈利迪小姐，我尤其知根知底，因为我曾在韦兰德女子学校（Wayland House）当了多年助理教师，她就是在那儿念的书。她是个迷人的姑娘，热心肠，直性子，一点就着。……不过这些您大概懒得听吧。”

===Original===

“On the contrary,” said Psmith, “I could listen for hours. You have stumbled upon my favourite subject.”

Miss Clarkson eyed him a little doubtfully, and decided that it would be best to reintroduce the business theme.

“Perhaps, when you say you are looking for a nurse, you mean you need a hospital nurse?”

===Chinese===

“恰恰相反，”皮史密斯说，“听上几个钟头也不倦。您恰好误打误撞，撞进了在下最心爱的话题。”

克拉克森小姐略带疑虑地端详他，决定还是把话题重新引回生意主题为妙。

“或许，您说想找护士，指的是需要一位医院看护？”

===Original===

“My friends have sometimes suggested it.”

“Miss Halliday’s greatest experience has, of course, been as a governess.”

“A governess is just as good,” said Psmith agreeably.

Miss Clarkson began to be conscious of a sensation of being out of her depth.

===Chinese===

“敝友之中，倒也不时有人作此建议。”

“哈利迪小姐资历最深的，自然当数家庭教师一职。”

“家庭教师亦无不可。”皮史密斯欣然首肯。

克拉克森小姐渐渐体味到一种力不能及的惶惑之感。

===Original===

“How old are your children, sir?” she asked.

“I fear,” said Psmith, “you are peeping into Volume Two. This romance has only just started.”

“I am afraid,” said Miss Clarkson, now completely fogged, “I do not quite understand. What exactly are you looking for?”

Psmith flicked a speck of fluff from his coat-sleeve.

===Chinese===

“您的孩子们年方几何，先生？”她问。

“恕我直言，”皮史密斯说，“您这是窥见第二卷了。这部罗曼史方才启幕。”

“恐怕，”克拉克森小姐说——此刻她已是彻底如坠云雾——“我不甚明了。您要找的究竟是什么？”

皮史密斯从衣袖上弹去一星绒絮。

===Original===

“A job,” he said.

“A job!” echoed Miss Clarkson, her voice breaking in an amazed squeak.

Psmith raised his eyebrows.

“You seem surprised. Isn’t this a job emporium?”

===Chinese===

“差事。”他说。

“差事！”克拉克森小姐应声复述，惊得嗓音劈作一声尖哨。

皮史密斯扬起双眉。

“您似乎颇为诧异。此地莫非不是一座差事大卖场？”

===Original===

“This *is* an Employment Bureau,” admitted Miss Clarkson.

“I knew it, I knew it,” said Psmith. “Something seemed to tell me. Possibly it was the legend ‘Employment Bureau’ over the door. And those framed testimonials would convince the most sceptical. Yes, Miss Clarkson, I want a job, and I feel somehow that you are the woman to find it for me. I have inserted an advertisement in the papers, expressing my readiness to undertake any form of employment, but I have since begun to wonder if after all this will lead to wealth and fame. At any rate, it is wise to attack the great world from another angle as well, so I come to you.”

“But you must excuse me if I remark that this application of yours strikes me as most extraordinary.”

===Chinese===

“这里*确实*是一家职业介绍所，”克拉克森小姐承认道。

“我就知道，我就知道，”皮史密斯说，“冥冥之中似有神启。或许正是门楣上那块‘职业介绍所’的招牌起了作用。再者，那几份镶了框的荐书，最能令疑心最重的人心悦诚服。正是，克拉克森小姐，我要找一份差事，而且不知怎的，我总觉得替我觅得此差的人选，舍您其谁。我已在报端刊出广告，申明本人甘愿承担任何形式的职业；只是此后我又渐生疑虑，不知此路终究能否通向财富与声名。无论如何，再从另一个角度向大千世界发动攻势，总归是明智之举，故而在下这就登门造访。”

“不过恕我直言，您这番求职之举，依我看实属异乎寻常。”

===Original===

“Why? I am young, active, and extremely broke.”

“But your﻿—er﻿—your clothes﻿ ﻿…”

Psmith squinted, not without complacency, down a faultlessly fitting waistcoat, and flicked another speck of dust off his sleeve.

===Chinese===

“何以见得？在下年轻，干练，而且穷得叮当响。”

“可是您那——呃——您这一身衣裳……”

皮史密斯眯起眼睛，不无自得地俯视那件剪裁得无可挑剔的马甲，又从袖上弹去一粒微尘。

===Original===

“You consider me well dressed?” he said. “You find me natty? Well, well, perhaps you are right, perhaps you are right. But consider, Miss Clarkson. If one expects to find employment in these days of strenuous competition, one must be neatly and decently clad. Employers look askance at a baggy trouser-leg. A zippy waistcoat is more to them than an honest heart. This beautiful crease was obtained with the aid of the mattress upon which I tossed feverishly last night in my attic room.”

“I can’t take you seriously.”

===Chinese===

“您认为我衣着考究？”他说，“您觉得我入时？好好，或许您说得对，或许您说得对。可是您想想，克拉克森小姐，如今时世，竞争惨烈，要想谋得职业，就必须穿得齐整体面。雇主们见了宽松发胖的裤腿便侧目而视；一件神气的马甲，在他们眼里比一颗诚实的心更为紧要。这条漂亮的裤线，得来全靠那张床垫——昨夜在阁楼斗室里，我就在它上面辗转反侧、心潮澎湃，裤线便这般压了出来。”

“我没法把您的话当真。”

===Original===

“Oh, don’t say that, please.”

“You really want me to find you work?”

“I prefer the term ‘employment.’ ”

Miss Clarkson produced a notebook.

===Chinese===

“哎，请千万别这么说。”

“您当真要我替您寻份差事？”

“我更属意‘职业’一词。”

克拉克森小姐取出一册记事簿。

===Original===

“If you are really not making this application just as a joke﻿ ﻿…”

“I assure you, no. My entire capital consists, in specie, of about ten pounds.”

“Then perhaps you will tell me your name.”

“Ah! Things are beginning to move. The name is Psmith. P-smith. The *p* is silent.”

===Chinese===

“倘若您此番求职，当真并非戏言……”

“我向您保证，绝非戏言。本人全部资产，以现款计，约莫十英镑。”

“那么，或许您愿以尊姓大名见告。”

“啊！事机开始运转了。敝姓皮史密斯。P-smith。打头的 *p* 不发音。”

===Original===

“Psmith?”

“Psmith.”

Miss Clarkson brooded over this for a moment in almost pained silence, then recovered her slipping grip of affairs.

“I think,” she said, “you had better give me a few particulars about yourself.”

===Chinese===

“皮史密斯？”

“皮史密斯。”

克拉克森小姐默然玩味了片刻，神情近乎痛苦，随后重新攥牢了正自指间滑脱的公务缰绳。

“我想，”她说，“您最好还是向我略述几项有关您本人的详情。”

===Original===

“There is nothing I should like better,” responded Psmith warmly. “I am always ready﻿—I may say eager﻿—to tell people the story of my life, but in this rushing age I get little encouragement. Let us start at the beginning. My infancy. When I was but a babe, my eldest sister was bribed with sixpence an hour by my nurse to keep an eye on me and see that I did not raise Cain. At the end of the first day she struck for a shilling, and got it. We now pass to my boyhood. At an early age I was sent to Eton, everybody predicting a bright career for me. Those were happy days, Miss Clarkson. A merry, laughing lad with curly hair and a sunny smile, it is not too much to say that I was the pet of the place. The old cloisters.﻿ ﻿… But I am boring you. I can see it in your eye.”

“No, no,” protested Miss Clarkson. “But what I meant was﻿ ﻿… I thought you might have had some experience in some particular line of﻿ ﻿… In fact, what sort of work﻿ ﻿… ?”

===Chinese===

“正中下怀，求之不得，”皮史密斯热忱应道，“向世人讲述生平事迹，我一向奉陪——不妨说求知若渴——只恨这仓促匆忙的世代，少有人肯加以鼓励。那就从头说起。先叙襁褓。当年我尚在乳臭，乳母以每小时六便士的报酬贿赂我大姐，请她看住我，勿使大闹天宫。头一天日终，她便罢工加价，非一先令不可，而且得偿所愿。次叙少年。年方幼冲，我便被送入伊顿公学，人人皆预言我前程似锦。那是何等快活的日子啊，克拉克森小姐。一个笑声朗朗的孩童，鬈发满头，笑靥如晴日，说我是彼处之宠儿，亦不为过。古老的回廊。……可是我把您讲烦了。您眼里写得明明白白。”

“不烦，不烦，”克拉克森小姐连忙辩白，“可我的意思是……我以为您或许在某个特定行当里有过些经验……说实在的，您想找的是哪一类工作……？”

===Original===

“Employment.”

“What sort of employment do you require?”

“Broadly speaking,” said Psmith, “any reasonably salaried position that has nothing to do with fish.”

“Fish!” quavered Miss Clarkson, slipping again. “Why fish?”

===Chinese===

“职业。”

“您需要的是哪一类职业？”

“概括地说，”皮史密斯道，“凡薪俸尚称合理、且与鱼绝无瓜葛的职位，一体欢迎。”

“鱼！”克拉克森小姐颤声道，又一次晕了头。“为何是鱼？”

===Original===

“Because, Miss Clarkson, the fish trade was until this morning my walk in life, and my soul has sickened of it.”

“You are in the *fish* trade?” squeaked Miss Clarkson, with an amazed glance at the knife-like crease in his trousers.

“These are not my working clothes,” said Psmith, following and interpreting her glance. “Yes, owing to a financial upheaval in my branch of the family, I was until this morning at the beck and call of an uncle who unfortunately happens to be a Mackerel Monarch or a Sardine Sultan, or whatever these merchant princes are called who rule the fish market. He insisted on my going into the business to learn it from the bottom up, thinking, no doubt, that I would follow in his footsteps and eventually work my way to the position of a Whitebait Wizard. Alas! he was too sanguine. It was not to be,” said Psmith solemnly, fixing an owl-like gaze on Miss Clarkson through his eyeglass.

“No?” said Miss Clarkson.

===Chinese===

“因为，克拉克森小姐，鱼行买卖直到今晨还是本人的安身立命之业，而我的灵魂早已对它深恶痛绝。”

“您是做*鱼*行生意的？”克拉克森小姐失声叫道，一面惊愕地瞥了瞥他裤腿上刀锋似的挺括裤线。

“这并非我的工作服，”皮史密斯说，一面追随她的目光，并参透了其中意味。“是这样的：敝家族这一支突遭财务剧变，于是直到今晨，我都在听凭一位叔父呼来喝去。不巧此公恰是一位鲭鱼大王（Mackerel Monarch），抑或沙丁鱼苏丹（Sardine Sultan），总之便是那类称霸鱼市的商业帝王。他坚持要我入行学生意，自底层拾级而上，无疑指望我亦步亦趋，终有一日攀上银鱼大法师（Whitebait Wizard）的尊位。呜呼！他过于乐观了。天不遂人愿。”皮史密斯庄重地说，隔着单片眼镜，将一副猫头鹰式的凝视定在克拉克森小姐脸上。

“没有吗？”克拉克森小姐说。

===Original===

“No. Last night I was obliged to inform him that the fish business was all right, but it wouldn’t do, and that I proposed to sever my connection with the firm for ever. I may say at once that there ensued something in the nature of a family earthquake. Hard words,” sighed Psmith. “Black looks. Unseemly wrangle. And the upshot of it all was that my uncle washed his hands of me and drove me forth into the great world. Hence my anxiety to find employment. My uncle has definitely withdrawn his countenance from me, Miss Clarkson.”

“Dear, dear!” murmured the proprietress sympathetically.

===Chinese===

“没有。昨夜我不得不向他进言：鱼行生意固然无可指摘，但于我并不相宜，并表明本人有意与商号永绝干系。可以直说，随之而来的是一场不啻家族地震的事变。恶言，”皮史密斯喟叹道，“冷面。有失体统的口角。而这一切的结局是，叔父对我撒手不管，将我逐入茫茫大千世界。故而本人急欲谋求职业。叔父已断然收回了对我的眷顾，克拉克森小姐。”

“哎呀，哎呀！”女所长同情地喃喃道。

===Original===

“Yes. He is a hard man, and he judges his fellows solely by their devotion to fish. I never in my life met a man so wrapped up in a subject. For years he has been practically a monomaniac on the subject of fish. So much so that he actually looks like one. It is as if he had taken one of those autosuggestion courses and had kept saying to himself, ‘Every day, in every way, I grow more and more like a fish.’ His closest friends can hardly tell now whether he more nearly resembles a halibut or a cod.﻿ ﻿… But I am boring you again with this family gossip?”

He eyed Miss Clarkson with such a sudden and penetrating glance that she started nervously.

===Chinese===
