伊芙的恼火见涨。她一直有种说不清的恐惧：长此以往，哪天他再求一次，兴许她会错口应一声「好」。她但愿科学界能想个法子，叫他一劳永逸地断念。情急之下，她想起一条还没用过的论路。

===Original===
“It’s so absurd, Freddie,” she said. “Really, it is. Apart from the fact that I don’t want to marry you, how can you marry anyone﻿—anyone, I mean, who hasn’t plenty of money?”

===Chinese===
「这事荒唐，弗雷迪，」她说。「真的，太荒唐了。且不说我不想嫁你；抛开这个不谈，你怎么能娶任何人——我是说，任何没有大笔进项的人？」

===Original===
“Wouldn’t dream of marrying for money.”

===Chinese===
「做梦也不会为钱结婚。」

===Original===
“No, of course not, but﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「当然不会，可是……」

===Original===
“Cupid,” said Freddie woodenly, “pines and sickens in a gilded cage.”

===Chinese===
「丘比特，」弗雷迪木然背诵，「在镀金的笼中憔悴枯萎。」

===Original===
Eve had not expected to be surprised by anything her companion might say, it being her experience that he possessed a vocabulary of about forty-three words and a sum-total of ideas that hardly ran into two figures; but this poetic remark took her back.

===Chinese===
伊芙原以为同伴说什么都不足以使她吃惊了——凭她的经验，他的词汇量约莫四十三个，主意加起来凑不满两位数——可这句诗意的话把她打了个措手不及。

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
Freddie repeated the observation. When it had been flashed on the screen as a spoken subtitle in the six-reel wonder film, “Love or Mammon” (Beatrice Comely and Brian Fraser), he had approved and made a note of it.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪复述了一遍这句名言。当它作为台词字幕闪现在六本部的奇情巨片《爱情还是财富》（比阿特丽斯·康利、布莱恩·弗雷泽主演）的银幕上时，他激赏之余抄录了下来。

===Original===
“Oh!” said Eve, and was silent. As Miss Peavey would have put it, it held her for a while. “What I meant,” she went on after a moment, “was that you can’t possibly marry a girl without money unless you’ve some money of your own.”

===Chinese===
「哦！」伊芙说，一时无言。用皮维小姐的话说，这话把她「定」住了一阵。「我的意思是，」她片刻后接下去，「你自己没钱的话，就绝不能娶没钱的姑娘。」

===Original===
“I say, dash it!” A strange note of jubilation had come into the wooer’s voice. “I say, is that really all that stands between us? Because﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我说，嘿！」求偶人的嗓音里冒出一声异样的欢腾。「我说，当真就差这一层了？因为……」

===Original===
“No, it isn’t!”

===Chinese===
「不，不是！」

===Original===
“Because, look here, I’m going to have quite a good deal of money at any moment. It’s more or less of a secret, you know﻿—in fact a pretty deadish secret﻿—so keep it dark, but Uncle Joe is going to give me a couple of thousand quid. He promised me. Two thousand of the crispest. Absolutely!”

===Chinese===
「因为，你听我说，我不定哪会儿就有一大笔钱了。这事多少算个秘密，你知道——实际上瞒得挺死——所以别外传，乔姨父要给我两千镑。他许了愿的。两千张最脆的票子。妥妥的！」

===Original===
“Uncle Joe?”

===Chinese===
「乔姨父？」

===Original===
“*You* know. Old Keeble. He’s going to give me a couple of thousand quid, and then I’m going to buy a partnership in a bookie’s business and simply coin money. Stands to reason, I mean. You can’t help making your bally fortune. Look at all the mugs who are losing money all the time at the races. It’s the bookies that get the stuff. A pal of mine who was up at Oxford with me is in a bookie’s office, and they’re going to let me in if I﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「*你*知道的呀。老基布尔。他要给我两千镑，我就拿去入伙赛马庄家的生意，坐着数钱。想也明白，我是说。干这行想不发财都难。瞧瞧赛马场上那些整天输钱的冤大头。钱全进了庄家腰包。我牛津的一个哥们儿在庄家的事务所里做事，他们说只要我……」

===Original===
The momentous nature of his information had caused Eve to deviate now from her policy of keeping her eyes off Freddie when in emotional vein. And, if she had desired to check his lecture on finance, she could have chosen no better method than to look at him; for, meeting her gaze, Freddie immediately lost the thread of his discourse and stood yammering. A direct hit from Eve’s eyes always affected him in this way.

===Chinese===
这条消息的分量之重，使伊芙破了她在弗雷迪情绪激动时目不斜视的方针。倘若她想打断他的财政学讲演，再没有比看他一眼更好的法子了；因为一迎上她的目光，弗雷迪立时断了话头，站在那儿干张嘴。伊芙目光的直接命中，向来对他有此奇效。

===Original===
“Mr. Keeble is going to give you two thousand pounds!”

===Chinese===
「基布尔先生要给你两千镑！」

===Original===
A wave of mortification swept over Eve. If there was one thing on which she prided herself, it was the belief that she was a loyal friend, a staunch pal; and now for the first time she found herself facing the unpleasant truth that she had been neglecting Phyllis Jackson’s interests in the most abominable way ever since she had come to Blandings. She had definitely promised Phyllis that she would tackle this stepfather of hers and shame him with burning words into yielding up the three thousand pounds which Phyllis needed so desperately for her Lincolnshire farm. And what had she done? Nothing.

===Chinese===
一阵羞惭涌过伊芙全身。她平素最自矜的一点，便是自认够朋友、够义气；此刻她头一回直面这个不愉快的真相：自打进了布兰丁斯，她把菲利斯·杰克逊的事荒疏到了丧尽天良的地步。她明确答应过菲利斯，要去找她这位继父当面理论，用烧得滚烫的话臊着他，逼他吐出菲利斯急等着的林肯郡农场那三千镑。她做了什么呢？什么也没做。

===Original===
Eve was honest to the core, even in her dealings with herself. A less conscientious girl might have argued that she had had no opportunity of a private interview with Mr. Keeble. She scorned to soothe herself with this specious plea. If she had given her mind to it she could have brought about a dozen private interviews, and she knew it. No. She had allowed the pleasant persistence of Psmith to take up her time, and Phyllis and her troubles had been thrust into the background. She confessed, despising herself, that she had hardly given Phyllis a thought.

===Chinese===
伊芙骨子里诚实，连对自己也诚实。良心稍逊的姑娘也许会辩解，说她一直没机会与基布尔先生单独谈话。她不屑用这套似是而非的话宽慰自己。真要上心，她能制造出一打单独谈话的机会，这一点她心里有数。不。她任凭皮史密斯那甜腻腻的纠缠占去光阴，菲利斯和她的难处被挤到了幕后。她鄙夷着自己招认：她几乎没替菲利斯上过一回心。

===Original===
And all the while this Mr. Keeble had been in a position to scatter largess, thousands of pounds of it, to undeserving people like Freddie. Why, a word from her about Phyllis would have﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
而这位基布尔先生，却一直坐拥着向弗雷迪这般不肖之辈大把撒钱的余裕。哎呀，只要她提一句菲利斯，他岂不就……

===Original===
“Two thousand pounds?” she repeated dizzily. “Mr. Keeble!”

===Chinese===
「两千镑？」她晕乎乎地重复。「基布尔先生！」

===Original===
“Absolutely!” cried Freddie radiantly. The first shock of looking into her eyes had passed, and he was now revelling in that occupation.

===Chinese===
「妥妥的！」弗雷迪容光焕发地喊。她目光带来的头一阵震荡已经过去，此刻他正陶醉于这份注视。

===Original===
“What for?”

===Chinese===
「凭什么给？」

===Original===
Freddie’s rapt gaze flickered. Love, he perceived, had nearly caused him to be indiscreet.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪痴迷的凝视闪烁了一下。爱情，他醒悟到，险些害他口无遮拦。

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t know,” he mumbled. “He’s just giving it me, you know, don’t you know.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我也说不上，」他含混道。「他就是给我呗，你知道，可不是嘛。」

===Original===
“Did you simply go to him and ask him for it?”

===Chinese===
「你就这么直接上门管他要的？」

===Original===
“Well﻿—er﻿—well, yes. That was about the strength of it.”

===Chinese===
「这个——呃——嗯，是。大概就这么回事。」

===Original===
“And he didn’t object?”

===Chinese===
「他没反对？」

===Original===
“No. He seemed rather pleased.”

===Chinese===
「没有。他还挺乐呵。」

===Original===
“Pleased!” Eve found breathing difficult. She was feeling rather like a man who suddenly discovers that the hole in his back yard which he has been passing nonchalantly for months is a goldmine. If the operation of extracting money from Mr. Keeble was not only easy but also agreeable to the victim﻿ ﻿… She became aware of a sudden imperative need for Freddie’s absence. She wanted to think this thing over.

===Chinese===
「乐呵！」伊芙觉得呼吸都困难了。她的感觉，像一个人猛然发现自家后院那个几个月来天天漠然路过的坑竟是座金矿。倘若从基布尔先生身上抽钱这桩手术不但容易，还能叫受术者其乐融融……她骤然感到一阵当务之急：弗雷迪必须走开。她要好好把这事想透。

===Original===
“Well, then,” said Freddie, “coming back to it, will you?”

===Chinese===
「那么，」弗雷迪说，「回到刚才，你肯不肯？」

===Original===
“What?” said Eve, distrait.

===Chinese===
「什么？」伊芙心不在焉地说。

===Original===
“Marry me, you know. What I mean to say is, I worship the very ground you walk on, and all that sort of rot﻿ ﻿… I mean, and all that. And now that you realise that I’m going to get this couple of thousand﻿ ﻿… and the bookie’s business﻿ ﻿… and whatnot, I mean to say﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「嫁给我呗，你知道。我是说，你脚踏的每一寸地皮我都拜倒，以及诸如此类的混账话……我是说，诸如此类。这会儿你也知道我马上有这两千镑了……还有庄家生意……以及什么什么，我是说……」

===Original===
“Freddie,” said Eve tensely, expressing her harassed nerves in a voice that came hotly through clenched teeth, “go away!”

===Chinese===
「弗雷迪，」伊芙绷紧了说，把受折磨的神经化作从牙缝里滚出来的滚烫嗓音，「走开！」

===Original===
“Eh?”

===Chinese===
「嗯？」

===Original===
“I don’t want to marry you, and I’m sick of having to keep on telling you so. Will you please go away and leave me alone?” She stopped. Her sense of fairness told her that she was working off on her hapless suitor venom which should have been expended on herself. “I’m sorry, Freddie,” she said, softening; “I didn’t mean to be such a beast as that. I know you’re awfully fond of me, but really, really I can’t marry you. You don’t want to marry a girl who doesn’t love you, do you?”

===Chinese===
「我不想嫁你，一遍遍重申这话我烦透了。请你走开，让我一个人待会儿，行不行？」她顿了顿。她的公道心告诉她，她这是把本该冲自己发的火，撒在了倒霉的追求者头上。「对不起，弗雷迪，」她软下来，「我不是有意那么凶。我知道你特别喜欢我，可是真的，真的我不能嫁你。你总不见得想娶一个不爱你的姑娘吧？」

===Original===
“Yes, I do,” said Freddie stoutly. “If it’s you, I mean. Love is a tiny seed that coldness can wither, but if tended and nurtured in the fostering warmth of an honest heart﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「想，我还真想，」弗雷迪梗着脖子说。「要是你，我是说。爱情是一粒小小的种子，冷漠能叫它枯萎，但若在一颗诚心的暖巢里勤加培育……」

===Original===
“But, Freddie.”

===Chinese===
「可是，弗雷迪。」

===Original===
“Blossoms into a flower,” concluded Freddie rapidly. “What I mean to say is, love would come after marriage.”

===Chinese===
「便会开出花来，」弗雷迪飞快地收尾。「我是说，结了婚爱情自然就来了。」

===Original===
“Nonsense!”

===Chinese===
「胡说！」

===Original===
“Well, that’s the way it happened in ‘A Society Mating.’ ”

===Chinese===
「反正在《门第联姻》里就是这么回事。」

===Original===
“Freddie,” said Eve, “I really don’t want to talk any more. Will you be a dear and just go away? I’ve got a lot of thinking to do.”

===Chinese===
「弗雷迪，」伊芙说，「我真不想再谈了。乖一点，走开好不好？我有一大堆事要想。」

===Original===
“Oh, thinking?” said Freddie, impressed. “Right ho!”

===Chinese===
「哦，要想事？」弗雷迪肃然起敬。「得嘞！」

===Original===
“Thank you so much.”

===Chinese===
「多谢。」

===Original===
“Oh﻿—er﻿—not at all. Well, pip-pip.”

===Chinese===
「哦——呃——不客气。那么，回头见。」

===Original===
“Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「再见。」

===Original===
“See you later, what?”

===Chinese===
「一会儿见，嗯？」

===Original===
“Of course, of course.”

===Chinese===
「当然，当然。」

===Original===
“Fine! Well, toodle-oo!”

===Chinese===
「好极了！那么，回见啦！」

===Original===
And the Hon. Freddie, not ill-pleased﻿—for it seemed to him that at long last he detected signs of melting in the party of the second part﻿—swivelled round on his long legs and started for home.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪少爷心满意足——因为在他看来，对方阵营终于透出了一丝松动的迹象——长腿一拧，转身回府去了。

### III / III

===Original===
The little town of Market Blandings was a peaceful sight as it slept in the sun. For the first time since Freddie had left her, Eve became conscious of a certain tranquillity as she entered the old grey High Street, which was the centre of the place’s life and thought. Market Blandings had a comforting air of having been exactly the same for centuries. Troubles might vex the generations it housed, but they did not worry that lichened church with its sturdy foursquare tower, nor those red-roofed shops, nor the age-old inns whose second stories bulged so comfortably out over the pavements. As Eve walked in slow meditation towards the Emsworth Arms, the intensely respectable hostelry which was her objective, archways met her gaze, opening with a picturesque unexpectedness to show heartening glimpses of ancient nooks all cool and green. There was about the High Street of Market Blandings a suggestion of a slumbering cathedral close. Nothing was modern in it except the moving-picture house﻿—and even that called itself an Electric Theatre, and was ivy-covered and surmounted by stone gables.

===Chinese===
阳光下的布兰丁斯集镇酣睡着，是一派太平景象。弗雷迪走后，伊芙头一回觉出几分安宁——她走进了镇子那条灰色的老高街，全镇的生活与思想的中枢。布兰丁斯集镇带着一股几百年一成不变的抚慰气息。烦恼可以折磨一代代镇民，却搅扰不着那座爬满地衣的教堂和它敦实的方塔，搅扰不着那些红瓦的铺面，也搅扰不着那些二层楼舒舒服服地悬出人行道之上的老客栈。伊芙缓步冥行，朝她的目的地、那家极其端正的埃姆斯沃思纹章客栈走去，一路上拱门不断映入眼帘，出其不意地、画境般地敞开，露出一个个清凉碧绿的古老角落的暖心一瞥。布兰丁斯集镇的高街，隐隐有一座沉睡的大教堂庭院的意味。街上没有一样东西是现代的，只有那家电影院——而且连它也自称「电光戏院」，爬满常春藤，顶着石砌的山墙。

===Original===
On second thoughts, that statement is too sweeping. There was one other modern building in the High Street﻿—Jno. Banks, Hairdresser, to wit, and Eve was just coming abreast of Mr. Banks’s emporium now.

===Chinese===
再一想，这话下得太绝。高街上还有一栋现代建筑——理发师约·班克斯的铺子——伊芙此刻恰好走到班克斯先生那间铺面的门前。

===Original===
In any ordinary surroundings these premises would have been a tolerably attractive sight, but in Market Blandings they were almost an eyesore; and Eve, finding herself at the door, was jarred out of her reverie as if she had heard a false note in a solemn anthem. She was on the point of hurrying past, when the door opened and a short, solid figure came out. And at the sight of this short, solid figure Eve stopped abruptly.

===Chinese===
换了寻常环境，这店面本算得体面，可在布兰丁斯集镇，它简直有碍观瞻；伊芙到了门前，像在一支庄严颂歌里听见了个错音似的，从遐想里震了出来。她正要匆匆走过，店门开了，一个矮墩敦的实心身影走了出来。一看见这个矮墩敦的实心身影，伊芙猛地站住了。

===Original===
It was with the object of getting his grizzled locks clipped in preparation for the County Ball that Joseph Keeble had come to Mr. Banks’s shop as soon as he had finished lunch. As he emerged now into the High Street he was wondering why he had permitted Mr. Banks to finish off the job with a heliotrope-scented hair-wash. It seemed to Mr. Keeble that the air was heavy with heliotrope, and it came to him suddenly that heliotrope was a scent which he always found particularly objectionable.

===Chinese===
约瑟夫·基布尔午饭后头一件事就是来班克斯先生店里，把花白的头发修剪停当，好赴郡舞会。此刻他踱上高街，心里正纳闷自己怎么会由着班克斯先生最后给上了道天芥菜花香型的洗发水。基布尔先生觉得空气里天芥菜花味浓得化不开，而且他蓦地想起，天芥菜花香型恰恰是他素来格外受不了的一味。

===Original===
Ordinarily Joseph Keeble was accustomed to show an iron front to hairdressers who tried to inflict lotions upon him; and the reason his vigilance had relaxed under the ministrations of Jno. Banks was that the second post, which arrived at the castle at the luncheon hour, had brought him a plaintive letter from his stepdaughter Phyllis﻿—the second he had had from her since the one which had caused him to tackle his masterful wife in the smoking-room. Immediately after the conclusion of his business deal with the Hon. Freddie, he had written to Phyllis in a vein of optimism rendered glowing by Freddie’s promises, assuring her that at any moment he would be in a position to send her the three thousand pounds which she required to clinch the purchase of that dream-farm in Lincolnshire. To this she had replied with thanks. And after that there had been a lapse of days and still he had not made good. Phyllis was becoming worried, and said so in six closely-written pages.

===Chinese===
寻常日子，理发师傅们想硬塞香水发水，基布尔先生向来是铁面顶回的；这回在约·班克斯手里失了警惕，是因为午饭时分送达城堡的第二班邮递，捎来了继女菲利斯一封哀恳的信——自那封害他在吸烟室硬撼当家太太的信之后，这是第二封。与弗雷迪少爷那桩交易一谈妥，他就以一腔被弗雷迪的许诺映得红彤彤的乐观，写信告诉菲利斯：三千镑随时可以汇出，让她落定林肯郡那座梦想农场。她回信道了谢。此后过了好些天，钱仍无着落。菲利斯着了急，密密写了六页纸明说。

===Original===
Mr. Keeble, as he sat in the barber’s chair going over this letter in his mind, had groaned in spirit, while Jno. Banks with gleaming eyes did practically what he liked with the heliotrope bottle. Not for the first time since the formation of their partnership, Joseph Keeble was tormented with doubts as to his wisdom in entrusting a commission so delicate as the purloining of his wife’s diamond necklace to one of his nephew Freddie’s known feebleness of intellect. Here, he told himself unhappily, was a job of work which would have tested the combined abilities of a syndicate consisting of Charles Peace and the James Brothers, and he had put it in the hands of a young man who in all his life had only once shown genuine inspiration and initiative﻿—on the occasion when he had parted his hair in the middle at a time when all the other members of the Bachelors’ Club were brushing it straight back. The more Mr. Keeble thought of Freddie’s chances, the slimmer they appeared. By the time Jno. Banks had released him from the spotted apron he was thoroughly pessimistic, and as he passed out of the door, “so perfumed that the winds were lovesick with him,” his estimate of his colleague’s abilities was reduced to a point where he began to doubt whether the stealing of a mere milk-can was not beyond his scope. So deeply immersed was he in these gloomy thoughts that Eve had to call his name twice before he came out of them.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生坐在理发椅里，心里把这封信前后过堂，灵魂里不住呻吟；约·班克斯眼睛雪亮，拿那瓶天芥菜花水由着性子施展。自打合伙以来，约瑟夫·基布尔已经不是头一回犯嘀咕：把偷老婆钻石项链这样一桩精细入微的差事，托付给外甥弗雷迪那众所周知的智力水准，是否失算。他郁郁地想，这活儿拿去考「和平的佩斯」加詹姆斯兄弟帮联营的合力，都算艰险，他却交在了一个青年手里——此人平生只显过一回真正的灵感和魄力，就是全体单身汉俱乐部同好都往后梳头那年，他把头发中分。基布尔先生越掂量弗雷迪的胜算，胜算越薄。到约·班克斯解下他那条斑点围布时，他已经彻底悲观；他跨出门去，「香喷喷地，连风都为他害起相思」，他对同事能力的评估已跌落到怀疑连偷一只奶桶都非其力所能及。他沉在这堆阴郁念头里出不来，伊芙喊了两遍他的名字才把他喊醒。

===Original===
“Miss Halliday?” he said apologetically. “I beg your pardon. I was thinking.”

===Chinese===
「哈利迪小姐？」他赔笑道。「恕我失神。我在想心事。」

===Original===
Eve, though they had hardly exchanged a word since her arrival at the castle, had taken a liking to Mr. Keeble; and she felt in consequence none of the embarrassment which might have handicapped her in the discussion of an extremely delicate matter with another man. By nature direct and straightforward, she came to the point at once.

===Chinese===
伊芙虽自进堡以来几乎没同他交谈过，却对基布尔先生颇有好感；因此此刻要同一个男人商议一件极其微妙的事，她毫无那种可能缚手缚脚的窘迫。她天性爽直，开门见山。

===Original===
“Can you spare me a moment or two, Mr. Keeble?” she said. She glanced at the clock on the church tower and saw that she had ample time before her own appointment. “I want to talk to you about Phyllis.” Mr. Keeble jerked his head back in astonishment, and the world became noisome with heliotrope. It was as if the Voice of Conscience had suddenly addressed him.

===Chinese===
「能借您一两分钟吗，基布尔先生？」她说。她瞥了一眼教堂塔楼的钟，离她自己那场会面时间还很宽裕。「我想跟您说说菲利斯的事。」基布尔先生惊得头往后一仰，整个世界登时天芥菜花味熏天。这情形，仿佛良心之声突然对他开了口。

===Original===
“Phyllis!” he gasped, and the letter crackled in his breast-pocket.

===Chinese===
「菲利斯！」他倒抽一口气，胸口口袋里的信簌簌作响。

===Original===
“Your stepdaughter Phyllis.”

===Chinese===
「您的继女菲利斯。」

===Original===
“Do you know her?”

===Chinese===
「你认得她？」

===Original===
“She was my best friend at school. I had tea with her just before I came to the castle.”

===Chinese===
「她是我学生时代最要好的朋友。来城堡前刚跟她一起喝过茶。」

===Original===
“Extraordinary!” said Mr. Keeble.

===Chinese===
「真想不到！」基布尔先生说。

===Original===
A customer in quest of a shave thrust himself between them and went into the shop. They moved away a few paces.

===Chinese===
一位来刮脸的顾客挤到两人中间，进了店。他们挪开几步。

===Original===
“Of course if you say it is none of my business﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「当然，您要是说这事与我无关……」

===Original===
“My dear young lady﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的小姐……」

===Original===
“Well, it *is* my business, because she’s my friend,” said Eve firmly. “Mr. Keeble, Phyllis told me she had written to you about buying that farm. Why don’t you help her?”

===Chinese===
「不，这事*就是*与我有关，因为她是我朋友，」伊芙坚定地说。「基布尔先生，菲利斯告诉我，她为买农场的事给您写过信。您为什么不帮她？」

===Original===
The afternoon was warm, but not warm enough to account for the moistness of Mr. Keeble’s brow. He drew out a large handkerchief and mopped his forehead. A hunted look was in his eyes. The hand which was not occupied with the handkerchief had sought his pocket and was busy rattling keys.

===Chinese===
午后的天是暖，可暖不足以解释基布尔先生额头上那层潮气。他抽出一方大手帕，抹了抹脑门。他眼里的神色像被追捕。没放手帕的那只手摸进了口袋，把一串钥匙拨得叮当乱响。

===Original===
“I want to help her. I would do anything in the world to help her.”

===Chinese===
「我想帮她。只要能帮她，叫我干什么都成。」

===Original===
“Then why don’t you?”

===Chinese===
「那您为什么不帮？」

===Original===
“I﻿—I am curiously situated.”

===Chinese===
「我——我处境特殊。」

===Original===
“Yes, Phyllis told me something about that. I can see that it is a difficult position for you. But, Mr. Keeble, surely, surely if you can manage to give Freddie Threepwood two thousand pounds to start a bookmaker’s business﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「是的，菲利斯跟我说了些。我明白您左右为难。可是，基布尔先生， 说真的，说真的，您既然能挤出两千镑给弗雷迪·思里普伍德去开庄家生意……」

===Original===
Her words were cut short by a strangled cry from her companion. Sheer panic was in his eyes now, and in his heart an overwhelming regret that he had ever been fool enough to dabble in crime in the company of a mere animated talking-machine like his nephew Freddie. This girl knew! And if she knew, how many others knew? The young imbecile had probably babbled his hideous secret into the ears of every human being in the place who would listen to him.

===Chinese===
话没说完，被同伴一声扼住的喊叫截断了。他眼里此刻是纯粹的恐慌，心里是对自己的切齿悔恨：怎么会蠢到跟弗雷迪那样一台会说话的活机器搭伙作奸犯科。这姑娘知道了！她既知道，还有多少人也知道？这小痴货八成把这桩骇人的秘密，见着能听的人就往外倒。

===Original===
“He told you!” he stammered. “He t-told you!”

===Chinese===
「他告诉你了！」他结结巴巴。「他、他告诉你了！」

===Original===
“Yes. Just now.”

===Chinese===
「是的。刚刚。」

===Original===
“Goosh!” muttered Mr. Keeble brokenly.

===Chinese===
「天！」基布尔先生碎裂地喃喃。

===Original===
Eve stared at him in surprise. She could not understand this emotion. The handkerchief, after a busy session, was lowered now, and he was looking at her imploringly.

===Chinese===
伊芙愕然瞪着他。她闹不懂这阵情绪。那方手帕忙了一阵之后垂了下来，他正一脸恳求地望着她。

===Original===
“You haven’t told anyone?” he croaked hoarsely.

===Chinese===
「你没告诉旁人吧？」他哑着嗓子问。

===Original===
“Of course not. I said I had only heard of it just now.”

===Chinese===
「当然没有。我说了，我也是刚听说。」

===Original===
“You wouldn’t tell anyone?”

===Chinese===
「你也不会告诉旁人吧？」

===Original===
“Why should I?”

===Chinese===
「我为什么要？」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble’s breath, which had seemed to him for a moment gone for ever, began to return timidly. Relief for a space held him dumb. What nonsense, he reflected, these newspapers and people talked about the modern girl. It was this very broad-mindedness of hers, to which they objected so absurdly, that made her a creature of such charm. She might behave in certain ways in a fashion that would have shocked her grandmother, but how comforting it was to find her calm and unmoved in the contemplation of another’s crime. His heart warmed to Eve.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生那口仿佛一去不返的气，开始怯生生地回流。宽慰有一阵子噎得他说不出话。如今这些报纸和世人讲的现代姑娘如何如何，他想，全是胡说。恰恰是他们荒唐指摘的她这份宽宏，使她成了这般可人的造物。她在某些行事上也许会吓坏她外祖母，可发现她面对他人的罪案竟能如此平静不动声色，多么令人宽慰啊。他对伊芙热络起来。

===Original===
“You’re wonderful!” he said.

===Chinese===
「你真是了不起！」他说。

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
「您这是什么意思？」

===Original===
“Of course,” argued Mr. Keeble, “it isn’t really stealing.”

===Chinese===
「再说了，」基布尔先生论辩道，「这也实在算不上偷。」

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
“I shall buy my wife another necklace.”

===Chinese===
「我会给我太太另买一条项链。」

===Original===
“You will﻿—what?”

===Chinese===
「您要——什么？」

===Original===
“So everything will be all right. Constance will be perfectly happy, and Phyllis will have her money, and﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「所以一切都妥帖了。康斯坦丝照样欢天喜地，菲利斯拿到钱，而且……」

===Original===
Something in Eve’s astonished gaze seemed to smite Mr. Keeble.

===Chinese===
伊芙满眼惊愕的注视里有点什么，似乎痛击了基布尔先生一下。

===Original===
“Don’t you *know*?” he broke off.

===Chinese===
「您*不知道*？」他收住话头。

===Original===
“Know? Know what?”

===Chinese===
「知道？知道什么？」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble perceived that he had wronged Freddie. The young ass had been a fool even to mention the money to this girl, but he had at least, it seemed, stopped short of disclosing the entire plot. An oyster-like reserve came upon him.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生醒悟到自己冤枉了弗雷迪。那头蠢驴把这个数目透露给这姑娘已经是蠢，不过看起来，他总算还没把全盘计划抖搂出去。他摆出牡蛎般的缄默。

===Original===
“Nothing, nothing,” he said hastily. “Forget what I was going to say. Well, I must be going, I must be going.”

===Chinese===
「没什么，没什么，」他急忙说。「忘了我方才要说的话吧。好了，我该走了，该走了。」

===Original===
Eve clutched wildly at his retreating sleeve. Unintelligible though his words had been, one sentence had come home to her, the one about Phyllis having her money. It was no time for half-measures. She grabbed him.

===Chinese===
伊芙一把死死薅住他撤退的袖子。他的话虽然没头没脑，有一句却扎进了她心里——菲利斯会拿到钱那句。此刻不是温吞从事的时候。她拽住他。

===Original===
“Mr. Keeble,” she cried urgently. “I don’t know what you mean, but you were just going to say something which sounded﻿ ﻿… Mr. Keeble, do trust me. I’m Phyllis’s best friend, and if you’ve thought out any way of helping her I wish you would tell me﻿ ﻿… You must tell me. I might be able to help﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「基布尔先生，」她急切地喊。「我不明白您的意思，可您刚要说的话听着像是……基布尔先生，务必信我。我是菲利斯最好的朋友，您若想出了什么帮她的法子，求您告诉我……您必须告诉我。兴许我能帮上忙……」

===Original===
Mr. Keeble, as she began her broken speech, had been endeavouring with deprecatory tugs to disengage his coat from her grasp. But now he ceased to struggle. Those doubts of Freddie’s efficiency, which had troubled him in Jno. Banks’s chair, still lingered. His opinion that Freddie was but a broken reed had not changed. Indeed, it had grown. He looked at Eve. He looked at her searchingly. Into her pleading eyes he directed a stare that sought to probe her soul, and saw there honesty, sympathy, and﻿—better still﻿—intelligence. He might have stood and gazed into Freddie’s fishy eyes for weeks without discovering a tithe of such intelligence. His mind was made up. This girl was an ally. A girl of dash and vigour. A girl worth a thousand Freddies﻿—not, however, reflected Mr. Keeble, that that was saying much. He hesitated no longer.

===Chinese===
基布尔先生在她开讲的当口，本还在赔着小心一点点往回抽外套；此刻他住手不挣了。在约·班克斯椅子里折磨过他的那些对弗雷迪才干的疑虑，仍未散去。弗雷迪不过一根折了的芦苇，这看法没变。非但没变，还长了。他看伊芙。他细细地看。他把一道直探她灵魂的凝视，钉进她那双恳求的眼睛，看见里面是诚实、同情，以及——更好的一味——才智。换成弗雷迪那双鱼眼，他站上几个礼拜也盯不出十分之一这样的才智。他拿定了主意。这姑娘是盟友。一位有胆有识的姑娘。一个抵得上一千个弗雷迪的姑娘——不过，基布尔先生又想，这话也不算多大恭维。他不再迟疑。

===Original===
“It’s like this,” said Mr. Keeble.

===Chinese===
「事情是这样的，」基布尔先生说。

### IV / IV

===Original===
The information, authoritatively conveyed to him during breakfast by Lady Constance, that he was scheduled that night to read select passages from Ralston McTodd’s *Songs of Squalor* to the entire house-party assembled in the big drawing-room, had come as a complete surprise to Psmith, and to his fellow-guests﻿—such of them as were young and of the soulless sex﻿—as a shock from which they found it hard to rally. True, they had before now gathered in a vague sort of way that he was one of those literary fellows, but so normal and engaging had they found his whole manner and appearance that it had never occurred to them that he concealed anything up his sleeve as lethal as *Songs of Squalor*. Among these members of the younger set the consensus of opinion was that it was a bit thick, and that at such a price even the lavish hospitality of Blandings was scarcely worth having. Only those who had visited the castle before during the era of her ladyship’s flirtation with Art could have been described as resigned. These stout hearts argued that while this latest blister was probably going to be pretty bad, he could hardly be worse than the chappie who had lectured on Theosophy last November, and must almost of necessity be better than the bird who during the Shifley race-week had attempted in a two-hour discourse to convert them to vegetarianism.

===Chinese===
早餐时由康斯坦丝夫人权威发布的消息——当晚他要在大客厅向阖府宾客朗诵拉尔斯顿·麦克托德《阴郁之歌》选段——对皮史密斯完全是晴天霹雳，而对他的宾客同仁——其中年轻而无灵魂的那一半——则不啻一记闷棍，半天缓不过神来。诚然，他们先前约莫风闻他是文人之流，可他的言谈做派如此正常、如此可亲，谁也不曾想到他袖子里还藏着《阴郁之歌》这般致命的家伙。年轻一辈的公论是：这未免过分；照这个价钱，布兰丁斯再慷慨的款待也不大值得领受。唯有那些在夫人向艺术抛媚眼的年代造访过城堡的老人，才谈得上处变不惊。这些坚强的灵魂争辩道：眼下这位瘟神多半会相当糟糕，但比起去年十一月办通神学讲座的那位家伙想必坏不到哪儿去，比起希夫利赛马周上那位企图用两个钟头的布道劝大家吃素的老兄，则几乎注定高明。

===Original===
Psmith himself regarded the coming ordeal with equanimity. He was not one of those whom the prospect of speaking in public afflicts with nervous horror. He liked the sound of his own voice, and night, when it came, found him calmly cheerful. He listened contentedly to the murmur of the drawing-room filling up as he strolled on the starlit terrace, smoking a last cigarette before duty called him elsewhere. And when, some few yards away, seated on the terrace wall gazing out into the velvet darkness, he perceived Eve Halliday, his sense of well-being became acute.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯本人则以平常心看待这场酷刑。他不属于那种一想到当众讲话便神经战栗的人。他爱听自己的嗓音，及至夜晚降临，他从容而愉快。宾客的私语渐渐灌满客厅，他却在星光下的露台上溜达，赶赴使命前吸掉最后一支烟，听得心满意足。而当他望见几码开外、坐在露台墙上凝望天鹅绒般夜色的伊芙·哈利迪时，他的舒适感达到了顶点。

===Original===
All day he had been conscious of a growing desire for another of those cosy chats with Eve which had done so much to make life agreeable for him during his stay at Blandings. Her prejudice﻿—which he deplored﻿—in favour of doing a certain amount of work to justify her salary, had kept him during the morning away from the little room off the library where she was wont to sit cataloguing books; and when he had gone there after lunch he had found it empty. As he approached her now, he was thinking pleasantly of all those delightful walks, those excellent driftings on the lake, and those cheery conversations which had gone to cement his conviction that of all possible girls she was the only possible one. It seemed to him that in addition to being beautiful she brought out all that was best in him of intellect and soul. That is to say, she let him talk oftener and longer than any girl he had ever known.

===Chinese===
一整天来，他都越来越渴望同伊芙再来进行一场那样舒心的恳谈——做客布兰丁斯期间，正是那些恳谈大大安顿了他的生活。她那种偏爱干点活以对得起薪水的偏见（他深表遗憾），使他整个上午无法靠近藏书室旁那间她惯常坐着编书的小屋；午饭后再去，屋里已经空了。此刻他朝她走近，愉快地想着那些惬意的散步、湖上出色的荡桨、那些痛快的交谈——这一切都砌实了他的信念：天下姑娘虽多，她是唯一可能的那一个。在他看来，她除了美丽，还能把他心智与灵魂里最好的东西全引出来。也就是说，她让他说话的次数和长度，超过他认识的任何一位姑娘。

===Original===
It struck him as a little curious that she made no move to greet him. She remained apparently unaware of his approach. And yet the summer night was not of such density as to hide him from view﻿—and, even if she could not see him, she must undoubtedly have heard him; for only a moment before he had tripped with some violence over a large flowerpot, one of a row of sixteen which Angus McAllister, doubtless for some good purpose, had placed in the fairway that afternoon.

===Chinese===
她没有起身相迎的意思，这让他觉出一丝古怪。她似乎浑然不觉他在走近。而夏夜实在没有浓到能藏住他的地步——就算看不见，她也一定听得见他；因为就在片刻之前，他还结结实实地绊了一跤，磕在一只大花盆上——那是十六盆一排里的一盆，安格斯·麦卡利斯特那天下午不知出于什么高见，把它们码在了走道正中。

===Original===
“A pleasant night,” he said, seating himself gracefully beside her on the wall.

===Chinese===
「宜人的夜晚，」他说，风度优雅地在墙头她身旁落座。

===Original===
She turned her head for a brief instant, and, having turned it, looked away again.

===Chinese===
她的头偏过来一瞬，随即又转开了。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said.

===Chinese===
「是啊。」她说。

===Original===
Her manner was not effusive, but Psmith persevered.

===Chinese===
她的态度谈不上热络，但皮史密斯不气馁。

===Original===
“The stars,” he proceeded, indicating them with a kindly yet not patronising wave of the hand. “Bright, twinkling, and﻿—if I may say so﻿—rather neatly arranged. When I was a mere lad, someone whose name I cannot recollect taught me which was Orion. Also Mars, Venus, and Jupiter. This thoroughly useless chunk of knowledge has, I am happy to say, long since passed from my mind. However, I am in a position to state that that wiggly thing up there a little to the right is King Charles’s Wain.”

===Chinese===
「星星，」他接着说，抬手朝它们一挥，慈和而不失谦逊。「明亮，闪烁，而且——恕我直言——编排得相当利落。我还是小小孩儿那会儿，有个记不起姓名的人教过我哪颗是猎户座。还有火星、金星、木星。这一大块毫无用处的知识，所幸早已从我脑中流失。不过，我还能确定，那边偏右一点那个弯弯曲曲的东西，是查理王的车。」

===Original===
“Yes?”

===Chinese===
「是吗？」

===Original===
“Yes, indeed, I assure you.” It struck Psmith that Astronomy was not gripping his audience, so he tried Travel. “I hear,” he said, “you went to Market Blandings this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「是呀，真的，我担保。」皮史密斯发觉天文学没有抓住他的听众，便改试旅游。「听说，」他说，「你今天下午去布兰丁斯集镇了。」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是。」

===Original===
“An attractive settlement.”

===Chinese===
「一个招人爱的聚落。」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是。」

===Original===
There was a pause. Psmith removed his monocle and polished it thoughtfully. The summer night seemed to him to have taken on a touch of chill.

===Chinese===
一阵停顿。皮史密斯摘下单片眼镜，若有所思地擦拭起来。夏夜在他似乎起了一丝凉意。

===Original===
“What I like about the English rural districts,” he went on, “is that when the authorities have finished building a place they stop. Somewhere about the reign of Henry the Eighth, I imagine that the master-mason gave the final house a pat with his trowel and said, ‘Well, boys, that’s Market Blandings.’ To which his assistants no doubt assented with many a hearty ‘Grammercy!’ and ‘I’fackins!’ these being expletives to which they were much addicted. And they went away and left it, and nobody has touched it since. And I, for one, thoroughly approve. I think it makes the place soothing. Don’t you?”

===Chinese===
「我喜欢英格兰乡间的一点，」他接着说，「是当局把一个地方盖完之后，就收手了。大约亨利八世朝，我想，那位大工匠师傅给最后一栋房子用瓦刀拍上一拍，说道：『行了，伙计们，布兰丁斯集镇成了。』助手们想必齐声应了许多声热诚的『善哉！』和『真的！』——这类口头禅他们极是受用。然后他们收工走人，从此再没人动过它一指头。而我，头一个，举双手赞成。我认为这使此地令人心安。你说呢？」

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是。」

===Original===
As far as the darkness would permit, Psmith subjected Eve to an inquiring glance through his monocle. This was a strange new mood in which he had found her. Hitherto, though she had always endeared herself to him by permitting him the major portion of the dialogue, they had usually split conversations on at least a seventy-five﻿—twenty-five basis. And though it gratified Psmith to be allowed to deliver a monologue when talking with most people, he found Eve more companionable when in a slightly chattier vein.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯隔着单片眼镜，在夜色许可的范围内对伊芙投去询问的一瞥。这是他在她身上发现的一种陌生的新情绪。迄今为止，虽然她一向肯把对话的大头恩赏给他，使他们谈得拢，两人通常也至少按七十五比二十五分成。同多数人交谈时，人家肯让他唱独角戏，皮史密斯固然受用；但同伊芙，他更喜欢她话多一点的时候。

===Original===
“Are you coming in to hear me read?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「你来听我朗诵吗？」他问。

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
「不。」

===Original===
It was a change from “Yes,” but that was the best that could be said of it. A good deal of discouragement was always required to damp Psmith, but he could not help feeling a slight diminution of buoyancy. However, he kept on trying.

===Chinese===
好歹是从「是」换成了「不」。浇灭皮史密斯向来需要相当剂量的挫折，但他还是觉出 buoyancy 略有折损。然而他接着试。

===Original===
“You show your usual sterling good sense,” he said approvingly. “A scalier method of passing the scented summer night could hardly be hit upon.” He abandoned the topic of the reading. It did not grip. That was manifest. It lacked appeal. “I went to Market Blandings this afternoon, too,” he said. “Comrade Baxter informed me that you had gone thither, so I went after you. Not being able to find you, I turned in for half an hour at the local moving-picture palace. They were showing Episode Eleven of a serial. It concluded with the heroine, kidnapped by Indians, stretched on the sacrificial altar with the high-priest making passes at her with a knife. The hero meanwhile had started to climb a rather nasty precipice on his way to the rescue. The final picture was a closeup of his fingers slipping slowly off a rock. Episode Twelve next week.”

===Chinese===
「你显示了素来的精明，」他嘉许道。「要消磨这个芬芳的夏夜，再难找到比听诗更下策的法子了。」他放弃了朗诵这个话题。它抓不住人。这很明显。它缺乏号召力。「我今天下午也去了布兰丁斯集镇，」他说。「巴克斯特同志告诉我说你去了，我就追了去。没找着你，我便钻进本地那家电影宫坐了半个钟头。正放一部连集片的第十一集。结尾是女主角被印第安人掳走，绑上祭坛，大祭司拿刀在她身上比比画画。男主角与此同时已经动身爬一座相当险恶的悬崖，赶来搭救。最后一幅画面，是他的手指正从一块岩石上慢慢滑落的特写。下周放第十二集。」

===Original===
Eve looked out into the night without speaking.

===Chinese===
伊芙望着夜色，一言不发。

===Original===
“I’m afraid it won’t end happily,” said Psmith with a sigh. “I think he’ll save her.”

===Chinese===
「恐怕结局不会圆满，」皮史密斯叹道。「不过我看他会救出她。」

===Original===
Eve turned on him with a menacing abruptness.

===Chinese===
伊芙挟着威势猛地转向他。

===Original===
“Shall I tell you why I went to Market Blandings this afternoon?” she said.

===Chinese===
「要不要告诉你，我今天下午为什么去布兰丁斯集镇？」她说。

===Original===
“Do,” said Psmith cordially. “It is not for me to criticise, but as a matter of fact I was rather wondering when you were going to begin telling me all about your adventures. I have been monopolising the conversation.”

===Chinese===
「请讲，」皮史密斯热忱地说。「轮不到我挑剔；不过说实在的，我正纳闷你几时才开始给我讲讲你的奇遇呢。这一路光我霸着话头了。」

===Original===
“I went to meet Cynthia.”

===Chinese===
「我去见辛西娅了。」

===Original===
Psmith’s monocle fell out of his eye and swung jerkily on its cord. He was not easily disconcerted, but this unexpected piece of information, coming on top of her peculiar manner, undoubtedly jarred him. He foresaw difficulties, and once again found himself thinking hard thoughts of this confounded female who kept bobbing up when least expected. How simple life would have been, he mused wistfully, had Ralston McTodd only had the good sense to remain a bachelor.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯的单片眼镜从眼眶里跌出来，在绳上猛地晃荡。他不轻易失态，但这则意外消息，叠加她方才的异样态度，着实硌了他一下。他预见到了麻烦，于是再一次把这位神出鬼没、专在料不到处冒头的可恶女性，在心里狠狠数落了一遍。要是拉尔斯顿·麦克托德当初有点见识、安心当个单身汉，他想，人生何等简单。

===Original===
“Oh, Cynthia?” he said.

===Chinese===
「哦，辛西娅？」他说。

===Original===
“Yes, Cynthia,” said Eve. The inconvenient Mrs. McTodd possessed a Christian name admirably adapted for being hissed between clenched teeth, and Eve hissed it in this fashion now. It became evident to Psmith that the dear girl was in a condition of hardly suppressed fury and that trouble was coming his way. He braced himself to meet it.

===Chinese===
「是，辛西娅，」伊芙说。这位碍事的麦克托德太太的教名，简直天生供人从咬紧的牙缝里嘶出来，伊芙此刻正是这样嘶的。皮史密斯明白了：亲爱的姑娘正处于一触即发的怒火之中，麻烦正朝他走来。他挺直了迎上去。

===Original===
“Directly after we had that talk on the lake, the day I arrived,” continued Eve tersely, “I wrote to Cynthia, telling her to come here at once and meet me at the Emsworth Arms﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「游湖那天谈过之后——我到的那天，」伊芙简短地接着说，「我立刻写信给辛西娅，叫她马上过来，在埃姆斯沃思纹章客栈见我……」

===Original===
“In the High Street,” said Psmith. “I know it. Good beer.”

===Chinese===
「在高街上，」皮史密斯说。「我知道。啤酒好。」

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
“I said they sell good beer﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我说他们家的啤酒好……」

===Original===
“Never mind about the beer,” cried Eve.

===Chinese===
「别提啤酒，」伊芙喊道。

===Original===
“No, no. I merely mentioned it in passing.”

===Chinese===
「好，好。我随口一提。」

===Original===
“At lunch today I got a letter from her saying that she would be there this afternoon. So I hurried off. I wanted﻿—” Eve laughed a hollow, mirthless laugh of a calibre which even the Hon. Freddie Threepwood would have found beyond his powers, and he was a specialist﻿—“I wanted to try to bring you two together. I thought that if I could see her and have a talk with her that you might become reconciled.”

===Chinese===
「今天午饭我收到她的信，说今天下午到。我就赶去了。我原想——」伊芙发出一声空洞无笑的苦笑，其成色连弗雷迪·思里普伍德少爷这等行家也要自愧不如——「我原想设法让你们俩和好。我想，我若见着她、同她谈谈，你们也许能重归于好。」

===Original===
Psmith, though obsessed with a disquieting feeling that he was fighting in the last ditch, pulled himself together sufficiently to pat her hand as it lay beside him on the wall like some white and fragile flower.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯虽被一种困兽犹斗的不祥感缠着，还是撑住了，轻轻拍了拍搭在墙头、像一朵白皙易碎的花的那只手。

===Original===
“That was like you,” he murmured. “That was an act worthy of your great heart. But I fear that the rift between Cynthia and myself has reached such dimensions﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「这正是你的为人，」他喃喃道。「配得上你那颗伟大的心。但恐怕辛西娅与我之间的裂痕，已到了这般地步……」

===Original===
Eve drew her hand away. She swung round, and the battery of her indignant gaze raked him furiously.

===Chinese===
伊芙把手抽了回去。她猛一转身，愤慨的目光像一排炮， 怒不可遏地把他犁了个遍。

===Original===
“I saw Cynthia,” she said, “and she told me that her husband was in Paris.”

===Chinese===
「我见到辛西娅了，」她说，「她告诉我，她丈夫在巴黎。」

===Original===
“Now, how in the world,” said Psmith, struggling bravely but with a growing sense that they were coming over the plate a bit too fast for him, “how in the world did she get an idea like that?”

===Chinese===
「咦，天底下，」皮史密斯说，一面奋勇挣扎、一面愈来愈觉得来球的球速超出了他的应付，「天底下她怎么会起这个念头？」

===Original===
“Do you really want to know?”

===Chinese===
「你当真想知道？」

===Original===
“I do, indeed.”

===Chinese===
「当然。」

===Original===
“Then I’ll tell you. She got the idea because she had had a letter from him, begging her to join him there. She had just finished telling me this, when I caught sight of you from the inn window, walking along the High Street. I pointed you out to Cynthia, and she said she had never seen you before in her life.”

===Chinese===
「那我告诉你。她起这个念头，是因为她收到了他的信，求她去巴黎团聚。她刚说到这儿，我从客栈窗口望见你正走过高街。我把你指给辛西娅看，她说她这辈子从没见过你。」

===Original===
“Women soon forget,” sighed Psmith.

===Chinese===
「女人健忘啊，」皮史密斯叹道。

===Original===
“The only excuse I can find for you,” stormed Eve in a vibrant undertone necessitated by the fact that somebody had just emerged from the castle door and they no longer had the terrace to themselves, “is that you’re mad. When I think of all you said to me about poor Cynthia on the lake that afternoon, when I think of all the sympathy I wasted on you﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我能替你找出的唯一借口，」伊芙低声怒斥——之所以压着嗓子，是因为恰好有人从城堡大门里出来，露台不再是他们两个的了——「就是疯了。我一想到你那天下午在湖上跟我讲的那些可怜的辛西娅，一想到我在你身上白白浪费的同情……」

===Original===
“Not wasted,” corrected Psmith firmly. “It was by no means wasted. It made me love you﻿—if possible﻿—even more.”

===Chinese===
「没白费，」皮史密斯坚定地更正。「绝非白费。它使我爱你——如果还能更爱的话。」

===Original===
Eve had supposed that she had embarked on a tirade which would last until she had worked off her indignation and felt composed again, but this extraordinary remark scattered the thread of her harangue so hopelessly that all she could do was to stare at him in amazed silence.

===Chinese===
伊芙本以为这一通檄文要骂到火气泄尽、心平气和为止，可这句奇语把她的话头搅得七零八落，她只能瞠目结舌地瞪着他。

===Original===
“Womanly intuition,” proceeded Psmith gravely, “will have told you long ere this that I love you with a fervour which with my poor vocabulary I cannot hope to express. True, as you are about to say, we have known each other but a short time, as time is measured. But what of that?”

===Chinese===
「女人的直觉，」皮史密斯庄重地接着说，「想必早已告诉你：我爱你，爱得我贫瘠的词汇无望表达。诚然，你会说，以常情计，我们相识尚短。可那又如何？」

===Original===
Eve raised her eyebrows. Her voice was cold and hostile.

===Chinese===
伊芙扬起眉。她的声音又冷又硬。

===Original===
“After what has happened,” she said, “I suppose I ought not to be surprised at finding you capable of anything, but﻿—are you really choosing this moment to﻿—to propose to me?”

===Chinese===
「出了这许多事，」她说，「我想再没什么能让我吃惊的了，可是——你当真挑这个节骨眼上——向我求婚？」

===Original===
“To employ a favourite word of your own﻿—yes.”

===Chinese===
「借用你心爱的一个词——是。」

===Original===
“And you expect me to take you seriously?”

===Chinese===
「你还指望我当真？」

===Original===
“Assuredly not. I look upon the present disclosure purely as a sighting shot. You may regard it, if you will, as a kind of formal proclamation. I wish simply to go on record as an aspirant to your hand. I want you, if you will be so good, to make a note of my words and give them a thought from time to time. As Comrade Cootes﻿—a young friend of mine whom you have not yet met﻿—would say, ‘Chew on them.’ ”

===Chinese===
「当然不指望。我把此刻的披露纯粹视作一次试射。你若愿意，不妨把它当作一份正式宣言。我只想作为一个求娶者记录在案。请你行个好，把我的话记下，时不时想一想。用库茨同志——你还没见过的一位青年朋友——的话说：『嚼烂了咽下去。』」

===Original===
“I﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我……」

===Original===
“It is possible,” continued Psmith, “that black moments will come to you﻿—for they come to all of us, even the sunniest﻿—when you will find yourself saying, ‘Nobody loves me!’ On such occasions I should like you to add, ‘No, I am wrong. There *is* somebody who loves me.’ At first, it may be, that reflection will bring but scant balm. Gradually, however, as the days go by and we are constantly together and my nature unfolds itself before you like the petals of some timid flower beneath the rays of the sun﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「可能，」皮史密斯接着说，「你会有阴暗的时刻——人人都有，连最阳光的人也不例外——你会发现自己叹道：『没有人爱我！』那种时候，我希望你补上一句：『不，我说错了。*是*有人爱我的。』起初，这个念头也许聊慰无几。然而渐渐地，随着时日推移，随着我们朝夕相处、我的天性如一朵羞怯的花在阳光下那般，在你面前一瓣瓣舒展……」

===Original===
Eve’s eyes opened wider. She had supposed herself incapable of further astonishment, but she saw that she had been mistaken.

===Chinese===
伊芙的眼睛睁得更大了。她本以为自己再无可惊，看来是错了。

===Original===
“You surely aren’t dreaming of staying on here *now*?” she gasped.

===Chinese===
「你总不至于还做梦*继续*待下去吧？」她倒抽一口气。

===Original===
“Most decidedly. Why not?”

===Chinese===
「千真万确要待下去。有何不可？」

===Original===
“But﻿—but what is to prevent me telling everybody that you are not Mr. McTodd?”

===Chinese===
「可是——可是，我逢人就说你不是麦克托德先生，你怎么挡？」

===Original===
“Your sweet, generous nature,” said Psmith. “Your big heart. Your angelic forbearance.”

===Chinese===
「靠你甜美慷慨的天性，」皮史密斯说。「靠你宽大的心。靠你天使般的克制。」

===Original===
“Oh!”

===Chinese===
「哦！」

===Original===
“Considering that I only came here as McTodd﻿—and if you had seen him you would realise that he is not a person for whom the man of sensibility and refinement would lightly allow himself to be mistaken﻿—I say considering that I only took on the job of understudy so as to get to the castle and be near you, I hardly think that you will be able to bring yourself to get me slung out. You must try to understand what happened. When Lord Emsworth started chatting with me under the impression that I was Comrade McTodd, I encouraged the mistake purely with the kindly intention of putting him at his ease. Even when he informed me that he was expecting me to come down to Blandings with him on the five o’clock train, it never occurred to me to do so. It was only when I saw you talking to him in the street and he revealed the fact that you were about to enjoy his hospitality that I decided that there was no other course open to the man of spirit. Consider! Twice that day you had passed out of my life﻿—may I say taking the sunshine with you?﻿—and I began to fear you might pass out of it for ever. So, loath though I was to commit the solecism of planting myself in this happy home under false pretences, I could see no other way. And here I am!”

===Chinese===
「考虑到我此来不过是顶着麦克托德的名——你要是见过他本人，就知道他绝非一个感性与教养兼备之士肯轻易让人误认的人——我是说，考虑到我接下这场替补差事，纯是为了进城堡、到你身边，我想你终究狠不下心把我轰出去。你必须试着理解事情的原委。当初埃姆斯沃思勋爵错把我当麦克托德同志攀谈，我顺着这个误会，纯出好意，只为让他老人家自在。及至他告知我，正候我同乘五点钟的火车前往布兰丁斯，我也绝未想过应卯。直到我看见你在街上同他说话、他道破你即将叨光做客，我才认定：有血性的人至此已别无选择。你想！那一天里，你两度走出我的生命——恕我直言，还把阳光一并带走了——我开始担心你会永远走出它。于是，尽管我很不愿顶着假名义在这户幸福人家安钉子，实在别无良策。于是我就来了！」

===Original===
“You *must* be mad!”

===Chinese===
「你*一定*是疯了！」

===Original===
“Well, as I was saying, the days will go by, you will have ample opportunity of studying my personality, and it is quite possible that in due season the love of an honest heart may impress you as worth having. I may add that I have loved you since the moment when I saw you sheltering from the rain under that awning in Dover Street, and I recall saying as much to Comrade Walderwick when he was chatting with me some short time later on the subject of his umbrella. I do not press you for an answer now﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「好吧，正如我所说，时日会过去，你将有大把机会研究我的人格，而你极可能会发现，时机一到，一颗诚心的爱在你眼里也值得领受。我还可以补充：自打望见你在多佛街那领遮阳篷下避雨，我就爱上了你；记得事后不久，沃尔德威克同志同我闲聊他的雨伞时，我便如此坦言过。我现在并不逼你回答……」

===Original===
“I should hope not!”

===Chinese===
「最好别逼！」

===Original===
“I merely say ‘Think it over.’ It is nothing to cause you mental distress. Other men love you. Freddie Threepwood loves you. Just add me to the list. That is all I ask. Muse on me from time to time. Reflect that I may be an acquired taste. You probably did not like olives the first time you tasted them. Now you probably do. Give me the same chance you would an olive. Consider, also, how little you actually have against me. What, indeed, does it amount to, when you come to examine it narrowly? All you have against me is the fact that I am not Ralston McTodd. Think how comparatively few people *are* Ralston McTodd. Let your meditations proceed along these lines and﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我只说一句：『考虑考虑。』此事不致引起你精神上的痛苦。别的男人也爱你。弗雷迪·思里普伍德爱你。把我添进名单就行。这是我全部的请求。不时把我掂量掂量。想想我也许得像后天后养的那类口味。你头一回吃橄榄多半也不喜欢。如今八成喜欢了。请给我橄榄的同等待遇。再想想，你指控我的事实又有多少。仔细盘点起来，究竟算得了什么呢？你指控我的全部事实，无非我不是拉尔斯顿·麦克托德。想想看，天下*是*拉尔斯顿·麦克托德的人何其少也。让你的沉思沿此推进，那么……」

===Original===
He broke off, for at this moment the individual who had come out of the front door a short while back loomed beside them, and the glint of starlight on glass revealed him as the Efficient Baxter.

===Chinese===
他顿住了，因为方才从正门出来的那位，此刻已立在两人身旁，星光在玻璃上一闪，照出那是能干的巴克斯特。

===Original===
“Everybody is waiting, Mr. McTodd,” said the Efficient Baxter. He spoke the name, as always, with a certain sardonic emphasis.

===Chinese===
「大家都在等，麦克托德先生，」能干的巴克斯特说。这个名字，他一如既往说得带着讥诮的重音。

===Original===
“Of course,” said Psmith affably, “of course. I was forgetting. I will get to work at once. You are quite sure you do not wish to hear a scuttleful of modern poetry, Miss Halliday?”

===Chinese===
「正是，」皮史密斯和蔼地说，「正是。我把正事忘了。这就开工。当真不想来听一煤斗的现代诗歌吗，哈利迪小姐？」

===Original===
“Quite sure.”

===Chinese===
「当真不想。」

===Original===
“And yet even now, so our genial friend here informs us, a bevy of youth and beauty is crowding the drawing-room, agog for the treat. Well, well! It is these strange clashings of personal taste which constitute what we call Life. I think I will write a poem about it some day. Come, Comrade Baxter, let us be up and doing. I must not disappoint my public.”

===Chinese===
「然而就在此刻，据我们这位热心的朋友说，一群青春与美人正挤满客厅，翘首以待这道盛馔。哎呀哎呀！正是个人趣味的这类奇异冲撞，构成了我们所谓的人生。我想改天要为此写一首诗。来吧，巴克斯特同志，咱们起身干活。我不能辜负我的公众。」

===Original===
For some moments after the two had left her﻿—Baxter silent and chilly, Psmith, all debonair chumminess, kneading the other’s arm and pointing out as they went objects of interest by the wayside﻿—Eve remained on the terrace wall, thinking. She was laughing now, but behind her amusement there was another feeling, and one that perplexed her. A good many men had proposed to her in the course of her career, but none of them had ever left her with this odd feeling of exhilaration. Psmith was different from any other man who had come her way, and difference was a quality which Eve esteemed.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
两人离去后——巴克斯特沉默而阴冷，皮史密斯一团和气地揉着对方胳膊，一路指点道旁景观——伊芙在露台墙上又坐了几分钟，想着心事。她此刻在笑，可笑声背后还有另一种感觉，一种令她困惑的感觉。她的生涯里向她求过婚的男人不算少，却没有一个给她留下过这般奇异的振奋。皮史密斯与她遇到的任何男人都不一样，而「不一样」，正是伊芙看重的品质。……

===Original===
She had just reached the conclusion that life for whatever girl might eventually decide to risk it in Psmith’s company would never be dull, when strange doings in her immediate neighbourhood roused her from her meditations.

===Chinese===
她刚得出结论：哪个姑娘若终于拿定主意，冒险与皮史密斯为伴，她的生活绝不会乏味——近旁的怪事便把她从沉思里惊了起来。

===Original===
The thing happened as she rose from her seat on the wall and started to cross the terrace on her way to the front door. She had stopped for an instant beneath the huge open window of the drawing-room to listen to what was going on inside. Faintly, with something of the quality of a far-off phonograph, the sound of Psmith reading came to her; and even at this distance there was a composed blandness about his voice which brought a smile to her lips.

===Chinese===
事情发生在她从墙上起身、横穿露台走向正门的时候。她在大客厅那扇敞开的大窗下驻足了一瞬，听听里头的动静。皮史密斯的朗诵声隐隐传来，带着几分远处留声机的音色；隔这么远，那嗓音里从容圆滑的腔调依然引得她唇边浮起微笑。

===Original===
And then, with a startling abruptness, the lighted window was dark. And she was aware that all the lighted windows on that side of the castle had suddenly become dark. The lamp that shone over the great door ceased to shine. And above the hubbub of voices in the drawing-room she heard Psmith’s patient drawl.

===Chinese===
随即，敞亮的窗口陡然一黑，毫无征兆。她察觉，城堡这一侧的窗户霎时全黑了。照耀大门的那盏灯灭了。客厅的人声鼎沸之上，她听见皮史密斯不紧不慢的拖腔。

===Original===
“Ladies and gentlemen, I think the lights have gone out.”

===Chinese===
「女士们，先生们，我想，灯灭了。」

===Original===
The night air was rent by a single piercing scream. Something flashed like a shooting star and fell at her feet; and, stooping, Eve found in her hands Lady Constance Keeble’s diamond necklace.

===Chinese===
夜空被一声刺耳的尖叫划破。什么东西流星似的一闪，落在她脚边；伊芙俯身拾起，掌心里是康斯坦丝·基布尔夫人的钻石项链。

### V / V

===Original===
To be prepared is everything in this life. Ever since her talk with Mr. Joseph Keeble in the High Street of Market Blandings that afternoon Eve’s mind had been flitting nimbly from one scheme to another, all designed to end in this very act of seizing the necklace in her hands and each rendered impracticable by some annoying flaw. And now that Fate in its impulsive way had achieved for her what she had begun to feel she could never accomplish for herself, she wasted no time in bewildered inaction. The miracle found her ready for it.

===Chinese===
有备无患，是人生第一要义。自那天下午在布兰丁斯集镇高街与约瑟夫·基布尔先生一谈之后，伊芙的头脑便轻捷地在一个个方案间跳来跳去，个个都为的正是这手「把项链攥进手心」，又个个因某个恼人的破绽而不可行。如今，命运以它性急的方式，替她办成了她已开始觉得自力永难办成的事，她没有浪费片刻在发怔上。奇迹降临时，她是备好了的。

===Original===
For an instant she debated with herself the chances of a dash through the darkened hall up the stairs to her room. But the lights might go on again, and she might meet someone. Memories of sensational novels read in the past told her that on occasions such as this people were detained and searched.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
有一瞬，她盘算过趁黑冲过大厅、上楼进自己房间的胜算。可灯随时会亮，她也可能撞见人。读过的惊险小说在记忆里告诫她：这种场合，人会遭扣留搜身。……

===Original===
Suddenly, as she stood there, she found the way. Close beside her, lying on its side, was the flowerpot which Psmith had overturned as he came to join her on the terrace wall. It might have defects as a cache, but at the moment she could perceive none. Most flowerpots are alike, but this was a particularly easily-remembered flowerpot: for in its journeying from the potting shed to the terrace it had acquired on its side a splash of white paint. She would be able to distinguish it from its fellows when, late that night, she crept out to retrieve the spoil. And surely nobody would ever think of suspecting﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
她正站着，蓦地，路子有了。就在她脚边横倒着的，正是皮史密斯来露台墙找她时绊翻的那只花盆。论藏处，它或有缺陷，可此刻她挑不出一星。花盆大都长得彼此彼此，但这只尤其好认：从花棚一路搬到露台，它腰上蹭上了一块白漆。夜深她溜出来起赃时，一眼就能把它从同侪里认出。再说，谁也不会疑心到……

===Original===
She plunged her fingers into the soft mould, and straightened herself, breathing quickly. It was not an ideal piece of work, but it would serve.

===Chinese===
她把手指插进松软的盆土，随即直起身，呼吸急促。活干得不甚讲究，但够用了。

===Original===
She rubbed her fingers on the turf, put the flowerpot back in the row with the others, and then, like a flying white phantom, darted across the terrace and into the house. And so with beating heart, groping her way, to the bathroom to wash her hands.

===Chinese===
她在草皮上蹭净手指，把花盆归回队列，随即像一道飞掠的白色幽灵，闪过露台，蹿进宅子。于是，心跳着、摸着黑，摸进盥洗室去洗手。

===Original===
The twenty-thousand-pound flowerpot looked placidly up at the winking stars.

===Chinese===
那盆价值两万镑的花盆，安然仰望着一眨一眨的星星。

### VI / VI

===Original===
It was perhaps two minutes later that Mr. Cootes, sprinting lustily, rounded the corner of the house and burst on to the terrace. Late as usual.

===Chinese===
约莫两分钟后，库茨先生撒腿飞奔，绕过屋角，冲上露台。照例迟了一步。

## XI Almost Entirely About Flowerpots / 几乎全与花盆有关

### I / I

===Original===
The Efficient Baxter prowled feverishly up and down the yielding carpet of the big drawing-room. His eyes gleamed behind their spectacles, his dome-like brow was corrugated. Except for himself, the room was empty. As far as the scene of the disaster was concerned, the tumult and the shouting had died. It was going on vigorously in practically every other part of the house, but in the drawing-room there was stillness, if not peace.

===Chinese===
能干的巴克斯特在大客厅下陷的地毯上狂躁地踱来踱去。镜片后的双眼灼灼放光，穹顶似的额头拧出沟壑。除了他，屋里空无一人。就灾祸现场本身而言，喧嚣与呐喊俱已平息。宅子里几乎其他每个角落，追索正闹得热火朝天，唯独客厅里，静——纵非安——笼罩一切。

===Original===
Baxter paused, came to a decision, went to the wall and pressed the bell.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特驻足，拿定主意，走到墙边按铃。

===Original===
“Thomas,” he said when that footman presented himself a few moments later.

===Chinese===
「托马斯，」男仆片刻后到，他说。

===Original===
“Sir?”

===Chinese===
「先生？」

===Original===
“Send Susan to me.”

===Chinese===
「叫苏珊来见我。」

===Original===
“Susan, sir?”

===Chinese===
「苏珊，先生？」

===Original===
“Yes, Susan,” snapped the Efficient One, who had always a short way with the domestic staff. “Susan, Susan, Susan.﻿ ﻿… The new parlourmaid.”

===Chinese===
「对，苏珊，」能干的那位厉声说，他整治家仆向来一步到位。「苏珊，苏珊，苏珊。……新来的客厅女仆。」

===Original===
“Oh, yes, sir. Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
「哦，是，先生。遵命，先生。」

===Original===
Thomas withdrew, outwardly all grave respectfulness, inwardly piqued, as was his wont, at the airy manner in which the secretary flung his orders about at the castle. The domestic staff at Blandings lived in a perpetual state of smouldering discontent under Baxter’s rule.

===Chinese===
托马斯退了出去，表面上一派庄重的恭谨，心里照例来气——秘书在城堡里发号施令的那副轻狂做派，向来叫他愤愤。布兰丁斯的家仆，在巴克斯特治下过着一种文火慢炖、经年不熄的憋屈日子。

===Original===
“Susan,” said Thomas when he arrived in the lower regions, “you’re to go up to the drawing-room. Nosey Parker wants you.”

===Chinese===
「苏珊，」托马斯下到仆役区便说，「让你上客厅去。包打听大爷找你。」

===Original===
The pleasant-faced young woman whom he addressed laid down her knitting.

===Chinese===
他招呼的那位脸庞讨喜的年轻姑娘放下了针线。

===Original===
“Who?” she asked.

===Chinese===
「谁？」她问。

===Original===
“Mister Blooming Baxter. When you’ve been here a little longer you’ll know that he’s the feller that owns the place. How he got it I don’t know. Found it,” said Thomas satirically, “in his Christmas stocking, I expect. Anyhow, you’re to go up.”

===Chinese===
「 开花儿的巴克斯特先生。你在这儿待久点就知道，这地方归他所有。怎么到手的我不清楚。圣诞袜子里捡的，」托马斯讥诮地说，「八成。总之，让你上去。」

===Original===
Thomas’s fellow-footman, Stokes, a serious-looking man with a bald forehead, shook that forehead solemnly.

===Chinese===
托马斯的同事男仆斯托克斯，一位前额光秃、面相严肃的人，庄严地摇了摇那片前额。

===Original===
“Something’s the matter,” he asserted. “You can’t tell me that wasn’t a scream we heard when them lights was out. Or,” he added weightily, for he was a man who looked at every side of a question, “a shriek. It was a shriek or scream. I said so at the time. ‘There,’ I said, ‘listen!’ I said. ‘That’s somebody screaming,’ I said. ‘Or shrieking.’ Something’s up.”

===Chinese===
「出事了，」他断言。「你可别说刚才灭灯那阵子咱们听见的那声不是尖叫。要不，」他加重语气补上一句——他是那种每个问题都要看足四面的人——「是尖嚎。是尖嚎或尖叫。当时我就这么说的。『瞧，』我说，『听！』我说。『有人在尖叫，』我说。『或者尖嚎。』出事了。」

===Original===
“Well, Baxter hasn’t been murdered, worse luck,” said Thomas. “He’s up there screaming or shrieking for Susan. ‘Send Susan to me!’ ” proceeded Thomas, giving an always popular imitation. “ ‘Susan, Susan, Susan.’ So you’d best go, my girl, and see what he wants.”

===Chinese===
「反正巴克斯特没被谋杀，倒霉就倒霉在这儿，」托马斯说。「他正在上头为苏珊尖叫或尖嚎呢。『叫苏珊来见我！』」托马斯接着表演起这出永远叫座的模仿，「『苏珊，苏珊，苏珊。』所以你最好去一趟，大姑娘，看看他要什么。」

===Original===
“Very well.”

===Chinese===
「好吧。」

===Original===
“And, Susan,” said Thomas, a tender note creeping into his voice, for already, brief as had been her sojourn at Blandings, he had found the new parlourmaid making a deep impression on him, “if it’s a row of any kind﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「还有，苏珊，」托马斯说，嗓音里爬进一丝温柔——虽说她来布兰丁斯的日子还短，这位新客厅女仆已在他心头刻下了深深的印记，「要是他不管为什么跟你发狠……」

===Original===
“Or description,” interjected Stokes.

===Chinese===
「或者发横，」斯托克斯插嘴。

===Original===
“Or description,” continued Thomas, accepting the word, “if ’e’s ’arsh with you for some reason or other, you come right back to me and sob out your troubles on my chest, see? Lay your little ’ead on my shoulder and tell me all about it.”

===Chinese===
「或者发横，」托马斯从善如流地接过这个词，「你就径直回来找我，趴在我胸口把委屈哭出来，明白不？把你那颗小脑袋靠上我的肩膀，一五一十讲给我听。」

===Original===
The new parlourmaid, primly declining to reply to this alluring invitation, started on her journey upstairs; and Thomas, with a not unmanly sigh, resumed his interrupted game of halfpenny nap with colleague Stokes.

===Chinese===
新客厅女仆端端正正地谢绝了这项诱人的邀请，动身上楼；托马斯以一声不失男子气概的叹息，重新捡起与同事斯托克斯打了一半的半便士纳普牌局。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The Efficient Baxter had gone to the open window and was gazing out into the night when Susan entered the drawing-room.

===Chinese===
能干的巴克斯特已踱到敞开的窗前，正凝望夜色，苏珊进了客厅。

===Original===
“You wished to see me, Mr. Baxter?”

===Chinese===
「您找我，巴克斯特先生？」

===Original===
The secretary spun round. So softly had she opened the door, and so noiselessly had she moved when inside the room, that it was not until she spoke that he had become aware of her arrival. It was a characteristic of this girl Susan that she was always apt to be among those present some time before the latter became cognisant of the fact.

===Chinese===
秘书霍地转身。她开门那样轻，进屋后移动那样无声，直到她开口他才察觉人已到了。这位苏珊姑娘的一大特性，便是总能比众人早到多时，众人才后知后觉。

===Original===
“Oh, good evening, Miss Simmons. You came in very quietly.”

===Chinese===
「哦，晚上好，西蒙斯小姐。你进来真轻。」

===Original===
“Habit,” said the parlourmaid.

===Chinese===
「习惯，」客厅女仆说。

===Original===
“You gave me quite a start.”

===Chinese===
「吓了我一跳。」

===Original===
“I’m sorry. What was it,” she asked, dismissing in a positively unfeeling manner the subject of her companion’s jarred nerves, “that you wished to see me about?”

===Chinese===
「抱歉。那么，」她问，以一种全无同情心的干脆撂开同伴受惊的神经这个话题，「您找我什么事？」

===Original===
“Shut that door.”

===Chinese===
「把门关上。」

===Original===
“I have. I always shut doors.”

===Chinese===
「关了。我从来都关门。」

===Original===
“Please sit down.”

===Chinese===
「请坐。」

===Original===
“No, thank you, Mr. Baxter. It might look odd if anyone should come in.”

===Chinese===
「不谢谢，巴克斯特先生。万一有人进来，看着不像话。」

===Original===
“Of course. You think of everything.”

===Chinese===
「当然。你什么都想到了。」

===Original===
“I always do.”

===Chinese===
「我一向如此。」

===Original===
Baxter stood for a moment, frowning.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特皱着眉站了片刻。

===Original===
“Miss Simmons,” he said, “when I thought it expedient to install a private detective in this house, I insisted on Wragge’s sending you. We had worked together before﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「西蒙斯小姐，」他说，「当我认定有必要在这宅子里安插一名私家侦探时，我坚持要拉格事务所派你来。我们从前共过事……」

===Original===
“Sixteenth of December, 1918, to January twelve, 1919, when you were secretary to Mr. Horace Jevons, the American millionaire,” said Miss Simmons as promptly as if he had touched a spring. It was her hobby to remember dates with precision.

===Chinese===
「1918 年 12 月 16 日至 1919 年 1 月 12 日，您当时是美国百万富翁霍勒斯·杰文斯先生的秘书，」西蒙斯小姐应声答道，利落得像他碰响了某根发条。精确记日期是她的癖好。

===Original===
“Exactly. I insisted upon your being sent because I knew from experience that you were reliable. At that time I looked on your presence here merely as a precautionary measure. Now, I am sorry to say﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「正是。我坚持派你来，因为我凭经验知道你可靠。当时我把你的在场仅视作一项预防措施。而现在，我很遗憾……」

===Original===
“Did someone steal Lady Constance’s necklace tonight?”

===Chinese===
「今晚是不是有人偷了康斯坦丝夫人的项链？」

===Original===
“Yes!”

===Chinese===
「是！」

===Original===
“When the lights went out just now?”

===Chinese===
「就是刚才灭灯那会儿？」

===Original===
“Exactly.”

===Chinese===
「正是。」

===Original===
“Well, why couldn’t you say so at once? Good gracious, man, you don’t have to break the thing gently to me.”

===Chinese===
「那你何必吞吞吐吐？天哪，伙计，跟我用不着把这事儿掰开了慢慢说。」

===Original===
The Efficient Baxter, though he strongly objected to being addressed as “man,” decided to overlook the solecism.

===Chinese===
能干的巴克斯特虽极不喜欢被唤作「伙计」，决定不计较这个失礼。

===Original===
“The lights suddenly went out,” he said. “There was a certain amount of laughter and confusion. Then a piercing shriek﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「灯突然灭了，」他说。「起了一阵哄笑和混乱。接着一声刺耳的尖叫……」

===Original===
“I heard it.”

===Chinese===
「我听见了。」

===Original===
“And immediately after Lady Constance’s voice crying that her jewels had been snatched from her neck.”

===Chinese===
「紧接着是康斯坦丝夫人的喊声，说她的珠宝被人从脖子上薅走了。」

===Original===
“Then what happened?”

===Chinese===
「然后呢？」

===Original===
“Still greater confusion, which lasted until one of the maids arrived with a candle. Eventually the lights went on again, but of the necklace there was no sign whatever.”

===Chinese===
「乱上加乱，直到有个女仆取来蜡烛。后来灯又亮了，可项链踪影全无。」

===Original===
“Well? Were you expecting the thief to wear it as a watch-chain or hang it from his teeth?”

===Chinese===
「哦？你还指望那贼把它当表链戴、用牙叼着不成？」

===Original===
Baxter was finding his companion’s manner more trying every minute, but he preserved his calm.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特发觉同伴的态度一分钟比一分钟难受，但保持着镇静。

===Original===
“Naturally the doors were barred and a complete search instituted. And extremely embarrassing it was. With the single exception of the scoundrel who has been palming himself off as McTodd, all those present were well-known members of Society.”

===Chinese===
「门自然都闩上了，进行了彻底搜查。尴尬之极。在场诸君，除了那个顶着麦克托德名头招摇的恶棍，全是社会上素有声望的人士。」

===Original===
“Well-known members of Society might not object to getting hold of a twenty-thousand pound necklace. But still, with the McTodd fellow there, you oughtn’t to have had far to look. What had he to say about it?”

===Chinese===
「素有声望的人士未必不想要一条两万镑的项链。不过，既有麦克托德那家伙在场，你本不用远寻。他怎么说？」

===Original===
“He was among the first to empty his pockets.”

===Chinese===
「他属于头一批掏空自己口袋的。」

===Original===
“Well, then, he must have hidden the thing somewhere.”

===Chinese===
「那么，他一定把东西藏哪儿了。」

===Original===
“Not in this room. I have searched assiduously.”

===Chinese===
「这间屋里没有。我掘地三尺搜过了。」

===Original===
“H’m.”

===Chinese===
「哼。」

===Original===
There was a silence.

===Chinese===
一阵沉默。

===Original===
“It is baffling,” said Baxter, “baffling.”

===Chinese===
「扑朔迷离，」巴克斯特说，「扑朔迷离。」

===Original===
“It is nothing of the kind,” replied Miss Simmons tartly. “This wasn’t a one-man job. How could it have been? I should be inclined to call it a three-man job. One to switch off the lights, one to snatch the necklace, and one to﻿—was that window open all the time? I thought so﻿—and one to pick up the necklace when the second fellow threw it out on to the terrace.”

===Chinese===
「一点不迷，」西蒙斯小姐尖刻地答。「这不是一人干的活。怎么可能？我倾向于称之为三人活。一个拉闸，一个薅项链，还有一个——那窗户当时一直开着？我料到了——在第二人把东西扔上露台后拾取。」

===Original===
“Terrace!”

===Chinese===
「露台！」

===Original===
The word shot from Baxter’s lips with explosive force. Miss Simmons looked at him curiously.

===Chinese===
这个词从巴克斯特唇间迸出，带着爆破的力道。西蒙斯小姐好奇地看着他。

===Original===
“Thought of something?”

===Chinese===
「想起什么了？」

===Original===
“Miss Simmons,” said the Efficient One impressively, “everybody was assembled in here waiting for the reading to begin, but the pseudo-McTodd was nowhere to be found. I discovered him eventually on the terrace in close talk with the Halliday girl.”

===Chinese===
「西蒙斯小姐，」能干的那位郑重地说，「大家齐集此地候着朗诵开场时，那位伪麦克托德却踪影全无。我最后是在露台上发现他的，正与哈利迪那丫头密谈。」

===Original===
“His partner,” said Miss Simmons, nodding. “We thought so all along. And let me add my little bit. There’s a fellow down in the servants’ hall that calls himself a valet, and I’ll bet he didn’t know what a valet was till he came here. I thought he was a crook the moment I set eyes on him. I can tell ’em in the dark. Now, do you know whose valet he is? This McTodd fellow’s!”

===Chinese===
「他的同伙，」西蒙斯小姐点头说。「我们从头就这么看。再添我一小块：楼下仆役食堂里有个自称贴身男仆的家伙，我打赌他来这儿之前连贴身男仆是干什么的都不知道。我一眼就看出他是个歪门邪道的。黑地里我也认得出。那么，你可知道他是谁的贴身男仆？正是这位麦克托德家伙的！」

===Original===
Baxter bounded to and fro like a caged tiger.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特像笼中虎般蹦来跳去。

===Original===
“And with my own ears,” he cried excitedly, “I heard the Halliday girl refuse to come to the drawing-room to listen to the reading. She was out on the terrace throughout the whole affair. Miss Simmons, we must act! We must act!”

===Chinese===
「而且我亲耳听见，」他激动地喊，「哈利迪那丫头拒绝来客厅听朗诵。整场事里她都在外头露台上。西蒙斯小姐，我们必得行动！必得行动！」

===Original===
“Yes, but not like idiots,” replied the detective frostily.

===Chinese===
「对，但不能像蠢货那样，」女侦探冷冰冰地答。

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
「什么意思？」

===Original===
“Well, you can’t charge out, as you looked as if you wanted to just then, and denounce these crooks where they sit. We’ve got to go carefully.”

===Chinese===
「你总不能像你方才那副架势，冲出去当着他们的坐席揭发这伙人。咱们得稳着来。」

===Original===
“But meanwhile they will smuggle the necklace away!”

===Chinese===
「可这当口他们要把项链偷运走了！」

===Original===
“They won’t smuggle any necklace away, not while I’m around. Suspicion’s no good. We’ve made out a nice little case against the three of them, but it’s no use unless we catch them with the goods. The first thing we have to do is to find out where they’ve hidden the stuff. And that’ll take patience. I’ll start by searching that girl’s room. Then I’ll search the valet fellow’s room. And if the stuff isn’t there, it’ll mean they’ve hidden it out in the open somewhere.”

===Chinese===
「运不走，只要我还在。光靠疑心没用。咱们给这三位织了套漂亮的小案子，可拿不到人赃俱获就白搭。头一件要办的，是查出东西藏在哪儿。这得有耐性。我先搜那丫头的房间。再搜贴身男仆那家伙的房间。东西若不在那儿，就说明他们藏到外头什么地方了。」

===Original===
“But this McTodd fellow. This fellow who poses as McTodd. He may have it all the while.”

===Chinese===
「可这位麦克托德家伙。这个冒充麦克托德的家伙。东西也许一直就在他手里。」

===Original===
“No. I’ll search his room, too, but the stuff won’t be there. He’s the fellow who’s going to get it in the end, because he’s got that place out in the woods to hide it in. But they wouldn’t have had time to slip it to him yet. That necklace is somewhere right here. And if,” said Miss Simmons with grim facetiousness, “they can hide it from me, they may keep it as a birthday present.”

===Chinese===
「不会。他的房间我也要搜，但东西不会在。他是最后拿东西的主儿，因为他有林子里那个地方可藏。可他们还没来得及把货递到他手上。项链就在这一带。要是，」西蒙斯小姐冷着脸打趣道，「他们能把它藏过我的眼，那就留着当生日礼物吧。」

### II / II

===Original===
How wonderful, if we pause to examine it, is Nature’s inexorable law of compensation. Instead of wasting time in envy of our mental superiors, we would do well to reflect that these gifts of theirs which excite our wistful jealousy are ever attended by corresponding penalties. To take an example that lies to hand, it was the very fact that he possessed a brain like a buzz-saw that rendered the Efficient Baxter a bad sleeper. Just as he would be dropping off, bing! would go that brain of his, melting the mists of sleep like snow in a furnace.

===Chinese===
细想起来，大自然的补偿法则何其精妙无情的公平。与其把光阴虚掷在嫉妒比我们聪明的人上，不如细想一层：他们那些惹我们眼热的天赋，无不伴生着对等的代价。信手拈一个例子：正因为能干的巴克斯特长着一副电锯般的头脑，他才睡不好觉。每每一合眼，嘣！那副头脑便开动，把睡意的薄雾像炉膛里的雪一样熔尽。

===Original===
This was so even when life was running calmly for him and without excitement. Tonight, his mind, bearing the load it did, firmly declined even to consider the question of slumber. The hour of two, chiming from the clock over the stables, found him as wide awake as ever he was at high noon.

===Chinese===
生活风平浪静、毫无波澜时已是如此。今夜，他头脑里驮着这副重担，干脆拒绝考虑入睡的问题。马厩上方的钟敲两点时，他还像正午十二点一样清醒。

===Original===
Lying in bed in the darkness, he reviewed the situation as far as he had the data. Shortly before he retired, Miss Simmons had made her report about the bedrooms. Though subjected to the severest scrutiny, neither Psmith’s boudoir nor Cootes’s attic nor Eve’s little nook on the third floor had yielded up treasure of any description. And this, Miss Simmons held, confirmed her original view that the necklace must be lying concealed in what might almost be called a public spot﻿—on some window-ledge, maybe, or somewhere in the hall.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他躺在床上，就着黑暗，把手头的数据所能支撑的局势通盘检阅了一遍。就寝前不久，西蒙斯小姐交了卧室方面的报告：经最严苛的检视，皮史密斯的小闺房、库茨的顶楼间、伊芙在三楼的小窝，一概未曾吐出任何形态的财宝。这一点，西蒙斯小姐认为，证实了她的原有判断：项链必定藏在几乎可称公共场所的什么所在——兴许哪个窗台上，或者大厅里的什么地方。……

===Original===
Baxter lay considering this theory. It did appear to be the only tenable one; but it offended him by giving the search a frivolous suggestion of being some sort of round game like Hunt the Slipper or Find the Thimble. As a child he had held austerely aloof from these silly pastimes, and he resented being compelled to play them now. Still﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
巴克斯特躺着，把这套理论翻来覆去。它似乎确是唯一站得住的一套；但令他着恼的是，它使这场搜索染上了瞎胡闹的意味，仿佛「找拖鞋」「寻顶针」一类的围坐游戏。孩提时代他便严峻地远离这类傻玩意，如今被迫奉陪，心中不平。不过……

===Original===
He sat up, thinking. He had heard a noise.

===Chinese===
他坐起身，侧耳。他听见了一个响动。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The attitude of the majority of people towards noises in the night is one of cautious noninterference. But Rupert Baxter was made of sterner stuff. The sound had seemed to come from downstairs somewhere﻿—perhaps from that very hall where, according to Miss Simmons, the stolen necklace might even now be lying hid. Whatever it was, it must certainly not be ignored. He reached for the spectacles which lay ever ready to his hand on the table beside him: then climbed out of bed, and, having put on a pair of slippers and opened the door, crept forth into the darkness. As far as he could ascertain by holding his breath and straining his ears, all was still from cellar to roof; but nevertheless he was not satisfied. He continued to listen. His room was on the second floor, one of a series that ran along a balcony overlooking the hall; and he stood, leaning over the balcony rail, a silent statue of Vigilance.

===Chinese===
多数人对夜间响动的态度，是谨慎的不干预。但鲁珀特·巴克斯特是更硬的材料铸的。那声音像是从楼下什么地方传来——也许正出自西蒙斯小姐断言失窃项链此刻可能窝藏的大厅。不管是什么，绝不可置之不理。他伸手够到随时搁在手边桌上的眼镜：随即爬下床，趿上一双拖鞋，开了门，潜入黑暗。他屏息侧耳，尽力探测，从地窖到屋顶一片寂静；可他还不放心，继续听。他的房间在二层，是俯瞰大厅的那排阳台房之一；他立在阳台栏杆上，探身俯望，一尊沉默的警戒之像。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The noise which had acted so electrically upon the Efficient Baxter had been a particularly noisy noise; and only the intervening distance and the fact that his door was closed had prevented it sounding to him like an explosion. It had been caused by the crashing downfall of a small table containing a vase, a jar of potpourri, an Indian sandalwood box of curious workmanship, and a cabinet-size photograph of the Earl of Emsworth’s eldest son, Lord Bosham; and the table had fallen because Eve, en route across the hall in quest of her precious flowerpot, had collided with it while making for the front door. Of all indoor sports﻿—and Eve, as she stood pallidly among the ruins, would have been the first to endorse this dictum﻿—the one which offers the minimum of pleasure to the participant is that of roaming in pitch darkness through the hall of a country-house. Easily navigable in the daytime, these places become at night mere traps for the unwary.

===Chinese===
在能干的巴克斯特身上起了这般电击之效的响动，实在是一记响动中的响动；若非隔着距离又关着门，听上去必似一场爆炸。肇因是一张小桌轰然倾覆——桌上原有花瓶一只、干香花一罐、工艺奇巧的印度檀香木盒一只，还有埃姆斯沃思伯爵长子博瑟姆勋爵的内阁照一幅；桌子之所以倒，是因为伊芙为寻她的宝贝花盆横穿大厅、直奔前门时，一头撞上了它。一切室内运动之中——伊芙面无人色地立在废墟中间，必是头一个赞成这句断言的——最不给参与者半分乐趣的，莫过于摸着纯黑穿越乡间宅邸的大厅。这些地方白天通行无碍，入夜便纯粹是防不胜防的陷阱。

===Original===
Eve paused breathlessly. So terrific had the noise sounded to her guilty ears that every moment she was expecting doors to open all over the castle, belching forth shouting men with pistols. But as nothing happened, courage returned to her, and she resumed her journey. She found the great door, ran her fingers along its surface, and drew the chain. The shooting back of the bolts occupied but another instant, and then she was out on the terrace running her hardest towards the row of flowerpots.

===Chinese===
伊芙气喘吁吁地停住。那声巨响在她做贼心虚的耳朵听来惊天动地，她时刻等着全堡的门一齐洞开，喷出持枪呐喊的汉子。结果毫无动静，勇气还了魂，她重又上路。她摸到大门，手指顺着门面滑过去，拉开门链。退闩不过又是一瞬，随后她已置身露台，朝着那排花盆全力飞奔。

===Original===
Up on his balcony, meanwhile, the Efficient Baxter was stopping, looking, and listening. The looking brought no results, for all below was black as pitch; but the listening proved more fruitful. Faintly from down in the well of the hall there floated up to him a peculiar sound like something rustling in the darkness. Had he reached the balcony a moment earlier, he would have heard the rattle of the chain and the click of the bolts; but these noises had occurred just before he came out of his room. Now all that was audible was this rustling.

===Chinese===
与此同时，二层阳台上，能干的巴克斯特正在驻足、张望、聆听。张望一无所获，楼下一团漆黑；聆听却较有收成。大厅深井里，隐隐浮上来一缕奇特的窸窣声，像什么东西在暗处蹭动。他若早一步到阳台，本可听见门链哗啦、门闩咔哒；可这些声响恰恰发生在他出房之前。此刻听得见的，只有这阵窸窣。

===Original===
He could not analyse the sound, but the fact that there was any sound at all in such a place at such an hour increased his suspicions that dark doings were toward which would pay for investigation. With stealthy steps he crept to the head of the stairs and descended.

===Chinese===
他辨不出这声响，但此地此时居然有声响这一事实，加深了他的疑心：黑暗中正在进行的勾当，值得侦查。他蹑手蹑脚挪到楼梯口，向下——

===Original===
One uses the verb “descend” advisedly, for what is required is some word suggesting instantaneous activity. About Baxter’s progress from the second floor to the first there was nothing halting or hesitating. He, so to speak, did it now. Planting his foot firmly on a golf-ball which the Hon. Freddie Threepwood, who had been practising putting in the corridor before retiring to bed, had left in his casual fashion just where the steps began, he took the entire staircase in one majestic, volplaning sweep. There were eleven stairs in all separating his landing from the landing below, and the only ones he hit were the third and tenth. He came to rest with a squattering thud on the lower landing, and for a moment or two the fever of the chase left him.

===Chinese===
「下」字用得斟酌，因为这里需要的是一个意味着瞬间动作的词。巴克斯特从二层到一层的进程，没有半点迟疑滞涩。他可以说是一步到位。他一脚踏实了弗雷迪·思里普伍德少爷临睡前在走廊练推杆、随手撂在梯口的高尔夫球，以一个雄浑的滑翔弧线，把整段楼梯一气呵成。从他的楼梯口到下一层楼梯口，共十一级，他踩中的只有第三级和第十级。他噗通一声墩在下层楼梯口，好一阵，追索的热血离他而去。

===Original===
The fact that many writers in their time have commented at some length on the mysterious manner in which Fate is apt to perform its work must not deter us now from a brief survey of this latest manifestation of its ingenious methods. Had not his interview with Eve that afternoon so stimulated the Hon. Freddie as to revive in him a faint yet definite desire to putt, there would have been no golf-ball waiting for Baxter on the stairs. And had he been permitted to negotiate the stairs in a less impetuous manner, Baxter would not at this juncture have switched on the light.

===Chinese===
历来多少作家就命运行事的神秘方式大发过议论，这一事实不应妨碍我们如今扼要检视它最新一招的神妙手法。倘若那天下午与伊芙的一晤不曾把弗雷迪少爷刺激得复活了一丝虽淡却实的推杆欲，楼梯上便不会有高尔夫球恭候巴克斯特。倘若他获准以不那么鲁莽的方式下楼梯，巴克斯特在此节骨眼上便不会开灯。

===Original===
It had not been his original intention to illuminate the theatre of action, but after that Lucifer-like descent from the second floor to the first he was taking no more chances. “Safety First” was Baxter’s slogan. As soon, therefore, as he had shaken off a dazed sensation of mental and moral collapse, akin to that which comes to the man who steps on the teeth of a rake and is smitten on the forehead by the handle, he rose with infinite caution to his feet and, feeling his way down by the banisters, groped for the switch and pressed it. And so it came about that Eve, heading for home with her precious flowerpot in her arms, was stopped when at the very door by a sudden warning flood of light. Another instant, and she would have been across the threshold of disaster.

===Chinese===
照亮战场本非他的初衷，但经了那趟从二层到一层的路西法式俯冲，他不再冒任何风险。「安全第一」是巴克斯特的口号。因此，等他把那阵类似踩上耙齿、被耙柄拍中前额的晕眩甩掉之后，他无比小心地起身，扶着栏杆摸下去，摸到开关，一按。于是，怀抱宝贝花盆、正朝家门口进发的伊芙，在门槛上被一道突如其来的警告性光浪钉住了。再有一瞬，她就跨过灾难的门槛了。

===Original===
For a moment paralysis gripped her. The light had affected her like someone shouting loudly and unexpectedly in her ear. Her heart gave one convulsive bound, and she stood frozen. Then, filled with a blind desire for flight, she dashed like a hunted rabbit into the friendly shelter of a clump of bushes.

===Chinese===
一时间她瘫住了。那光之作用于她，如同有人在她耳边猛然高呼。她的心痉挛似的一跳，人僵在原地。随即，盲目的逃窜欲灌满全身，她像一只被追猎的兔子，窜进了近旁一丛灌木的友善掩体。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Baxter stood blinking. Gradually his eyes adjusted themselves to the light, and immediately they had done so he was seized by a fresh frenzy of zeal. Now that all things were made visible to him he could see that that faint rustling sound had been caused by a curtain flapping in the breeze, and that the breeze which made the curtain flap was coming in through the open front door.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特站在那儿直眨眼。他的眼睛渐渐适应了光，一适应，立刻又被一股新的狂热攫住。如今万物皆现于眼前，他看明白了：方才那缕轻轻的窸窣，是帘子迎风摆动，而吹动帘子的风，是从敞开的前门灌进来的。

===Original===
Baxter wasted no time in abstract thought. He acted swiftly and with decision. Straightening his spectacles on his nose, he girded up his pyjamas and galloped out into the night.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特不浪费工夫于抽象思考。他行动迅捷果决。他把眼镜在鼻梁上架正，束好睡衣裤，冲进夜色。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The smooth terrace slept under the stars. To a more poetic man than Baxter it would have seemed to wear that faintly reproachful air which a garden always assumes when invaded at unseemly hours by people who ought to be in bed. Baxter, never fanciful, was blind to this. He was thinking, thinking. That shaking-up on the stairs had churned into activity the very depths of his brain and he was at the fever-point of his reasoning powers. A thought had come like a full-blown rose, flushing his brow. Miss Simmons, arguing plausibly, had suggested that the stolen necklace might be concealed in the hall. Baxter, inspired, fancied not. Whoever it was that had been at work in the hall just now had been making for the garden. It was not the desire to escape which had led him﻿—or her﻿—to open the front door, for the opening had been done before he, Baxter, had come out on to the balcony﻿—otherwise he must have heard the shooting of the bolts. No. The enemy’s objective had been the garden. In other words, the terrace. And why? Because somewhere on the terrace was the stolen necklace.

===Chinese===
平滑的露台在星空下沉睡。换一位比巴克斯特多几分诗意的，会觉得它带着花园在不当之时被本该就寝之人侵扰时总有的那副幽幽的责备之色。从不多愁善感的巴克斯特对此视而不见。他在想，想。楼梯上那顿颠簸把他脑子最深处的东西全颠活了，他的推理力正处在沸点。一个念头像一朵盛放的玫瑰轰然绽开，红透了他的额头。西蒙斯小姐言之成理，推测失窃的项链可能藏在大厅。巴克斯特灵光一闪，不以为然。方才在大厅活动的那个——或那个女人——奔的是花园。开门不是为了逃走，因为门是他巴克斯特上阳台之前就开好的——不然门闩声他必已听见。不。敌人的目标是花园。换言之，露台。而为什么？因为失窃的项链就在露台上的什么地方。

===Original===
Standing there in the starlight, the Efficient Baxter endeavoured to reconstruct the scene, and did so with remarkable accuracy. He saw the jewels flashing down. He saw them picked up. But there he stopped. Try as he might, he could not see them hidden. And yet that they had been hidden﻿—and that within a few feet of where he was now standing﻿—he felt convinced.

===Chinese===
立在星光下，能干的巴克斯特努力复原现场，而且复原得惊人地准。他看见珠宝火光四溅地坠落。他看见有人拾起。但到此为止。任凭他怎么想，也想不出藏于何处。然而它们已被藏起——而且就在离他此刻所站之地几英尺之内——这一点他深信不疑。

===Original===
He moved from his position near the door and began to roam restlessly. His slippered feet padded over the soft turf.

===Chinese===
他离开门前位置，开始焦躁地逡巡。拖鞋的双脚在软草皮上噗噗作响。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Eve peered out from her clump of bushes. It was not easy to see any great distance, but Fate, her friend, was still with her.
