There had been a moment that night when Baxter, disrobing for bed, had wavered absently between his brown and his lemon-coloured pyjamas, little recking of what hung upon the choice. Fate had directed his hand to the lemon-coloured, and he had put them on; with the result that he shone now in the dim light like the white plume of Navarre. Eve could follow his movements perfectly, and, when he was far enough away from his base to make the enterprise prudent, she slipped out and raced for home and safety. Baxter at the moment was leaning on the terrace wall, thinking, thinking, thinking.

===Chinese===
伊芙从灌木丛里窥探。要看多远不容易，但她的朋友命运仍与她同在。那晚就寝前，巴克斯特脱衣时曾在棕色睡衣与柠檬色睡衣之间心不在焉地摇摆，浑不知多少事系于这一选择。命运把他的手引向了柠檬色那套，他便穿上了；结果此刻，他在微光里亮得像纳瓦拉的白翎。伊芙把他的行动看得清清楚楚；等他离他的基地够远、行动已是上策时，她溜出灌木，撒腿奔回家去。此刻巴克斯特正倚着露台墙，想，想，想。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
It was possibly the cool air, playing about his bare ankles, that at last chilled the secretary’s dashing mood and brought the disquieting thought that he was doing something distinctly dangerous in remaining out here in the open like this. A gang of thieves are ugly customers, likely to stick at little when a valuable necklace is at stake, and it came to the Efficient Baxter that in his light pyjamas he must be offering a tempting mark for any marauder lurking﻿—say in those bushes. And at the thought, the summer night, though pleasantly mild, grew suddenly chilly. With an almost convulsive rapidity he turned to reenter the house. Zeal was well enough, but it was silly to be rash. He covered the last few yards of his journey at a rare burst of speed.

===Chinese===
多半是掠过他裸踝的凉风，终于镇住了秘书的锐气，送来了那个不安的念头：这样敞在外头，实属冒险。盗窃团伙可不是善茬，为了一条值钱的项链，什么狠事都做得出来；能干的巴克斯特想到，自己这身轻薄睡衣，对任何潜伏的歹徒——比方说就潜伏在那丛灌木里——简直是个招人的靶子。这念头一起，夏夜虽温煦宜人，霎时冷了。他以近乎痉挛的迅捷转身回宅。热忱归热忱，鲁莽犯不上。他以罕见的一股冲刺速度跑完了归程的最后几码。

===Original===
It was at this point that he discovered that the lights in the hall had been switched off and that the front door was closed and bolted.

===Chinese===
正是在这一刻他发现：大厅的灯已被关掉，前门已闭，且上了闩。

### III / III

===Original===
It is the opinion of most thoughtful students of life that happiness in this world depends chiefly on the ability to take things as they come. An instance of one who may be said to have perfected this attitude is to be found in the writings of a certain eminent Arabian author who tells of a traveller who, sinking to sleep one afternoon upon a patch of turf containing an acorn, discovered when he woke that the warmth of his body had caused the acorn to germinate and that he was now some sixty feet above the ground in the upper branches of a massive oak. Unable to descend, he faced the situation equably. “I cannot,” he observed, “adapt circumstances to my will: therefore I shall adapt my will to circumstances. I decide to remain here.” Which he did.

===Chinese===
多数有心的人生研习者都认为：尘世的幸福，主要系于随遇而安的本事。某位杰出的阿拉伯作家笔下，记着一个把这份修养修炼到家的人：一个旅人午后睡在一块埋着橡子的草皮上，醒来发现体温催得橡子发了芽，自己已离地六十来英尺，高踞在一株参天橡树的顶梢。下不来了，他坦然处之。「我不能，」他说，「叫环境迁就我的意志：那便让我的意志迁就环境。我决定留在这儿。」他也真留下来了。

===Original===
Rupert Baxter, as he stood before the barred door of Blandings Castle, was very far from imitating this admirable philosopher. To find oneself locked out of a country-house at half-past two in the morning in lemon-coloured pyjamas can never be an unmixedly agreeable experience, and Baxter was a man less fitted by nature to endure it with equanimity than most men. His was a fiery and an arrogant soul, and he seethed in furious rebellion against the intolerable position into which Fate had manoeuvred him. He even went so far as to give the front door a petulant kick. Finding, however, that this hurt his toes and accomplished no useful end, he addressed himself to the task of ascertaining whether there was any way of getting in﻿—short of banging the knocker and rousing the house, a line of action which did not commend itself to him. He made a practice of avoiding as far as possible the ribald type of young man of which the castle was now full, and he had no desire to meet them at this hour in his present costume. He left the front door and proceeded to make a circuit of the castle walls; and his spirits sank even lower. In the Middle Ages, during that stormy period of England’s history when walls were built six feet thick and a window was not so much a window as a handy place for pouring molten lead on the heads of visitors, Blandings had been an impregnable fortress. But in all its career it can seldom have looked more of a fortress to anyone than it did now to the Efficient Baxter.

===Chinese===
鲁珀特·巴克斯特立在布兰丁斯城堡上了闩的大门前，离这位可敬的哲人差得远了不得。凌晨两点半，穿一身柠檬色睡衣被锁在乡间宅邸门外，这滋味任谁也痛快不起来；而较之常人，巴克斯特天生更不是安然忍受它的材料。他有一副火辣骄横的灵魂，对命运把他调度进的这个忍无可忍的处境，五内俱沸。他甚至抬脚朝大门赌气踢了一脚。发觉这一脚除了疼脚趾别无所成，便转而着手查明：除了抡门环叫醒全宅——这条路线他不取——还有没有别的进法。他素来尽量躲着满宅那路起哄的青年，无意在此刻、以此身装束与他们相会。他离开正门，沿城堡外墙巡行一周；情绪愈发低落。中世纪，英格兰历史上那段烽火岁月，墙砌六尺厚，窗户与其说是窗，不如说是往来访者头顶浇铅水的顺手设施，布兰丁斯曾是座攻不破的堡垒。但在它全部生涯里，怕是从没有在哪位眼里比此刻在能干的巴克斯特眼里更像堡垒的。

===Original===
One of the disadvantages of being a man of action, impervious to the softer emotions, is that in moments of trial the beauties of Nature are powerless to soothe the anguished heart. Had Baxter been of a dreamy and poetic temperament he might now have been drawing all sorts of balm from the loveliness of his surroundings. The air was full of the scent of growing things; strange, shy creatures came and went about him as he walked; down in the woods a nightingale had begun to sing; and there was something grandly majestic in the huge bulk of the castle as it towered against the sky. But Baxter had temporarily lost his sense of smell; he feared and disliked the strange, shy creatures; the nightingale left him cold; and the only thought the towering castle inspired in him was that it looked as if a fellow would need half a ton of dynamite to get into it.

===Chinese===
做一个行动家、绝缘于柔软情感的不便之一，是身处患难时，自然之美全然无力抚慰苦楚的心。巴克斯特若生得梦意诗情，此刻本可从四周的可爱中汲取各式安慰。空气里满是草木生长的芬芳；古怪而怕人的小生灵在他走动时来来去去；林子深处一只夜莺起了歌喉；城堡巨影衬天而立，自有一种堂皇的威严。可巴克斯特暂时丧失了嗅觉；他怕那些古怪而怕人的小生灵，还讨厌它们；夜莺于他如浮云；巍峨的城堡在他心里激起的唯一念头是：要进这宅子，怕得半吨炸药。

===Original===
Baxter paused. He was back now near the spot from which he had started, having completed two laps without finding any solution of his difficulties. The idea in his mind had been to stand under somebody’s window and attract the sleeper’s attention with soft, significant whistles. But the first whistle he emitted had sounded to him in the stillness of early morn so like a steam syren that thereafter he had merely uttered timid, mouse-like sounds which the breezes had carried away the moment they crept out. He proposed now to halt for awhile and rest his lips before making another attempt. He proceeded to the terrace wall and sat down. The clock over the stables struck three.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特驻足。他已绕回出发点附近，跑了两圈，难题无解。他心里的打算，是站到某人窗下，用轻柔而意味深长的口哨唤醒睡者。可他吹的头一声口哨，在凌晨的寂静里听着活像汽笛，吓得他此后只敢发出怯生生的鼠叫般的细声，风一吹便散。此刻他打算歇一歇，让嘴唇缓口气，再作打算。他踱到露台墙边坐下。马厩上方的钟敲了三下。

===Original===
To the restless type of thinker like Rupert Baxter, the act of sitting down is nearly always the signal for the brain to begin working with even more than its customary energy. The relaxed body seems to invite thought. And Baxter, having suspended for the moment his physical activities﻿—and glad to do so, for his slippers hurt him﻿—gave himself up to tense speculation as to the hiding-place of Lady Constance Keeble’s necklace. From the spot where he now sat he was probably, he reflected, actually in a position to see that hiding-place﻿—if only, when he saw it, he were able to recognise it for what it was. Somewhere out here﻿—in yonder bushes or in some unsuspected hole in yonder tree﻿—the jewels must have been placed. Or﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
对鲁珀特·巴克斯特这种躁动型思想家，坐下这个动作，几乎总是一声号令，号令大脑以超出惯常的能量开动。松弛的躯体仿佛在邀请思想。巴克斯特既已暂停了体力活动——也乐得暂停，拖鞋硌得他脚疼——便全神贯注地推演起康斯坦丝·基布尔夫人项链的藏所。从他此刻所坐之处，他想，说不定真能望见那个藏所——只消望见时认得出它是什么。东西必是放在外头这一带的什么地方——那边的灌木丛，或那边树上某个料想不到的洞里。要不……

===Original===
Something seemed to go off inside Baxter like a touched spring. One moment, he was sitting limply, keenly conscious of a blister on the sole of his left foot; the next, regardless of the blister, he was off the wall and racing madly along the terrace in a flurry of flying slippers. Inspiration had come to him.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特体内有什么像被触碰的弹簧般弹开了。前一瞬他还软软地坐着，敏锐地感觉到左脚掌一个水泡；后一瞬，水泡也顾不上了，他已翻身下墙，拖鞋横飞，沿露台狂奔。灵感来了。

===Original===
Day dawns early in the summer months, and already a sort of unhealthy pallor had begun to manifest itself in the sky. It was still far from light, but objects hitherto hidden in the gloom had begun to take on uncertain shape. And among these there had come into the line of Baxter’s vision a row of fifteen flowerpots.

===Chinese===
夏日天亮得早，天上已开始泛出一种病恹恹的鱼肚白。离天光大亮还远，但先前隐在昏暗里的物件，已开始现出恍惚的轮廓。而这些物件之中，有一排十五只花盆进入了巴克斯特的视界。

===Original===
There they stood, side by side, round and inviting, each with a geranium in its bed of mould. Fifteen flowerpots. There had originally been sixteen, but Baxter knew nothing of that. All he knew was that he was on the trail.

===Chinese===
它们列队立在那里，只挨肩并肩，圆滚滚，惹人下手，每盆土床上各栽着一株天竺葵。十五只花盆。原本是十六只，但这巴克斯特无从知晓。他只知道：线索到了他手上。

===Original===
The quest for buried treasure is one which right through the ages has exercised an irresistible spell over humanity. Confronted with a spot where buried treasure may lurk, men do not stand upon the order of their digging; they go at it with both hands. No solicitude for his employer’s geraniums came to hamper Rupert Baxter’s researches. To grasp the first flowerpot and tilt out its contents was with him the work of a moment. He scrabbled his fingers through the little pile of mould﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
寻宝这项事业，千百年来对人类施着不可抗拒的魔法。一临藏宝可能潜伏之地，人们绝不管什么先来后到；直接双手齐上。对东家天竺葵的怜惜，半点不曾拖累鲁珀特·巴克斯特的科研。抓起头一只花盆、倒净内容物，于他只是一瞬间的事。他的手指在那一小堆土里刨挠……

===Original===
Nothing.

===Chinese===
没有。

===Original===
A second geranium lay broken on the ground﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
第二株天竺葵横尸在地……

===Original===
Nothing.

===Chinese===
没有。

===Original===
A third﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
第三只……

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The Efficient Baxter straightened himself painfully. He was unused to stooping, and his back ached. But physical discomfort was forgotten in the agony of hope frustrated. As he stood there, wiping his forehead with an earth-stained hand, fifteen geranium corpses gazed up at him in the growing light, it seemed with reproach. But Baxter felt no remorse. He included all geraniums, all thieves, and most of the human race in one comprehensive black hatred.

===Chinese===
能干的巴克斯特艰难地直起腰。他不惯弯腰，腰背酸痛。可肉体这点苦楚，在希望落空的剧痛面前不值一提。他站在那儿，用一只沾泥的手抹着额头，十五具天竺葵的尸身在渐亮的晨光里仰望他，似乎含着责备。但巴克斯特毫无悔意。他把天下所有天竺葵、所有窃贼、以及人类的大多数，一并纳入一场无所不包的黑色憎恨。

===Original===
All that Rupert Baxter wanted in this world now was bed. The clock over the stables had just struck four, and he was aware of an overpowering fatigue. Somehow or other, if he had to dig through the walls with his bare hands, he must get into the house. He dragged himself painfully from the scene of carnage and blinked up at the row of silent windows above him. He was past whistling now. He stooped for a pebble, and tossed it up at the nearest window.

===Chinese===
此刻鲁珀特·巴克斯特在这世上唯一想要的是床。马厩上方的钟刚敲四点，一股压倒性的疲惫袭遍全身。不管怎么着——哪怕徒手刨穿墙壁——他也必须进宅。他从屠场惨状中一步一挪地离开，仰头朝头顶那排哑窗直眨眼。吹口哨他是不再吹了。他弯腰拾了颗石子，朝最近的一扇窗扔上去。

===Original===
Nothing happened. Whoever was sleeping up there continued to sleep. The sky had turned pink, birds were twittering in the ivy, other birds had begun to sing in the bushes. All Nature, in short, was waking﻿—except the unseen sluggard up in that room.

===Chinese===
毫无动静。上头睡着的那位照睡。天已转粉，常春藤里鸟声啁啾，灌木丛里又有别的鸟开了腔。总而言之，整个大自然都醒了一只除了那屋里那位看不见的懒虫。

===Original===
He threw another pebble﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他又扔了一颗石子……

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
It seemed to Rupert Baxter that he had been standing there throwing pebbles through a nightmare eternity. The whole universe had now become concentrated in his efforts to rouse that log-like sleeper; and for a brief instant fatigue left him, driven away by a sort of Berserk fury. And there floated into his mind, as if from some previous existence, a memory of somebody once standing near where he was standing now and throwing a flowerpot in at a window at someone. Who it was that had thrown the thing at whom, he could not at the moment recall; but the outstanding point on which his mind focused itself was the fact that the man had had the right idea. This was no time for pebbles. Pebbles were feeble and inadequate. With one voice the birds, the breezes, the grasshoppers, the whole chorus of Nature waking to another day seemed to shout to him, “Say it with flowerpots!”

===Chinese===
在鲁珀特·巴克斯特的感觉里，他仿佛已在梦魇的永恒里站了一世，扔了一世的石子。全宇宙如今浓缩成一件事：唤醒那位木头似的睡者；有一瞬，疲惫竟离他而去，被一种狂暴的战意驱散了。他脑际浮起一段仿佛前世的记忆：有人曾站在他此刻所站的地方附近，朝窗里的什么人扔进过一只花盆。是谁朝谁扔的，他一时想不起；但他脑子聚焦的要点只有一个：那人的思路是对的。此刻不是扔石子的时节。石子软弱，不顶事。鸟雀、微风、蚱蜢、醒来迎接新一天的整个大自然的合唱，仿佛齐声向他高呼：「用花盆说话！」

### IV / IV

===Original===
The ability to sleep soundly and deeply is the prerogative, as has been pointed out earlier in this straightforward narrative of the simple home-life of the English upper classes, of those who do not think quickly. The Earl of Emsworth, who had not thought quickly since the occasion in the summer of 1874 when he had heard his father’s footsteps approaching the stable-loft in which he, a lad of fifteen, sat smoking his first cigar, was an excellent sleeper. He started early and finished late. It was his gentle boast that for more than twenty years he had never missed his full eight hours. Generally he managed to get something nearer ten.

===Chinese===
正如这部关于英国上层阶级家常生活的直白叙事前文所指出的，酣睡之权，属于脑不快者。埃姆斯沃思伯爵自 1874 年夏天那个时刻起就没快过——那时他十五岁，正坐在草料棚里抽平生第一支雪茄，听见了父亲的脚步声——他是位杰出的睡眠家。开工早，收工晚。他温文的自夸是：二十多年来从不曾错失足额的八小时。通常他还能设法睡足十小时上下。

===Original===
But then, as a rule, people did not fling flowerpots through his window at four in the morning.

===Chinese===
不过，寻常日子里，没有人凌晨四点朝他窗户里扔花盆。

===Original===
Even under this unusual handicap, however, he struggled bravely to preserve his record. The first of Baxter’s missiles, falling on a settee, produced no change in his regular breathing. The second, which struck the carpet, caused him to stir. It was the third, colliding sharply with his humped back, that definitely woke him. He sat up in bed and stared at the thing.

===Chinese===
然而即便背着这份非常的劣势，他仍奋勇力保自己的纪录。巴克斯特的头一枚投掷物落进长沙发，没能扰动他匀停的呼吸。第二枚砸中地毯，叫他动了动。是第三枚，结结实实撞上他拱起的脊背，把他彻底打醒了。他在床上坐起，瞪着那东西。

===Original===
In the first moment of his waking, relief was, oddly enough, his chief emotion. The blow had roused him from a disquieting dream in which he had been arguing with Angus McAllister about early spring bulbs, and McAllister, worsted verbally, had hit him in the ribs with a spud. Even in his dream Lord Emsworth had been perplexed as to what his next move ought to be; and when he found himself awake and in his bedroom he was at first merely thankful that the necessity for making a decision had at any rate been postponed. Angus McAllister might on some future occasion smite him with a spud, but he had not done it yet.

===Chinese===
醒来的头一刻，说来奇怪，他的头号情绪竟是如释重负。这一击把他从一场不安的梦里救了出来——梦里他正同安格斯·麦卡利斯特争论早春球茎，麦卡利斯特词穷理屈，抡起起薯铲敲了他的肋骨。连在梦里，埃姆斯沃思勋爵都已为下一步棋发愁；待发觉自己醒着、且在卧室里，他起初只庆幸：决策的必要至少推迟了。安格斯·麦卡利斯特将来没准还会用起薯铲敲他，但毕竟还没敲。

===Original===
There followed a period of vague bewilderment. He looked at the flowerpot. It held no message for him. He had not put it there. He never took flowerpots to bed. Once, as a child, he had taken a dead pet rabbit, but never a flowerpot. The whole affair was completely inscrutable; and his lordship, unable to solve the mystery, was on the point of taking the statesmanlike course of going to sleep again, when something large and solid whizzed through the open window and crashed against the wall, where it broke, but not into such small fragments that he could not perceive that in its prime it, too, had been a flowerpot. And at this moment his eyes fell on the carpet and then on the settee; and the affair passed still farther into the realm of the inexplicable. The Hon. Freddie Threepwood, who had a poor singing-voice but was a game trier, had been annoying his father of late by crooning a ballad ending in the words:

===Chinese===
随后是一阵茫然的困惑。他看着那只花盆。它不向他传递任何信息。不是他放的。他从不带花盆上床。小时候带过一只死宠物兔，可从不带花盆。整个事件全然费解；勋爵大人解不开这个谜，正要采取政治家的办法——接着睡——忽然一样又大又实的东西嗖地穿窗而入，撞在墙上，碎了；碎得还不算太碎，足以让他认出它生前也是一只花盆。这一刻他的目光落到地毯上，又落到长沙发上；事件愈发进入了不可解的疆域。弗雷迪·思里普伍德少爷嗓子平平却屡败屡战，近来老哼一支结尾如下的小曲折磨他父亲：


===Original===
> “It is not raining rain at all:

===Chinese===
> 「天上落下的哪里是雨：




===Original===
 It’s raining vi-o-lets.”

===Chinese===
 落的原是紫——罗——兰。」


===Original===
It seemed to Lord Emsworth now that matters had gone a step farther. It was raining flowerpots.

===Chinese===
此刻在埃姆斯沃思勋爵看来，事态更进了一步。下的是花盆。

===Original===
The customary attitude of the Earl of Emsworth towards all mundane affairs was one of vague detachment; but this phenomenon was so remarkable that he found himself stirred to quite a little flutter of excitement and interest. His brain still refused to cope with the problem of why anybody should be throwing flowerpots into his room at this hour﻿—or, indeed, at any hour; but it seemed a good idea to go and ascertain who this peculiar person was.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思伯爵对一切尘世俗务的惯常态度，是一种茫然的超然；可这现象实在离奇，他发现自己竟被激起了一小阵兴奋与兴致。他的头脑依旧拒绝解答「何以有人在这个钟点朝他屋里扔花盆」——其实任何钟点也一样；但去看看这位怪人是谁，似乎是个不坏的主意。

===Original===
He put on his glasses and hopped out of bed and trotted to the window. And it was while he was on his way there that memory stirred in him, as some minutes ago it had stirred in the Efficient Baxter. He recalled that odd episode of a few days back, when that delightful girl, Miss What’s-her-name, had informed him that his secretary had been throwing flowerpots at that poet fellow, McTodd. He had been annoyed, he remembered, that Baxter should so far have forgotten himself. Now, he found himself more frightened than annoyed. Just as every dog is permitted one bite without having its sanity questioned, so, if you consider it in a broad-minded way, may every man be allowed to throw one flowerpot. But let the thing become a habit, and we look askance. This strange hobby of his appeared to be growing on Baxter like a drug, and Lord Emsworth did not like it at all. He had never before suspected his secretary of an unbalanced mind, but now he mused, as he tiptoed cautiously to the window, that the Baxter sort of man, the energetic restless type, was just the kind that does go off his head. Just some such calamity as this, his lordship felt, he might have foreseen. Day in, day out, Rupert Baxter had been exercising his brain ever since he had come to the castle﻿—and now he had gone and sprained it. Lord Emsworth peeped timidly out from behind a curtain.

===Chinese===
他戴上眼镜，跳下床，小跑奔向窗口。就在赶过去的路上，记忆在他心里翻了个身，如同几分钟前在能干的巴克斯特心里翻过那样。他记起几天前那桩怪事：那位怪讨人喜欢的姑娘，什么来着小姐，告诉过他，说他的秘书朝那位诗人老兄麦克托德扔花盆。他记得自己当时还着恼，恼巴克斯特如此忘形。此刻，他发现自己害怕多于着恼。正如每条狗都获准咬一口而不遭怀疑神志，只要你心胸开阔地想，每个男人也该获准扔一只花盆。可这一旦成了习惯，我们就要侧目了。他这怪癖像是抽大烟似的在巴克斯特身上越长越大，埃姆斯沃思勋爵一点不喜欢。他从没疑心过秘书心智失衡，可这会儿他踮着脚溜向窗口，一路寻思：巴克斯特这种人，精力充沛坐立不安的那种，恰恰是会发疯的那种。这一类祸事，勋爵大人觉得，自己本可预见的。鲁珀特·巴克斯特自打进城堡，日复一日地开动脑筋——如今把脑筋扭伤了。埃姆斯沃思勋爵怯生生地从帘子后头朝外窥。

===Original===
His worst fears were realised. It was Baxter, sure enough; and a tousled, wild-eyed Baxter incredibly clad in lemon-coloured pyjamas.

===Chinese===
他最坏的担忧成真了。正是巴克斯特，错不了；一个头发蓬乱、双眼暴凸的巴克斯特，不可思议地裹着一身柠檬色睡衣。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Lord Emsworth stepped back from the window. He had seen sufficient. The pyjamas had in some curious way set the coping-stone on his dismay, and he was now in a condition approximating to panic. That Baxter should be so irresistibly impelled by his strange mania as actually to omit to attire himself decently before going out on one of these flowerpot-hurling expeditions of his seemed to make it all so sad and hopeless. The dreamy peer was no poltroon, but he was past his first youth, and it came to him very forcibly that the interviewing and pacifying of secretaries who ran amok was young man’s work. He stole across the room and opened the door. It was his purpose to put this matter into the hands of an agent. And so it came about that Psmith was aroused some few minutes later from slumber by a touch on the arm and sat up to find his host’s pale face peering at him in the weird light of early morning.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵从窗前退开。他看够了。那身睡衣不知怎么给了他的惊惶最后一击，此刻他的状态接近恐慌。巴克斯特竟被怪癖驱迫到这般地步——出门扔花盆都来不及穿戴齐整——这使一切显得如此可悲，如此无望。这位梦一样的贵族并非懦夫，但他早过了盛年，而接见并安抚发狂的秘书，他痛感是年轻人的活计。他蹑足穿过房间，开了门。他的打算是把这事交给一位代办。于是几分钟后，皮史密斯被臂上一触唤醒，坐起来，看见东道那张苍白的脸在凌晨怪异的天光里窥视他。

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” quavered Lord Emsworth.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的伙计，」埃姆斯沃思勋爵颤声道。

===Original===
Psmith, like Baxter, was a light sleeper; and it was only a moment before he was wide awake and exerting himself to do the courtesies.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯同巴克斯特一样觉轻；不出片刻他便全醒，忙着尽主人之仪的礼数。

===Original===
“Good morning,” he said pleasantly. “Will you take a seat.”

===Chinese===
「早上好，」他愉快地说。「请坐。」

===Original===
“I am extremely sorry to be obliged to wake you, my dear fellow,” said his lordship, “but the fact of the matter is, my secretary, Baxter, has gone off his head.”

===Chinese===
「万分抱歉不得不叫醒你，亲爱的伙计，」勋爵大人说，「可是实情是这样：我的秘书巴克斯特，疯了。」

===Original===
“Much?” inquired Psmith, interested.

===Chinese===
「疯得厉害吗？」皮史密斯饶有兴致地问。

===Original===
“He is out in the garden in his pyjamas, throwing flowerpots through my window.”

===Chinese===
「他穿着睡衣在花园里，朝我窗户里扔花盆。」

===Original===
“Flowerpots?”

===Chinese===
「花盆？」

===Original===
“Flowerpots!”

===Chinese===
「花盆！」

===Original===
“Oh, flowerpots!” said Psmith, frowning thoughtfully, as if he had expected it would be something else. “And what steps are you proposing to take? That is to say,” he went on, “unless you wish him to continue throwing flowerpots.”

===Chinese===
「哦，花盆！」皮史密斯说，若有所思地皱眉，仿佛原以为会是别的什么。「那么您打算采取什么步骤？也就是说，」他接着说，「除非您乐意他接着扔。」

===Original===
“My dear fellow﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的伙计……！」

===Original===
“Some people like it,” explained Psmith. “But you do not? Quite so, quite so. I understand perfectly. We all have our likes and dislikes. Well, what would you suggest?”

===Chinese===
「有人就好这口，」皮史密斯解释道。「可您不爱？是了，是了。我完全理解。人各有所好。那么，您有何高见？」

===Original===
“I was hoping that you might consent to go down﻿—er﻿—having possibly armed yourself with a good stout stick﻿—and induce him to desist and return to bed.”

===Chinese===
「我正指望你肯下去——呃——最好先给自己操上一根结实的棍子——劝他住手，回床上去。」

===Original===
“A sound suggestion in which I can see no flaw,” said Psmith approvingly. “If you will make yourself at home in here﻿—pardon me for issuing invitations to you in your own house﻿—I will see what can be done. I have always found Comrade Baxter a reasonable man, ready to welcome suggestions from outside sources, and I have no doubt that we shall easily be able to reach some arrangement.”

===Chinese===
「一条无可挑剔的稳健建议，」皮史密斯嘉许道。「请您在这屋里随意自便——恕我在您自己家里发号施令——我这就去看看能做点什么。我一向觉得巴克斯特同志是个讲理的人，乐于听取外界建议；我毫不怀疑，咱们轻易便能达成某种谅解。」

===Original===
He got out of bed, and, having put on his slippers, and his monocle, paused before the mirror to brush his hair.

===Chinese===
他下了床，趿上拖鞋，戴上单片眼镜，在镜前驻足，理了理头发。

===Original===
“For,” he explained, “one must be natty when entering the presence of a Baxter.”

===Chinese===
「因为，」他解释道，「觐见巴克斯特，仪表不可不整。」

===Original===
He went to the closet and took from among a number of hats a neat Homburg. Then, having selected from a bowl of flowers on the mantelpiece a simple white rose, he pinned it in the coat of his pyjama-suit and announced himself ready.

===Chinese===
他走到衣柜前，从一堆帽子里拣出一顶利落的家常礼帽。又从壁炉台的花钵里选了一朵素白的玫瑰，别在睡衣襟上，宣布准备停当。

### V / V

===Original===
The sudden freshet of vicious energy which had spurred the Efficient Baxter on to his recent exhibition of marksmanship had not lasted. Lethargy was creeping back on him even as he stooped to pick up the flowerpot which had found its billet on Lord Emsworth’s spine. And, as he stood there after hurling that final missile, he had realised that that was his last shot. If that produced no results, he was finished.

===Chinese===
那股驱使能干的巴克斯特进行近日这场神射表演的恶毒能量的突发洪峰，没能持久。当他弯腰去拾那只正中埃姆斯沃思勋爵脊柱的花盆时，委顿已悄悄爬回他身上。掷出最后那枚投掷物后，他立在原地，明白了这是他最后一投。这一投若无结果，他完了。

===Original===
And, as far as he could gather, it had produced no results whatever. No head had popped inquiringly out of the window. No sound of anybody stirring had reached his ears. The place was as still as if he had been throwing marshmallows. A weary sigh escaped from Baxter’s lips. And a moment later he was reclining on the ground with his head propped against the terrace wall, a beaten man.

===Chinese===
而就他所知，它毫无结果。没有脑袋从窗口探出来查问。没有丝毫有人起床的声息传进他耳朵。这地方静得像他扔的不过是几颗棉花糖。一声疲惫的叹息从巴克斯特唇间逸出。片刻后，他已瘫坐在地上，后脑抵着露台墙，一个一败涂地的人。

===Original===
His eyes closed. Sleep, which he had been denying to himself for so long, would be denied no more. When Psmith arrived, daintily swinging the Hon. Freddie Threepwood’s niblick like a clouded cane, he had just begun to snore.

===Chinese===
他合上了眼。被他压制了这么久的睡眠，再压不住了。皮史密斯赶到时——手里优雅地摆动着弗雷迪·思里普伍德少爷的九号铁杆，像拄一根云纹手杖——他刚刚打响鼾。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Psmith was a kindly soul. He did not like Rupert Baxter, but that was no reason why he should allow him to continue lying on turf wet with the morning dew, thus courting lumbago and sciatica. He prodded Baxter in the stomach with the niblick, and the secretary sat up, blinking. And with returning consciousness came a burning sense of grievance.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯是个厚道人。他不喜欢鲁珀特·巴克斯特，但这不成其为理由，任他继续躺在露水打湿的草皮上招惹腰痛和坐骨神经痛。他用九号铁杆捅了捅巴克斯特的肚子，秘书坐了起来，直眨眼。随着意识的回归，一股灼热的冤屈感也回来了。

===Original===
“Well, you’ve been long enough,” he growled. Then, as he rubbed his red-rimmed eyes and was able to see more clearly, he perceived who it was that had come to his rescue. The spectacle of Psmith of all people beaming benignly down at him was an added offence. “Oh, it’s you?” he said morosely.

===Chinese===
「哼，你可真够慢的，」他嘟哝。等他揉完红圈环绕的眼睛、看得清楚些，才认出来救的是谁。偏偏是皮史密斯笑吟吟俯视着他，这幅景象是一重新的冒犯。「哦，是你？」他阴郁地说。

===Original===
“I in person,” said Psmith genially. “Awake, beloved! Awake, for morning in the bowl of night has flung the stone that puts the stars to flight; and lo! the hunter of the East has caught the Sultan’s turret in a noose of light. The Sultan himself,” he added, “you will find behind yonder window, speculating idly on your motives for bunging flowerpots at him. Why, if I may venture the question, *did* you?”

===Chinese===
「正是鄙人，」皮史密斯亲切地说。「醒醒吧，亲爱的！醒醒，因为清晨已向夜的碗盂投出驱散群星的石子；看哪！东方的猎手已用光的绳索套住了苏丹的塔楼。苏丹本人嘛，」他补充道，「就在那边窗户后头，正闲着眼琢磨你朝他扔花盆的动机。恕我斗胆一问：*为什么*来着？」

===Original===
Baxter was in no confiding mood. Without replying, he rose to his feet and started to trudge wearily along the terrace to the front door. Psmith fell into step beside him.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特没有倾诉的心情。他不答话，起身，拖着疲惫的步子沿露台朝正门走去。皮史密斯与之并肩而行。

===Original===
“If I were you,” said Psmith, “and I offer the suggestion in the most cordial spirit of goodwill, I would use every effort to prevent this passion for flinging flowerpots from growing upon me. I know you will say that you can take it or leave it alone; that just one more pot won’t hurt you; but can you stop at one? Isn’t it just that first insidious flowerpot that does all the mischief? Be a man, Comrade Baxter!” He laid his hand appealingly on the secretary’s shoulder. “The next time the craving comes on you, fight it. Fight it! Are you, the heir of the ages, going to become a slave to a habit? Tush! You know and I know that there is better stuff in you than that. Use your willpower, man, use your willpower.”

===Chinese===
「我要是你，」皮史密斯说，「——这建议出自最诚挚的善意——我必千方百计不让这扔花盆的瘾在我身上坐大。我知道你会说：想扔就扔，想戒就戒；再说多扔一只盆又何伤。可你能在一只前打住吗？闯下一切大祸的，不正是那第一只阴险的花盆吗？做个男子汉，巴克斯特同志！」他的手恳切地搭上秘书的肩头。「下一回瘾头上来了，跟它斗。斗！你，万世的薪传，岂能沦为一桩癖好的奴隶？呸！你我心里都有数，你里头不是这路材料。用你的意志力，伙计，用你的意志力。」

===Original===
Whatever reply Baxter might have intended to make to this powerful harangue﻿—and his attitude as he turned on his companion suggested that he had much to say﻿—was checked by a voice from above.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特对这篇雄文可能有的任何回应——他转向同伴的架势表明他有很多要说——被头顶一个声音截住了。

===Original===
“Baxter! My dear fellow!”

===Chinese===
「巴克斯特！亲爱的伙计！」

===Original===
The Earl of Emsworth, having observed the secretary’s awakening from the safe observation-post of Psmith’s bedroom, and having noted that he seemed to be exhibiting no signs of violence, had decided to make his presence known. His panic had passed, and he wanted to go into first causes.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思伯爵一直在皮史密斯卧室这个安全观察哨里注视着秘书的苏醒，见他似乎并无暴戾迹象，决定露面。他的恐慌过去了，他要追究第一因。

===Original===
Baxter gazed wanly up at the window.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特惨淡地仰望窗口。

===Original===
“I can explain everything, Lord Emsworth.”

===Chinese===
「我能解释一切，埃姆斯沃思勋爵。」

===Original===
“What?” said his lordship, leaning farther out.

===Chinese===
「什么？」勋爵大人又探出些。

===Original===
“I can explain everything,” bellowed Baxter.

===Chinese===
「我能解释一切！」巴克斯特吼道。

===Original===
“It turns out after all,” said Psmith pleasantly, “to be very simple. He was practising for the Jerking The Geranium event at the next Olympic Games.”

===Chinese===
「原来如此，到头来很简单，」皮史密斯愉快地说。「他是在为下届奥运会的『甩天竺葵』项目备赛。」

===Original===
Lord Emsworth adjusted his glasses.

===Chinese===
埃姆斯沃思勋爵正了正眼镜。

===Original===
“Your face is dirty,” he said, peering down at his dishevelled secretary. “Baxter, my dear fellow, your face is dirty.”

===Chinese===
「你脸脏了，」他俯视着狼狈的秘书说。「巴克斯特，亲爱的伙计，你的脸脏了。」

===Original===
“I was digging,” replied Baxter sullenly.

===Chinese===
「我刚才在挖土，」巴克斯特阴沉地答。

===Original===
“What?”

===Chinese===
「什么？」

===Original===
“Digging!”

===Chinese===
「挖土！」

===Original===
“The terrier complex,” explained Psmith. “What,” he asked kindly, turning to his companion, “were you digging for? Forgive me if the question seems an impertinent one, but we are naturally curious.”

===Chinese===
「梗犬情结，」皮史密斯解释道。「那么，」他转向同伴，和蔼地问，「你是挖什么来着？这问题若有冒犯还请包涵，可我们自然好奇。」

===Original===
Baxter hesitated.

===Chinese===
巴克斯特迟疑。

===Original===
“What were you digging for?” asked Lord Emsworth.

===Chinese===
「你挖什么来着？」埃姆斯沃思勋爵问。

===Original===
“You see,” said Psmith. “*He* wants to know.”

===Chinese===
「你瞧，」皮史密斯说，「*他*也想知道。」

===Original===
Not for the first time since they had become associated, a mad feeling of irritation at his employer’s woolly persistence flared up in Rupert Baxter’s bosom. The old ass was always pottering about asking questions. Fury and want of sleep combined to dull the secretary’s normal prudence. Dimly he realised that he was imparting Psmith, the scoundrel who he was convinced was the ringleader of last night’s outrage, valuable information; but anything was better than to have to stand here shouting up at Lord Emsworth. He wanted to get it over and go to bed.

===Chinese===
自两人共事以来，鲁珀特·巴克斯特胸中不是头一回蹿起对雇主稀里糊涂死缠的狂怒。这头老驴总爱晃来晃去问东问西。狂怒加上缺觉，把秘书素常的审慎磨钝了。他模模糊糊地意识到，自己这是在向皮史密斯——他认定其为昨夜暴行的元首的那个恶棍——输送宝贵情报；可怎么着也比站在楼下朝楼上的埃姆斯沃思勋爵喊话强。他想赶紧了结，上床睡觉。

===Original===
“I thought Lady Constance’s necklace was in one of the flowerpots,” he shrilled.

===Chinese===
「我原以为康斯坦丝夫人的项链在一只花盆里，」他尖声喊。

===Original===
“What?”

===Chinese===
「什么？」

===Original===
The secretary’s powers of endurance gave out. This maddening inquisition, coming on top of the restless night he had had, was too much for him. With a low moan he made one agonised leap for the front door and passed through it to where beyond these voices there was peace.

===Chinese===
秘书的耐力到头了。这场逼疯人的盘问，压在一宿折腾的顶上，把他压垮了。他低哼一声，朝着正门一个痛楚的飞跃，穿门而过，投奔那门外万籁俱寂的太平去了。

===Original===
Psmith, deprived thus abruptly of his stimulating society, remained for some moments standing near the front door, drinking in with grave approval the fresh scents of the summer morning. It was many years since he had been up and about as early as this, and he had forgotten how delightful the first beginnings of a July day can be. Unlike Baxter, on whose self-centred soul these things had been lost, he revelled in the soft breezes, the singing birds, the growing pinkness of the eastern sky. He awoke at length from his reverie to find that Lord Emsworth had toddled down and was tapping him on the arm.

===Chinese===
就这样突然失去了这位令人振奋的伙伴，皮史密斯在前门边立了片刻，带着庄重的嘉许啜饮着夏日清晨的新鲜芬芳。他已有多年不曾起这么早、活动这么早，忘了七月之晨的开端竟能这般可爱。巴克斯特那颗自我为中心的灵魂无福消受的这些，皮史密斯尽情受用：柔风，鸣禽，东天渐浓的绯色。他终于从遐想中醒来，发觉埃姆斯沃思勋爵已经踱下楼来，正拍他的胳膊。

===Original===
“*What* did he say?” inquired his lordship. He was feeling like a man who has been cut off in the midst of an absorbing telephone conversation.

===Chinese===
「他*说*什么来着？」勋爵大人打听。他的感觉，像一个人正听到入港处被掐断了电话。

===Original===
“Say?” said Psmith. “Oh, Comrade Baxter? Now, let me think. What *did* he say?”

===Chinese===
「说什么？」皮史密斯说。「哦，巴克斯特同志？让我想想。他说*什么*来着？」

===Original===
“Something about something being in a flowerpot,” prompted his lordship.

===Chinese===
「说了个什么什么东西在花盆里，」勋爵大人提示。

===Original===
“Ah, yes. He said he thought that Lady Constance’s necklace was in one of the flowerpots.”

===Chinese===
「啊，对。他说他原以为康斯坦丝夫人的项链在一只花盆里。」

===Original===
“What!”

===Chinese===
「什么！」

===Original===
Lord Emsworth, it should be mentioned, was not completely in touch with recent happenings in his home. His habit of going early to bed had caused him to miss the sensational events in the drawing-room: and, as he was a sound sleeper, the subsequent screams﻿—or, as Stokes the footman would have said, shrieks﻿—had not disturbed him. He stared at Psmith, aghast. For a while the apparent placidity of Baxter had lulled his first suspicions, but now they returned with renewed force.

===Chinese===
应当说明，埃姆斯沃思勋爵对府中近事不甚了了。他惯于早睡，错过了客厅里那场惊变；他又睡得沉，随后的尖叫——或者按男仆斯托克斯的说法，尖嚎——也不曾吵醒他。他瞠目结舌地瞪着皮史密斯。方才有一阵，巴克斯特表面的平静曾压下他最初的疑心，此刻疑心卷土重来，来势更凶。

===Original===
“Baxter thought my sister’s necklace was in a flowerpot?” he gasped.

===Chinese===
「巴克斯特以为我妹妹的项链在花盆里？」他倒抽一口气。

===Original===
“So I understood him to say.”

===Chinese===
「我听他是这么说的。」

===Original===
“But why should my sister keep her necklace in a flowerpot?”

===Chinese===
「可我妹妹为什么要把项链搁在花盆里？」

===Original===
“Ah, there you take me into deep waters.”

===Chinese===
「啊，这一问可把在下问进深水里去了。」

===Original===
“The man’s mad,” cried Lord Emsworth, his last doubts removed. “Stark, staring mad! I thought so before, and now I’m convinced of it.”

===Chinese===
「这人疯了，」埃姆斯沃思勋爵喊，最后的疑虑一扫而空。「彻头彻尾，白眼乱翻地疯了！我先前就觉得，这会儿确凿了。」

===Original===
His lordship was no novice in the symptoms of insanity. Several of his best friends were residing in those palatial establishments set in pleasant parks and surrounded by high walls with broken bottles on them, to which the wealthy and aristocratic are wont to retire when the strain of modern life becomes too great. And one of his uncles by marriage, who believed that he was a loaf of bread, had made his first public statement on the matter in the smoking-room of this very castle. What Lord Emsworth did not know about lunatics was not worth knowing.

===Chinese===
勋爵大人在疯病症状学上并非新手。他的几位至交，正下榻在那种设在悦人的园林里、围着插满碎瓶碴的高墙、供富且贵者在现代生活的重压过甚时退居的堂皇院所。他还有一位姻叔，坚信自己是一条面包，就在这座城堡的吸烟室里就此事发表了首次公开声明。关于疯子，埃姆斯沃思勋爵不知道的事，不值得知道。

===Original===
“I must get rid of him,” he said. And at the thought the fair morning seemed to Lord Emsworth to take on a sudden new beauty. Many a time had he toyed wistfully with the idea of dismissing his efficient but tyrannical secretary, but never before had that sickeningly competent young man given him any reasonable cause to act. Hitherto, moreover, he had feared his sister’s wrath should he take the plunge. But now﻿ ﻿… Surely even Connie, pigheaded as she was, could not blame him for dispensing with the services of a secretary who thought she kept her necklaces in flowerpots, and went out into the garden in the early dawn to hurl them at his bedroom window.

===Chinese===
「我得辞了他，」他说。这个念头一起，明媚的清晨在埃姆斯沃思勋爵眼里陡然添了新的美。多少回，他怅惘地拨弄过辞退这位能干却专横的秘书的念头，可这位能干得叫人发怵的青年，从前从不曾给过他任何像样的把柄。何况他还怕妹妹发作，不敢下手。可如今……任凭康妮再犟，总不见得怪他辞退一位以为她把项链藏在花盆里、还趁黎明跑到花园里朝他卧室窗户扔花盆的秘书吧。

===Original===
His demeanour took on a sudden buoyancy. He hummed a gay air.

===Chinese===
他的气色陡然轻快起来，哼起一支欢快的小调。

===Original===
“Get rid of him,” he murmured, rolling the blessed words round his tongue. He patted Psmith genially on the shoulder. “Well, my dear fellow,” he said, “I suppose we had better be getting back to bed and seeing if we can’t get a little sleep.”

===Chinese===
「辞了他，」他念叨着，把这两个福字在舌尖上滚来滚去。他亲切地拍了拍皮史密斯的肩膀。「好了，亲爱的伙计，」他说，「咱们最好还是回床上去，看看还能不能补一小觉。」

===Original===
Psmith gave a little start. He had been somewhat deeply immersed in thought.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯轻轻一惊。他方才想得相当深。

===Original===
“Do not,” he said courteously, “let me keep you from the hay if you wish to retire. To me﻿—you know what we poets are﻿—this lovely morning has brought inspiration. I think I will push off to my little nook in the woods, and write a poem about something.”

===Chinese===
「您若想歇息，」他谦和地说，「切莫因我耽搁。至于我——你知道我们诗人的脾气——这个可爱的清晨送来了灵感。我想这就去林中我的小窝，写一首关于什么的东西。」

===Original===
He accompanied his host up the silent stairs, and they parted with mutual good will at their respective doors. Psmith, having cleared his brain with a hurried cold bath, began to dress.

===Chinese===
他陪着东道走上寂静的楼梯，两人在各自的门前互道了善意的晚安。皮史密斯匆匆冲了个冷水浴清了清脑，开始穿衣。

===Original===
As a rule, the donning of his clothes was a solemn ceremony over which he dwelt lovingly; but this morning he abandoned his customary leisurely habit. He climbed into his trousers with animation, and lingered but a moment over the tying of his tie. He was convinced that there was that before him which would pay for haste.

===Chinese===
照例，着装于他是一场流连的盛典；可今晨他撇下了惯常的从容。他利落地蹬上裤子，结领结只流连了一瞬。他确信，前方有一桩值得赶路的事。

===Original===
Nothing in this world is sadder than the frequency with which we suspect our fellows without just cause. In the happenings of the night before, Psmith had seen the hand of Edward Cootes. Edward Cootes, he considered, had been indulging in what﻿—in another﻿—he would certainly have described as funny business. Like Miss Simmons, Psmith had quickly arrived at the conclusion that the necklace had been thrown out of the drawing-room window by one of those who made up the audience at his reading: and it was his firm belief that it had been picked up and hidden by Mr. Cootes. He had been trying to think ever since where that persevering man could have concealed it, and Baxter had provided the clue. But Psmith saw clearer than Baxter. The secretary, having disembowelled fifteen flowerpots and found nothing, had abandoned his theory. Psmith went further, and suspected the existence of a sixteenth. And he proposed as soon as he was dressed to sally downstairs in search of it.

===Chinese===
世间再没有比这个更令人扼腕的了：我们动辄无端疑心同胞。昨夜的种种，皮史密斯看出了爱德华·库茨的手笔。爱德华·库茨，据他判断，干的正是那种——换了别人干——他必斥为滑稽戏法的勾当。同西蒙斯小姐一样，皮史密斯很快得出结论：项链是朗诵听众之一从客厅窗口扔出去的；而拾起并藏匿它的，正是库茨先生。此后他一直在琢磨，这位锲而不舍的先生能把东西藏在哪儿，而巴克斯特给他递来了线索。但皮史密斯比巴克斯特看得更远。秘书掏空了十五只花盆一无所获，已放弃了那套理论。皮史密斯更进一步：他疑心存在第十六只。他打算一穿好衣服就下楼搜寻它。

===Original===
He put on his shoes, and left the room, buttoning his waistcoat as he went.

===Chinese===
他穿上鞋，出了房，一路扣着马甲。

### VI / VI

===Original===
The hands of the clock over the stables were pointing to half-past five when Eve Halliday, tiptoeing furtively, made another descent of the stairs. Her feelings as she went were very different from those which had caused her to jump at every sound when she had started on this same journey three hours earlier. Then, she had been a prowler in the darkness and, as such, a fitting object of suspicion: now, if she happened to run into anybody, she was merely a girl who, unable to sleep, had risen early to take a stroll in the garden. It was a distinction that made all the difference.

===Chinese===
马厩上方的钟指向五点半，伊芙·哈利迪蹑手蹑脚，再度下楼。她此刻的心情，与三个钟点前上路时草木皆兵的心境大不相同。那时，她是黑暗中的潜行者，因而天然是可疑分子；如今，万一撞见什么人，她不过是个睡不着的姑娘，早起在花园里遛个弯。一字之差，天壤之别。

===Original===
Moreover, it covered the facts. She had not been able to sleep﻿—except for an hour when she had dozed off in a chair by her window; and she certainly proposed to take a stroll in the garden. It was her intention to recover the necklace from the place where she had deposited it, and bury it somewhere where no one could possibly find it. There it could lie until she had a chance of meeting and talking to Mr. Keeble, and ascertaining what was the next step he wished taken.

===Chinese===
何况这也对得上事实。她确实睡不着——只在窗边的椅子里打了一小时的盹；她也确实打算去花园遛弯。她的计划，是把项链从寄存处取出，另埋一个绝无人能找到的地方。先安放那里，等她有机会见到基布尔先生、问明下一步意向再说。

===Original===
Two reasons had led Eve, after making her panic dash back into the house after lurking in the bushes while Baxter patrolled the terrace, to leave her precious flowerpot on the sill of the window beside the front door. She had read in stories of sensation that for purposes of concealment the most open place is the best place: and, secondly, the nearer the front door she put the flowerpot, the less distance would she have to carry it when the time came for its removal. In the present excited condition of the household, with every guest an amateur detective, the spectacle of a girl tripping downstairs with a flowerpot in her arms would excite remark.

===Chinese===
之前蹲在灌木丛里避巴克斯特巡露台、随后魂飞魄散地窜回宅子之后，伊芙之所以把宝贝花盆搁在了前门旁边那扇窗的窗台上，理由有二。她在惊险小说里读到过：论藏匿，最敞亮的地方便是最好的地方；其二，花盆搁得离前门越近，起运之日的搬运距离便越短。眼下全家风声鹤唳，人人都是业余侦探，一个姑娘抱着花盆轻快下楼的景象，是要惹人议论的。

===Original===
Eve felt exhilarated. She was not used to getting only one hour’s sleep in the course of a night, but excitement and the reflection that she had played a difficult game and won it against odds bore her up so strongly that she was not conscious of fatigue: and so uplifted did she feel that as she reached the landing above the hall she abandoned her cautious mode of progress and ran down the remaining stairs. She had the sensation of being in the last few yards of a winning race.

===Chinese===
伊芙只觉意气风发。一夜只睡一个钟头她不习惯；但兴奋，加上自知打赢了一场逆势的硬仗的念头，把她稳稳托住，竟不觉疲乏；她振奋到这般地步，走到大厅上方楼梯口时，索性撇开谨慎的碎步，一路跑下了剩下的楼梯。她有种感觉：一场必胜的赛跑，只剩最后几码了。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
The hall was quite light now. Every object in it was plainly visible. There was the huge dinner-gong: there was the long leather settee: there was the table which she had upset in the darkness. And there was the sill of the window by the front door. But the flowerpot which had been on it was gone.

===Chinese===
此刻大厅里相当亮了。每件东西都看得清清楚楚。那面大餐锣在：那张长皮榻在：那张被她在黑暗里撞翻的桌子也在。还有前门旁那扇窗的窗台。可原先搁在上面的花盆，没了。

## XII More on the Flowerpot Theme / 花盆主题再谈

===Original===
In any community in which a sensational crime has recently been committed, the feelings of the individuals who go to make up that community must of necessity vary somewhat sharply according to the degree in which the personal fortunes of each are affected by the outrage. Vivid in their own way as may be the emotions of one who sees a fellow-citizen sandbagged in a quiet street, they differ in kind from those experienced by the victim himself. And so, though the theft of Lady Constance Keeble’s diamond necklace had stirred Blandings Castle to its depths, it had not affected all those present in quite the same way. It left the house-party divided into two distinct schools of thought﻿—the one finding in the occurrence material for gloom and despondency, the other deriving from it nothing but joyful excitement.

===Chinese===
在任何一桩骇人罪案新近发生的社群里，组成这个社群的诸位的感受，势必因各人身家所受牵连的深浅而大相径庭。眼看同城公民在僻静街头吃了一记闷棍的人，情绪再怎么鲜活，也与挨棍子那位本人的滋味两样。所以，康斯坦丝·基布尔夫人钻石项链失窃案固然把布兰丁斯城堡翻了个底朝天，在场诸公所受的触动却并不一致。它把做客的众人劈成了泾渭分明的两派思想——一派从这桩事变里只看出阴郁与沮丧的材料，另一派从它身上领受了纯粹的、欢乐的兴奋。

===Original===
To this latter section belonged those free young spirits who had chafed at the prospect of being herded into the drawing-room on the eventful night to listen to Psmith’s reading of *Songs of Squalor*. It made them tremble now to think of what they would have missed, had Lady Constance’s vigilance relaxed sufficiently to enable them to execute the quiet sneak for the billiard-room of which even at the eleventh hour they had thought so wistfully. As far as the Reggies, Berties, Claudes, and Archies at that moment enjoying Lord Emsworth’s hospitality were concerned the thing was top-hole, priceless, and indisputably what the doctor ordered. They spent a great deal of their time going from one country-house to another, and as a rule found the routine a little monotonous. A happening like that of the previous night gave a splendid zip to rural life. And when they reflected that, right on top of this binge, there was coming the County Ball, it seemed to them that God was in His heaven and all right with the world. They stuck cigarettes in long holders, and collected in groups, chattering like starlings.

===Chinese===
属于后一派的，是那些自由自在的年轻灵魂——他们对那天晚上要被圈进客厅去听皮史密斯朗诵《阴郁之歌》的前景，本来满腹牢骚。此刻他们想起来就后怕：倘若康斯坦丝夫人的警戒稍一松动，容他们实施了那场悄悄溜进台球室的逃亡——哪怕到了最后关头他们还在眼巴巴地盘算——他们将错过多少好戏。就当时叨光埃姆斯沃思勋爵的诸位雷吉们、伯蒂们、克劳德们、阿奇们而言，这事实在是顶呱呱、千金难买、不容置疑地正中下怀。他们平生大半光阴用来从一座乡间宅邸串到另一座，照例觉得这套日程有点单调。前夜这样的事变，给乡村生活添了绝妙的辣味。再一想到这场狂欢头顶上还压着一场郡舞会，他们只觉得上帝在天上安居，尘世一切安好。他们把香烟插进长烟嘴，成群结伙，叽叽喳喳赛过椋鸟。

===Original===
The gloomy brigade, those with hearts bowed down, listened to their effervescent babbling with wan distaste. These last were a small body numerically, but very select. Lady Constance might have been described as their head and patroness. Morning found her still in a state bordering on collapse. After breakfast, however, which she took in her room, and which was sweetened by an interview with Mr. Joseph Keeble, her husband, she brightened considerably. Mr. Keeble, thought Lady Constance, behaved magnificently. She had always loved him dearly, but never so much as when, abstaining from the slightest reproach of her obstinacy in refusing to allow the jewels to be placed in the bank, he spaciously informed her that he would buy her another necklace, just as good and costing every penny as much as the old one. It was at this point that Lady Constance almost seceded from the ranks of gloom. She kissed Mr. Keeble gratefully, and attacked with something approaching animation the boiled egg at which she had been pecking when he came in.

===Chinese===
阴郁的那一旅——心口压着石头的人们——听着他们冒泡的絮叨，惨然倒胃。这批人数量不多，成分极精。康斯坦丝夫人堪称他们的首领与主保。到了早晨，她还处在近乎崩溃的状态。不过，在房里用过早餐、又同丈夫约瑟夫·基布尔先生会谈过一场之后，她开朗了不少。基布尔先生，康斯坦丝夫人觉得，表现得极有气度。她素来深爱着他，却从没像此刻这样爱他——他非但没有半个字怪她执意不肯把珠宝存进银行，反而豪迈地宣布：要给她另买一条项链，一样的好，一个便士不省，跟旧的那条等价。正是在这一刻，康斯坦丝夫人几乎脱离了阴郁的队伍。她感激地亲吻了基布尔先生，以近乎活跃的劲头，向那只他进来时她还在啄食的水煮蛋发起了进攻。

===Original===
But a few minutes later the average of despair was restored by the enrolment of Mr. Keeble in the ranks of the despondent. He had gladsomely assumed overnight that one of his agents, either Eve or Freddie, had been responsible for the disappearance of the necklace. The fact that Freddie, interviewed by stealth in his room, gapingly disclaimed any share in the matter had not damped him. He had never expected results from Freddie. But when, after leaving Lady Constance, he encountered Eve and was given a short outline of history, beginning with her acquisition of the necklace, and ending﻿—like a modern novel﻿—on the sombre note of her finding the flowerpot gone, he too sat him down and mourned as deeply as anyone.

===Chinese===
可几分钟之后，绝望的平均值又回来了——基布尔先生本人入了沮丧者的伍。头天夜里他还乐滋滋地认定，项链失踪是他手下哪位执行人干的——不是伊芙就是弗雷迪。弗雷迪被人偷偷摸到他房里问话、张着嘴矢口否认与此事有份，也没扫他的兴。他本来就没指望弗雷迪出成果。可是，离开康斯坦丝夫人后撞见伊芙，听了一段简短的史事梗概——从她如何入手项链讲起，到发现花盆不翼而飞为止，像一部现代小说似的收在阴郁的调子上——他也坐下来，同任何人一样深深地哀恸起来。

===Original===
Passing with a brief mention over Freddie, whose morose bearing was the subject of considerable comment among the younger set; over Lord Emsworth, who woke at twelve o’clock disgusted to find that he had missed several hours among his beloved flowerbeds; and over the Efficient Baxter, who was roused from sleep at twelve-fifteen by Thomas the footman knocking on his door in order to hand him a note from his employer enclosing a cheque, and dispensing with his services; we come to Miss Peavey.

===Chinese===
弗雷迪那份阴沉做派正在年轻一辈中招致纷纷议论，埃姆斯沃思勋爵十二点醒来、痛心错失了在心爱花圃间劳作的几个钟头，能干的巴克斯特十二点一刻被男仆托马斯的敲门声唤起、接获雇主附支票一纸、辞谢其服务的字条——这几位按下不表；我们来说皮维小姐。

===Original===
At twenty minutes past eleven on this morning when so much was happening to so many people, Miss Peavey stood in the Yew Alley gazing belligerently at the stemless mushroom finial of a tree about halfway between the entrance and the point where the alley merged into the west wood. She appeared to be soliloquising. For, though words were proceeding from her with considerable rapidity, there seemed to be no one in sight to whom they were being addressed. Only an exceptionally keen observer would have noted a slight significant quivering among the tree’s tightly-woven branches.

===Chinese===
在这个人人都有大事发生的上午，十一点二十分，皮维小姐立在紫杉巷里，好斗地瞪着巷子中段一株树的蘑菇状圆顶——那树正在巷口与巷子没入西林子的交点之间。她像在自言自语。话从她嘴里出得相当急，视线里却没有可以对之说的对象。只有眼力格外尖的人，才会注意到那株树密织的枝叶间一阵轻微而意味深长的颤抖。

===Original===
“You poor boneheaded fish,” the poetess was saying with that strained tenseness which results from the churning up of a generous and emotional nature, “isn’t there anything in this world you can do without tumbling over your feet and making a mess of it? All I ask of you is to stroll under a window and pick up a few jewels, and now you come and tell me﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「你这个没长脑壳的鱼，」女诗人正在说，带着一副慷慨易感的天性被搅翻后特有的紧绷，「这世上就没有一件事你能不绊自己的脚、不办砸的？我对你的全部要求，不过是在一扇窗下遛个弯、捡几件珠宝，如今你倒跑来告诉我……」

===Original===
“But, Liz!” said the tree plaintively.

===Chinese===
「可是，莉兹！」那树委屈地说。

===Original===
“I do all the difficult part of the job. All that there was left for you to handle was something a child of three could have done on its ear. And now﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「难的活儿全是我干的。留给你的那点，三岁小孩闭着眼都办得了。结果呢……」

===Original===
“But, Liz! I’m telling you I couldn’t find the stuff. I was down there all right, but I couldn’t find it.”

===Chinese===
「可是，莉兹！我跟你说我找不着那玩意儿。我是到楼下了，没错，可我找不着。」

===Original===
“You couldn’t find it!” Miss Peavey pawed restlessly at the soft turf with a shapely shoe. “You’re the sort of dumb Isaac that couldn’t find a bass-drum in a telephone-booth. You didn’t *look*.”

===Chinese===
「你找不着！」皮维小姐一只好看的鞋在软草皮上烦躁地刨着。「你这种木头以撒，进了电话亭也找不着一只低音鼓。你压根没*找*。」

===Original===
“I did look. Honest, I did.”

===Chinese===
「我找了。真的，我找了。」

===Original===
“Well, the stuff was there. I threw it down the moment the lights went out.”

===Chinese===
「东西就在那儿。灯一灭我立刻扔下去的。」

===Original===
“Somebody must have got there first, and swiped it.”

===Chinese===
「准是有人抢了先，给顺走了。」

===Original===
“Who could have got there first? Everybody was up in the room where I was.”

===Chinese===
「谁能抢了先？那会儿人全在楼上我那间屋里。」

===Original===
“Are you sure?”

===Chinese===
「你敢肯定？」

===Original===
“Am I sure? Am I﻿ ﻿…” The poetess’s voice trailed off. She was staring down the Yew Alley at a couple who had just entered. She hissed a warning in a sharp undertone. “Hsst! Cheese it, Ed. There’s someone coming.”

===Chinese===
「我敢不敢肯定？我敢不——」女诗人的嗓音渐渐没了。她正沿紫杉巷望着刚进来的两个人。她压着嗓子嘶声警告。「嘘！住嘴，埃德。来人了。」

===Original===
The two intruders who had caused Miss Peavey to suspend her remarks to her erring lieutenant were of opposite sexes﻿—a tall girl with fair hair, and a taller young man irreproachably clad in white flannels who beamed down at his companion through a single eyeglass. Miss Peavey gazed at them searchingly as they approached. A sudden thought had come to her at the sight of them. Mistrusting Psmith as she had done ever since Mr. Cootes had unmasked him for the impostor that he was, the fact that they were so often together had led her to extend her suspicion to Eve. It might, of course, be nothing but a casual friendship, begun here at the castle; but Miss Peavey had always felt that Eve would bear watching. And now, seeing them together again this morning, it had suddenly come to her that she did not recall having observed Eve among the gathering in the drawing-room last night. True, there had been many people present, but Eve’s appearance was striking, and she was sure that she would have noticed her, if she had been there. And, if she had not been there, why should she not have been on the terrace? Somebody had been on the terrace last night, that was certain. For all her censorious attitude in their recent conversation, Miss Peavey had not really in her heart believed that even a dumbbell like Eddie Cootes would not have found the necklace if it had been lying under the window on his arrival.

===Chinese===
害得皮维小姐暂停训话的这两位不速之客，性别相异——一个金发的高挑姑娘，一个更高的青年，一身白法兰绒无懈可击，正隔着单片眼镜朝同伴眉开眼笑。两人走近时，皮维小姐细细打量。一见他们，一个念头蓦地来了。自打库茨先生揭穿皮史密斯的冒牌真身以来，她对他疑云重重；两人形影不离这一事实，又使她的疑心蔓延到了伊芙身上。这当然可能不过是在城堡里新起的泛泛之交；可皮维小姐一贯觉得，伊芙这丫头值得盯防。此刻又见他们清晨同出，她猛然想起：昨晚客厅济济一堂时，她不记得看见了伊芙。诚然在场的人很多，但伊芙的容貌惹眼，若她在场，自己必会留意到。而她若不在场——为什么不可以在露台上呢？昨晚露台上必定有人，这一点是笃定的。方才训话虽说得好凶，皮维小姐心里其实并不真信：连埃迪·库茨这号笨蛋，到了窗下而项链又确实在窗下的话，会找不着它。

===Original===
“Oh, good morning, Mr. McTodd,” she cooed. “I’m feeling *so* upset about this terrible affair. Aren’t *you*, Miss Halliday?”

===Chinese===
「哦，早上好，麦克托德先生，」她咕咕地说。「这场可怕的祸事，我心里*乱*极了。*你*呢，哈利迪小姐？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Eve, and she had never said a more truthful word.

===Chinese===
「是乱，」伊芙说，她说过的实话再没有比这句更实的了。

===Original===
Psmith, for his part, was in more debonair and cheerful mood even than was his wont. He had examined the position of affairs and found life good. He was particularly pleased with the fact that he had persuaded Eve to stroll with him this morning and inspect his cottage in the woods. Buoyant as was his temperament, he had been half afraid that last night’s interview on the terrace might have had disastrous effects on their intimacy. He was now feeling full of kindliness and goodwill towards all mankind﻿—even Miss Peavey; and he bestowed on the poetess a dazzling smile.

===Chinese===
皮史密斯这厢，兴致比素常还要轻快三分。他检视过局势，觉得人生美好。尤其让他得意的是：今早他说动了伊芙同来散步，参观他林中的小屋。他性情纵然豁达，昨夜露台那场会晤会否殃及两人的亲密，他原本还捏着半把汗。此刻他对全人类——连皮维小姐也在内——满怀慈爱与善意，朝女诗人绽放了一个晃眼的微笑。

===Original===
“We must always,” he said, “endeavour to look on the bright side. It was a pity, no doubt, that my reading last night had to be stopped at a cost of about twenty thousand pounds to the Keeble coffers, but let us not forget that but for that timely interruption I should have gone on for about another hour. I am like that. My friends have frequently told me that when once I start talking it requires something in the nature of a cataclysm to stop me. But, of course, there are drawbacks to everything, and last night’s rannygazoo perhaps shook your nervous system to some extent?”

===Chinese===
「我们必须永远，」他说，「力求看到光明的一面。我昨晚的朗诵落得个中途夭折，基布尔家的库房破费约两万镑，这固然可惜；但也别忘记：若无那场及时的打断，我还得再念上一个钟头上下。我这人就是这样。朋友们屡屡告诫我：我一开了讲，非得有点天崩地裂级别的事变，才停得下来。当然啦，凡事皆有代价；昨晚那场乱子，想必多少震了震你的神经系统？」

===Original===
“I was dreadfully frightened,” said Miss Peavey. She turned to Eve with a delicate shiver. “Weren’t *you*, Miss Halliday?”

===Chinese===
「我吓坏了，」皮维小姐说。她带着精致的寒噤转向伊芙。「*你*呢，哈利迪小姐？」

===Original===
“I wasn’t there,” said Eve absently.

===Chinese===
「我当时不在，」伊芙心不在焉地说。

===Original===
“Miss Halliday,” explained Psmith, “has had in the last few days some little experience of myself as orator, and with her usual good sense decided not to go out of her way to get more of me than was absolutely necessary. I was perhaps a trifle wounded at the moment, but on thinking it over came to the conclusion that she was perfectly justified in her attitude. I endeavour always in my conversation to instruct, elevate, and entertain, but there is no gainsaying the fact that a purist might consider enough of my chitchat to be sufficient. Such, at any rate, was Miss Halliday’s view, and I honour her for it. But here I am, rambling on again just when I can see that you wish to be alone. We will leave you, therefore, to muse. No doubt we have been interrupting a train of thought which would have resulted but for my arrival in a rondel or a ballade or some other poetic morceau. Come, Miss Halliday. A weird and repellent female,” he said to Eve as they drew out of hearing, “created for some purpose which I cannot fathom. Everything in this world, I like to think, is placed there for some useful end: but why the authorities unleashed Miss Peavey on us is beyond me. It is not too much to say that she gives me a pain in the gizzard.”

===Chinese===
「哈利迪小姐，」皮史密斯解释道，「近几日对我这演说家已有几分阅历，以她素来的明智，决定不专程去领受超出绝对必需量的我。我当时或许略感创伤，但细想之后，认定她的立场完全站得住。我在谈吐中一向力求寓教、升华、娱人于一体，但无可讳言，一个挑剔的人或许认为，我这份闲聊，聊一回也就够了。至少哈利迪小姐持此观点，我对此深表敬意。可是你看，我又絮叨起来了，明明看得出你想独处。那我们就此别过，留你沉思。不用问，我们方才打断的，是一列思想的列车，若非我至，本可结出一首回旋诗或叙事谣曲之类的诗中珍味。走吧，哈利迪小姐。一个古怪而倒胃口的女人，」走出听力所及后他对伊芙说，「造出来必有个用途，只是我参不透。我总乐于相信，世间万物的安放皆有其有用的归宿；可当局为何把皮维小姐放出来祸害我们，实非我所能解。可以毫不夸张地说：她硌得我砂囊疼。」

===Original===
Miss Peavey, unaware of these harsh views, had watched them out of sight, and now she turned excitedly to the tree which sheltered her ally.

===Chinese===
皮维小姐不知有这些刻薄话，目送两人出了视野，随即兴奋地转向庇护她盟友的那株树。

===Original===
“Ed!”

===Chinese===
「埃德！」

===Original===
“Hello?” replied the muffled voice of Mr. Cootes.

===Chinese===
「怎么？」库茨先生瓮声瓮气地应。

===Original===
“Did you hear?”

===Chinese===
「听见没有？」

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
「没有。」

===Original===
“Oh, my heavens!” cried his overwrought partner. “He’s gone deaf now! That girl﻿—you didn’t hear what she was saying? She said that she wasn’t in the drawing-room when those lights went out. Ed, she was down below on the terrace, that’s where she was, picking up the stuff. And if it isn’t hidden somewheres in that McTodd’s shack down there in the woods I’ll eat my Sunday rubbers.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我的老天！」他这位心力交瘁的搭档喊道。「他又聋了！那丫头——你没听见她说什么？她说灯灭的时候她不在客厅。埃德，她当时就在下面露台上，就在那儿，捡了货。这会儿东西要不是藏在林子里那麦克托德的棚子里，我把礼拜天套鞋吃了。」

===Original===
Eve, with Psmith prattling amiably at her side, pursued her way through the wood. She was wondering why she had come. She ought, she felt, to have been very cold and distant to this young man after what had occurred between them last night. But somehow it was difficult to be cold and distant with Psmith. He cheered her stricken soul. By the time they reached the little clearing and came in sight of the squat, shed-like building with its funny windows and stained door, her spirits, always mercurial, had risen to a point where she found herself almost able to forget her troubles.

===Chinese===
伊芙在皮史密斯和蔼的絮语陪伴下，穿行林中。她纳闷自己为什么要来。按理，经过昨夜那一幕，她该对这个青年冷若冰霜、敬而远之。可同皮史密斯冷得起来、远得起来的，天下少有。他给她受伤的灵魂提气。等走到那片小小的林间空地、望见那座蹲式棚屋——滑稽的窗，斑驳的门——她那素来善变的情绪已然回升，几乎能把烦恼撂在脑后了。

===Original===
“What a horrible-looking place!” she exclaimed. “Whatever did you want it for?”

===Chinese===
「这地方看着真寒碜！」她喊。「你要它干什么呀？」

===Original===
“Purely as a nook,” said Psmith, taking out his key. “You know how the man of sensibility and refinement needs a nook. In this rushing age it is imperative that the thinker shall have a place, however humble, where he can be alone.”

===Chinese===
「纯作一个窝巢，」皮史密斯说着取出钥匙。「你知道，感性与教养兼备之士少不了个窝巢。在这个忙乱的世代，思想者务必有一处——再简陋也罢——可以独处的地方。」

===Original===
“But you aren’t a thinker.”

===Chinese===
「可你不是思想者。」

===Original===
“You wrong me. For the last few days I have been doing some extremely brisk thinking. And the strain has taken its toll. The fierce whirl of life at Blandings is wearing me away. There are dark circles under my eyes and I see floating spots.” He opened the door. “Well, here we are. Will you pop in for a moment?”

===Chinese===
「你冤枉我了。近几日我一直在进行极为活跃的思想活动。劳损已经显出来了。布兰丁斯生活的猛烈旋涡正在把我磨薄。我眼下发暗，眼前飞黑点。」他开了门。「好了，到了。要不要进来坐一分钟？」

===Original===
Eve went in. The single sitting-room of the cottage certainly bore out the promise of the exterior. It contained a table with a red cloth, a chair, three stuffed birds in a glass case on the wall, and a small horsehair sofa. A depressing musty scent pervaded the place, as if a cheese had recently died there in painful circumstances. Eve gave a little shiver of distaste.

===Chinese===
伊芙走了进去。小屋唯一的起居间，果然印证了外观的许诺。屋里有一张红布桌、一把椅子、墙上玻璃罩里的三只标本鸟、一张马鬃小沙发。一股霉味弥漫全屋，仿佛最近有块奶酪在此惨死。伊芙嫌恶地轻轻一颤。

===Original===
“I understand your silent criticism,” said Psmith. “You are saying to yourself that plain living and high thinking is evidently the ideal of the gamekeepers on the Blandings estate. They are strong, rugged men who care little for the refinements of interior decoration. But shall we blame them? If I had to spend most of the day and night chivvying poachers and keeping an eye on the local rabbits, I imagine that in my off-hours practically anything with a roof would satisfy me. It was in the hope that you might be able to offer some hints and suggestions for small improvements here and there that I invited you to inspect my little place. There is no doubt that it wants doing up a bit, by a woman’s gentle hand. Will you take a look round and give out a few ideas? The wallpaper is, I fear, a fixture, but in every other direction consider yourself untrammelled.”

===Chinese===
「我明白你无言的批评，」皮史密斯说。「你在心里说：清苦的生活、高远的思想，显然是布兰丁斯庄园猎场看守们的理想。他们是些粗犷的汉子，不大讲究室内装修的雅致。可咱们能怪他们吗？倘若我必须日夜追偷猎者、看管本地兔子，我想歇工的钟点里，凡有个顶的都能将就我。我邀你来视察我的小地方，正是指望你能提点修葺方面的零星高见。毋庸置疑，此地需要一只女性温柔的手来拾掇拾掇。请你四处瞧瞧，发表几条意见如何？墙纸恐怕是钉死的，但其他一切方面，请视自己为不受羁勒。」

===Original===
Eve looked about her.

===Chinese===
伊芙环顾四周。

===Original===
“Well,” she began dubiously, “I don’t think﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「这个，」她迟疑着开口，「我看……」

===Original===
She stopped abruptly, tingling all over. A second glance had shown her something which her first careless inspection had overlooked. Half hidden by a ragged curtain, there stood on the windowsill a large flowerpot containing a geranium. And across the surface of the flowerpot was a broad splash of white paint.

===Chinese===
她猛地收住，浑身发麻。再看的一眼，让她看见了初看时漫不经心错过的东西。破窗帘半掩着的窗台上，立着一只大花盆，盆里一株天竺葵。而花盆腰身上，横着一道宽宽的白漆。

===Original===
“You were saying﻿ ﻿… ?” said Psmith courteously.

===Chinese===
「你方才说……？」皮史密斯彬彬有礼地说。

===Original===
Eve did not reply. She hardly heard him. Her mind was in a confused whirl. A monstrous suspicion was forming itself in her brain.

===Chinese===
伊芙没有作答。她几乎没有听见。她脑子里一片天旋地转。一个骇人的疑团正在脑中成形。

===Original===
“You are admiring the shrub?” said Psmith. “I found it lying about up at the castle this morning and pinched it. I thought it would add a touch of colour to the place.”

===Chinese===
「你在欣赏这株灌木？」皮史密斯说。「今早我在城堡里见它闲搁着，就顺了来。我想它能给这地方添一点颜色。」

===Original===
Eve, looking at him keenly as his gaze shifted to the flowerpot, told herself that her suspicion had been absurd. Surely this blandness could not be a cloak for guilt.

===Chinese===
伊芙敏锐地望着他——他的目光正移向花盆——她对自己说，那个疑团荒唐透了。这般的一团和气，总不会是负罪的伪装吧。

===Original===
“Where did you find it?”

===Chinese===
「你在哪儿找到它的？」

===Original===
“By one of the windows in the hall, more or less wasting its sweetness. I am bound to say I am a little disappointed in the thing. I had a sort of idea it would turn the old homestead into a floral bower, but it doesn’t seem to.”

===Chinese===
「大厅哪扇窗边，多多少少在空耗芳华。我必须承认，我对这东西有点失望。我原有点以为，它能把这所老宅变成花团锦簇的凉亭，可它似乎不打算。」

===Original===
“It’s a beautiful geranium.”

===Chinese===
「多好看的天竺葵。」

===Original===
“There,” said Psmith, “I cannot agree with you. It seems to me to have the glanders or something.”

===Chinese===
「这一点，」皮史密斯说，「恕我不敢苟同。依我看它染了鼻疽还是什么的。」

===Original===
“It only wants watering.”

===Chinese===
「它只是缺水。」

===Original===
“And unfortunately this cosy little place appears to possess no water supply. I take it that the late proprietor when in residence used to trudge to the back door of the castle and fetch what he needed in a bucket. If this moribund plant fancies that I am going to spend my time racing to and fro with refreshments, it is vastly mistaken. Tomorrow it goes into the dustbin.”

===Chinese===
「而不幸，这个温馨的小地方似乎没有水源。我推想，故去的原主人在住时，惯于跋涉到城堡后门，用桶把所需拎回来。倘若这株半死的植物以为，我会把光阴花在来回奔波送茶水上，它大大地想错了。明天它进垃圾桶。」

===Original===
Eve shut her eyes. She was awed by a sense of having arrived at a supreme moment. She had the sensations of a gambler who risks all on a single throw.

===Chinese===
伊芙闭上了眼。她被一种临到至高时刻的敬畏攫住。她有的是赌徒孤注一掷时的感觉。

===Original===
“What a shame!” she said, and her voice, though she tried to control it, shook. “You had better give it to me. I’ll take care of it. It’s just what I want for my room.”

===Chinese===
「多可惜呀！」她说，嗓音尽管竭力控制，仍在发颤。「你不如给我吧。我会照顾它的。我屋里正缺这么一盆。」

===Original===
“Pray take it,” said Psmith. “It isn’t mine, but pray take it. And very encouraging it is, let me add, that you should be accepting gifts from me in this hearty fashion; for it is well known that there is no surer sign of the dawning of the divine emotion﻿—love,” he explained, “than this willingness to receive presents from the hands of the adorer. I make progress, I make progress.”

===Chinese===
「请拿去，」皮史密斯说。「它本非我物，但请拿去。而且容我附言：你这样爽快地收下我的礼物，实在令人鼓舞；因为众所周知，神圣情感——爱，」他解释道，「之破晓，再没有比乐于从爱慕者手中收礼更可靠的征兆了。我在进步，我在进步。」

===Original===
“You don’t do anything of the kind,” said Eve. Her eyes were sparkling and her heart sang within her. In the revulsion of feeling which had come to her on finding her suspicions unfounded she was aware of a warm friendliness towards this absurd young man.

===Chinese===
「你才没有呢，」伊芙说。她双眼发亮，心在胸腔里唱歌。疑心落空带来的情感逆转中，她对这个荒唐青年涌起一股暖融融的好感。

===Original===
“Pardon me,” said Psmith firmly. “I am quoting an established authority﻿—Auntie Belle of *Home Gossip*.”

===Chinese===
「恕我直言，」皮史密斯坚定地说。「我引的是权威——《家常闲话》的贝儿姑姑。」

===Original===
“I must be going,” said Eve. She took the flowerpot and hugged it to her. “I’ve got work to do.”

===Chinese===
「我该走了，」伊芙说。她抱起花盆，搂在怀里。「我还有活儿呢。」

===Original===
“Work, work, always work!” sighed Psmith. “The curse of the age. Well, I will escort you back to your cell.”

===Chinese===
「工作，工作，永远是工作！」皮史密斯叹道。「时代的诅咒。好吧，我送你回你的小间。」

===Original===
“No, you won’t,” said Eve. “I mean, thank you for your polite offer, but I want to be alone.”

===Chinese===
「不，不用，」伊芙说。「我是说，谢谢你的美意，可我想一个人待着。」

===Original===
“Alone?” Psmith looked at her, astonished. “When you have the chance of being with *me*? This is a strange attitude.”

===Chinese===
「一个人？」皮史密斯讶异地看着她。「放着与我做伴的机会不要？这态度很奇怪。」

===Original===
“Goodbye,” said Eve. “Thank you for being so hospitable and lavish. I’ll try to find some cushions and muslin and stuff to brighten up this place.”

===Chinese===
「回见，」伊芙说。「多谢你的盛情与慷慨。我会找些坐垫、薄纱什么的，把这地方装点亮堂。」

===Original===
“Your presence does that adequately,” said Psmith, accompanying her to the door. “By the way, returning to the subject we were discussing last night, I forgot to mention, when asking you to marry me, that I can do card-tricks.”

===Chinese===
「你的莅临已够亮堂的了，」皮史密斯说着送她到门口。「对了，回到昨夜讨论的题目——我求婚时忘了提，我还会纸牌戏法。」

===Original===
“Really?”

===Chinese===
「真的？」

===Original===
“And also a passable imitation of a cat calling to her young. Has this no weight with you? Think! These things come in very handy in the long winter evenings.”

===Chinese===
「还会一段过得去的母猫唤崽的口技。这在天平上没有分量吗？想想！漫长的冬夜里，这些东西可大有用场。」

===Original===
“But I shan’t be there when you are imitating cats in the long winter evenings.”

===Chinese===
「可你学猫叫的那些漫长冬夜，我不会在场呀。」

===Original===
“I think you are wrong. As I visualise my little home, I can see you there very clearly, sitting before the fire. Your maid has put you into something loose. The light of the flickering flames reflects itself in your lovely eyes. You are pleasantly tired after an afternoon’s shopping, but not so tired as to be unable to select a card﻿—*any* card﻿—from the pack which I offer﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「我看你错了。就我对我小家的想象而言，你在场的画面清晰得很：坐在炉火前。你的女仆给你换上了宽松的便装。跳动的火光映在你可爱的眼睛里。一下午的采购使你愉快地疲乏，但还没乏到不能从我递上的那副牌里抽一张——*随便*哪一张……」

===Original===
“Goodbye,” said Eve.

===Chinese===
「回见，」伊芙说。

===Original===
“If it must be so﻿—goodbye. For the present. I shall see you anon?”

===Chinese===
「若必须如此——回见。暂时的。回头见？」

===Original===
“I expect so.”

===Chinese===
「多半吧。」

===Original===
“Good! I will count the minutes.”

===Chinese===
「好极了！我会一分钟一分钟地数。」

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Eve walked rapidly away. As she snuggled the flowerpot under her arm she was feeling like a child about to open its Christmas stocking. Before she had gone far, a shout stopped her and she perceived Psmith galloping gracefully in her wake.

===Chinese===
伊芙快步走开。花盆夹在臂弯里，她的心情像个即将拆圣诞袜的孩子。没走多远，一声呼喊止住了她：皮史密斯正姿势优雅地欢蹄追来。

===Original===
“Can you spare me a moment?” said Psmith.

===Chinese===
「能再占你一分钟吗？」皮史密斯说。

===Original===
“Certainly.”

===Chinese===
「当然。」

===Original===
“I should have added that I can also recite ‘Gunga-Din.’ Will you think that over?”

===Chinese===
「我本该补充：我还会朗诵《贡格丁》。可否请你把这条也考虑进去？」

===Original===
“I will.”

===Chinese===
「可以。」

===Original===
“Thank you,” said Psmith. “Thank you. I have a feeling that it may just turn the scale.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢，」皮史密斯说。「谢谢。我有种预感，压秤的兴许正这一条。」

===Original===
He raised his hat ambassadorially and galloped away again.

===Chinese===
他以大使的风度举了举帽，重新欢蹄而去。

===Original===
---

===Chinese===
---

===Original===
Eve found herself unable to wait any longer. Psmith was out of sight now, and the wood was very still and empty. Birds twittered in the branches, and the sun made little pools of gold upon the ground. She cast a swift glance about her and crouched down in the shelter of a tree.

===Chinese===
伊芙发觉自己再也等不得了。皮史密斯已出了视野，林中静而空。鸟在枝头啁啾，太阳在地上淌出一小汪一小汪的金。她飞快四下一扫，在一株树的掩蔽下蹲了下去。

===Original===
The birds stopped singing. The sun no longer shone. The wood had become cold and sinister. For Eve, with a heart of lead, was staring blankly at a little pile of mould at her feet; mould which she had sifted again and again in a frenzied, fruitless effort to find a necklace which was not there.

===Chinese===
鸟声停了。太阳不再照耀。林子变得又冷又阴森。伊芙端详着脚边那一小堆土，心像灌了铅；这堆土，她在疯狂而徒劳的努力里筛了又筛，要找一条项链——它不在。

===Original===
The empty flowerpot seemed to leer up at her in mockery.

===Chinese===
空花盆仿佛在朝她讪笑。

## XIII Psmith Receives Guests / 皮史密斯接待来客

### I / I

===Original===
Blandings Castle was astir from roof to hall. Lights blazed, voices shouted, bells rang. All over the huge building there prevailed a vast activity like that of a barracks on the eve of the regiment’s departure for abroad. Dinner was over, and the Expeditionary Force was making its final preparations before starting off in many motorcars for the County Ball at Shifley. In the bedrooms on every floor, Reggies, doubtful at the last moment about their white ties, were feverishly arranging new ones; Berties brushed their already glistening hair; and Claudes shouted to Archies along the passages insulting inquiries as to whether they had been sneaking their handkerchiefs. Valets skimmed like swallows up and down corridors, maids fluttered in and out of rooms in aid of Beauty in distress. The noise penetrated into every nook and corner of the house. It vexed the Efficient Baxter, going through his papers in the library preparatory to leaving Blandings on the morrow for ever. It disturbed Lord Emsworth, who stoutly declining to go within ten miles of the County Ball, had retired to his room with a book on Herbaceous Borders. It troubled the peace of Beach the butler, refreshing himself after his activities around the dinner table with a glass of sound port in the housekeeper’s room. The only person in the place who paid no attention to it was Eve Halliday.

===Chinese===
布兰丁斯城堡从屋顶闹腾到大厅。灯火通明，人声鼎沸，铃响不断。整座巨宅里一片繁忙，恰似兵营在开拔出国前夜。晚餐已毕，远征军正在做最后的整备，即将分乘多辆汽车开赴希夫利的郡舞会。各层卧室里，雷吉们临到末刻又对白领结起了疑，正狂乱地换系新的；伯蒂们梳理着本已锃亮的头发；克劳德们隔着走廊朝阿奇们高声骂阵，质问他们是不是偷了自己的手帕。贴身男仆们如燕掠过廊道，女仆们为落难的美人穿梭于各房之间。喧声钻进宅子的每一个犄角旮旯。它搅扰了能干的巴克斯特——他正在藏书室清点文件，预备明日永远辞别布兰丁斯。它惊动了埃姆斯沃思勋爵——他坚拒踏进郡舞会十英里之内，已携一部《花境专著》退回自己房中。它打搅了管家比奇的清静——他伺候完晚餐，正在管家妇房里啜一杯醇厚的波尔图提神。全宅唯一充耳不闻的，是伊芙·哈利迪。

===Original===
Eve was too furious to pay attention to anything but her deleterious thoughts. As she walked on the terrace, to which she had fled in quest of solitude, her teeth were set and her blue eyes glowed belligerently. As Miss Peavey would have put it in one of her colloquial moods, she was mad clear through. For Eve was a girl of spirit, and there is nothing your girl of spirit so keenly resents as being made a fool of, whether it be by Fate or by a fellow human creature. Eve was in the uncomfortable position of having had this indignity put upon her by both. But, while as far as Fate was concerned she merely smouldered rebelliously, her animosity towards Psmith was vivid in the extreme.

===Chinese===
伊芙火大到了极点，除了那些要命的念头什么都顾不上。她逃到露台上觅取清静，走在草坪上，牙关紧咬，蓝眼睛里燃着好斗的光。用皮维小姐口语体的话来说：她从里到外气了个透。伊芙是个有血性的姑娘，而有血性的姑娘最恨的，莫过于被人当猴耍——不论耍她的是命运还是同类。伊芙倒霉在于，这番羞辱两样都齐了。不过对命运，她不过是阴燃着一股叛火；对皮史密斯的敌忾，却是烧得亮堂堂。

===Original===
A hot wave of humiliation made her writhe as she remembered the infantile guilelessness with which she had accepted the preposterous story he had told her in explanation of his presence at Blandings in another man’s name. He had been playing with her all the time﻿—fooling her﻿—and, most unforgivable crime of all, he had dared to pretend that he was fond of her and﻿—Eve’s face burned again﻿—to make her﻿—almost﻿—fond of him. How he must have laughed﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
一想起自己当时那副婴儿般的轻信——听他讲那套荒唐透顶的说辞、解释他何以顶着别人的名字来到布兰丁斯、居然照单全收——一股滚烫的羞惭便烧得她浑身扭动。他从头到尾在耍她——哄她——而最不可饶恕的一宗罪是：他竟敢装作喜欢她，而且——伊芙的脸又烧起来了——害她——差一点——喜欢上了他。他准是笑坏了……

===Original===
Well, she was not beaten yet. Her chin went up and she began to walk quicker. He was clever, but she would be cleverer. The game was not over﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
好啊，她还没输。她扬起下巴，脚下加快了。他聪明，可她要比他更聪明。这局棋还没下完……

===Original===
“Hallo!”

===Chinese===
「你好！」

===Original===
A white waistcoat was gleaming at her side. Polished shoes shuffled on the turf. Light hair, brushed and brilliantined to the last possible pitch of perfection, shone in the light of the stars. The Hon. Freddie Threepwood was in her midst.

===Chinese===
一件白背心在她身侧发亮。锃亮的皮鞋在草皮上蹭蹭。浅色的头发梳理加发蜡，上到了可能的上限，在星光里熠熠生辉。弗雷迪·思里普伍德少爷到她跟前了。

===Original===
“Well, Freddie?” said Eve resignedly.

===Chinese===
「怎么啦，弗雷迪？」伊芙听天由命地说。

===Original===
“I say,” said Freddie in a voice in which self-pity fought with commiseration for her. “Beastly shame you aren’t coming to the hop.”

===Chinese===
「我说，」弗雷迪说，嗓音里自怜与怜她正在角力。「你不能来跳舞，真是可惜死了。」

===Original===
“I don’t mind.”

===Chinese===
「我不在乎。」

===Original===
“But I do, dash it! The thing won’t be anything without you. A bally washout. And I’ve been trying out some new steps with the Victrola.”

===Chinese===
「可我在乎，见鬼！没你，这场舞会就什么都不是。一场彻底的泡汤。我还跟着维克多拉留声机练了好几步新舞呢。」

===Original===
“Well, there will be plenty of other girls there for you to step on.”

===Chinese===
「放心，有的是别的姑娘供你踩。」

===Original===
“I don’t *want* other girls, dash them. I want you.”

===Chinese===
「我*不要*别的姑娘，去她们的。我要你。」

===Original===
“That’s very nice of you,” said Eve. The first truculence of her manner had softened. She reminded herself, as she had so often been obliged to remind herself before, that Freddie meant well. “But it can’t be helped. I’m only an employee here, not a guest. I’m not invited.”

===Chinese===
「你真好，」伊芙说。她态度里最初的火气软了下来。她提醒自己——此前已不得不提醒过许多回——弗雷迪没有恶意。「可没法子。我在这里只是个雇员，不是客人。没人请我。」

===Original===
“I know,” said Freddie. “And that’s what makes it so dashed sickening. It’s like that picture I saw once, *A Modern Cinderella*. Only there the girl nipped off to the dance﻿—disguised, you know﻿—and had a most topping time. I wish life was a bit more like the movies.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，」弗雷迪说。「这才叫人格外地憋闷。就像我有回看的那个片子，《现代灰姑娘》。不过那部里姑娘是溜去舞会了——化了装，你知道——玩得尽兴极了。真希望人生能多像电影一点。」

===Original===
“Well, it was enough like the movies last night when﻿ ﻿… Oh!”

===Chinese===
「哦，昨晚倒是够像电影的，那时候……啊！」

===Original===
Eve stopped. Her heart gave a sudden jump. Somehow the presence of Freddie was so inextricably associated in her mind with limp proposals of marriage that she had completely forgotten that there was another and a more dashing side to his nature, that side which Mr. Keeble had revealed to her at their meeting in Market Blandings on the previous afternoon. She looked at him with new eyes.

===Chinese===
伊芙收住了。她的心猛地一跳。不知怎么，弗雷迪这个人在她心里同软塌塌的求婚联结得太死，害她全然忘了他的天性还有另外一面、更敢作敢为的一面——前天下午在布兰丁斯集镇，基布尔先生向她揭开的正是那一面。她换了一副眼光看他。

===Original===
“Anything up?” said Freddie.

===Chinese===
「出什么事了？」弗雷迪说。

===Original===
Eve took him excitedly by the sleeve and drew him farther away from the house. Not that there was any need to do so, for the bustle within continued unabated.

===Chinese===
伊芙激动地拽住他的袖子，把他往离宅子更远处带。其实大可不必，因为宅内的忙乱有增无减。

===Original===
“Freddie,” she whispered, “listen! I met Mr. Keeble yesterday after I had left you, and he told me all about how you and he had planned to steal Lady Constance’s necklace.”

===Chinese===
「弗雷迪，」她悄声说，「听着！昨天离开你以后，我见到了基布尔先生，他把你们爷俩怎么密谋偷康斯坦丝夫人项链的事，原原本本告诉我了。」

===Original===
“Good Lord!” cried Freddie, and leaped like a stranded fish.

===Chinese===
「我的老天！」弗雷迪喊，一蹦老高，像条搁浅的鱼。

===Original===
“And I’ve got an idea,” said Eve.

===Chinese===
「而且我有个主意，」伊芙说。

===Original===
She had, and it was one which had only in this instant come to her. Until now, though she had tilted her chin bravely and assured herself that the game was not over and that she was not yet beaten, a small discouraging voice had whispered to her all the while that this was mere bravado. What, the voice had asked, are you going to do? And she had not been able to answer it. But now, with Freddie as an ally, she could act.

===Chinese===
她有，而且是此刻才来的。直到现在，她虽勇敢地扬着下巴、向自己保证棋局未完、自己未败，可一直有个泄气的小声音在耳边窃窃：这不过是逞强。那声音问：你打算怎么办？她答不上来。可现在，有弗雷迪做盟军，她能动手了。

===Original===
“Told you all about it?” Freddie was muttering pallidly. He had never had a very high opinion of his Uncle Joseph’s mentality, but he had supposed him capable of keeping a thing like that to himself. He was, indeed, thinking of Mr. Keeble almost the identical thoughts which Mr. Keeble in the first moments of his interview with Eve in Market Blandings had thought of him. And these reflections brought much the same qualms which they had brought to the elder conspirator. Once these things got talked about, mused Freddie agitatedly, you never knew where they would stop. Before his mental eye there swam a painful picture of his Aunt Constance, informed of the plot, tackling him and demanding the return of her necklace. “Told you all about it?” he bleated, and, like Mr. Keeble, mopped his brow.

===Chinese===
「原原本本全告诉你了？」弗雷迪面色发白地喃喃。他素来对他舅约瑟夫的脑子估价不高，但总以为他还守得住这样的秘密。的确，他此刻对基布尔先生的所思所想，与基布尔先生在布兰丁斯集镇同伊芙会晤的头几分钟里对他的所思所想，几乎一字不差。而这些念头带来的忐忑，也与年长那位阴谋家当时所差的无几。这些事一旦传开了，弗雷迪激动地寻思，天知道会传到哪步。他眼前浮起一幅痛心的画面：康斯坦丝姑妈获悉了全盘密谋，揪住他讨还项链。「原原本本全告诉你了？」他哀声说，并且同基布尔先生一样，抹起了脑门。

===Original===
“It’s all right,” said Eve impatiently. “It’s quite all right. He asked me to steal the necklace, too.”

===Chinese===
「没事的，」伊芙不耐烦地说。「真的没事。他还请我也去偷项链呢。」

===Original===
“You?” said Freddie, gaping.

===Chinese===
「你？」弗雷迪张着嘴说。

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。」

===Original===
“My Gosh!” cried Freddie, electrified. “Then was it you who got the thing last night?”

===Chinese===
「我的天！」弗雷迪喊，如遭电击。「那昨晚把货弄到手的就是你？」

===Original===
“Yes it was. But﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「是我。可是……」

===Original===
For a moment Freddie had to wrestle with something that was almost a sordid envy. Then better feelings prevailed. He quivered with manly generosity. He gave Eve’s hand a tender pat. It was too dark for her to see it, but he was registering renunciation.

===Chinese===
有一瞬间，弗雷迪不得不同一种近乎肮脏的妒意角力。随后高尚的情感占了上风。他因男儿的慷慨而颤抖。他温柔地拍了拍伊芙的手。天太黑，她看不见：他脸上正写着割爱。

===Original===
“Little girl,” he murmured, “there’s no one I’d rather got that thousand quid than you. If I couldn’t have it myself, I mean to say. Little girl﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「小姑娘，」他喃喃道，「那一千镑，给谁都不如给你我心里痛快。要是我自己捞不着的话，我是说。小姑娘……」

===Original===
“Oh, be quiet!” cried Eve. “I wasn’t doing it for any thousand pounds. I didn’t want Mr. Keeble to give me money﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「哦，闭嘴！」伊芙喊。「我才不为什么一千镑。我不要基布尔先生给我钱……」

===Original===
“You didn’t want him to give you money!” repeated Freddie wonderingly.

===Chinese===
「你不要他给你钱！」弗雷迪讶异地重复。

===Original===
“I just wanted to help Phyllis. She’s my friend.”

===Chinese===
「我只是想帮菲利斯。她是我朋友。」

===Original===
“Pals, pardner, pals! Pals till hell freezes!” cried Freddie, deeply moved.

===Chinese===
「哥们儿，搭档，哥们儿！地狱结冰也是哥们儿！」弗雷迪喊，深深感动了。

===Original===
“What *are* you talking about?”

===Chinese===
「你*到底*在说什么呀？」

===Original===
“Sorry. That was a subtitle from a thing called *Prairie Nell*, you know. Just happened to cross my mind. It was in the second reel where the two fellows are﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「对不住。那是部叫《草原奈尔》的片子的字幕，你知道。正好撞进脑子里。第二本里两个伙计正……」

===Original===
“Yes, yes; never mind.”

===Chinese===
「行了行了，别管这个。」

===Original===
“Thought I’d mention it.”

===Chinese===
「想着提一句。」

===Original===
“Tell me﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「告诉我……」

===Original===
“It seemed to fit in.”

===Chinese===
「觉得挺应景。」

===Original===
“Do *stop*, Freddie!”

===Chinese===
「*别说了*，弗雷迪！」

===Original===
“Right-ho!”

===Chinese===
「得嘞！」

===Original===
“Tell me,” resumed Eve, “is Mr. McTodd going to the ball?”

===Chinese===
「告诉我，」伊芙重新说，「麦克托德先生去不去舞会？」

===Original===
“Eh? Why, yes, I suppose so.”

===Chinese===
「嗯？咦，去，该去的吧。」

===Original===
“Then, listen. You know that little cottage your father has let him have?”

===Chinese===
「那么，听着。你知不知道你父亲批给他的那间小屋？」

===Original===
“Little cottage?”

===Chinese===
「什么小屋？」

===Original===
